# Yuki memory — design contract (v1)

Goal: Yuki understands its user — work, interests, people, routines, preferences, open loops — and remembers how things are done on this PC. Informed by MaxMi (a separate macOS/Swift project, reference only: reuse its ideas and prompts, never its code) and by the research on hierarchical memory (Generative Agents reflection, Mem0 add/update/delete, Graphiti bi-temporal facts, LangMem semantic/episodic/procedural split).

All rules in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` apply (no behaviour heuristics, no fixed sleeps, log everything, builders never touch the live desktop).

## Four layers

| Layer | What | Written by | Kept |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Captures** | What newly appeared on screen: conversation messages (fingerprinted, new or history) or main-text deltas of pages/documents | watcher | 30 days (TTL); message fingerprints forever |
| **Journal** | Short dated third-person facts ("Watched 'X' by MrBeast on YouTube in Arc"), each dated by the message or capture it comes from | journal worker, every few minutes, Haiku | forever |
| **Portrait** | One-page user model: work, interests, people (+ pending with each), routines, preferences, open loops | portrait worker, nightly + weekly, Sonnet | forever, bi-temporal |
| **Know-how** | Procedures that worked on this PC ("Arc: pass the URL as a launch argument") | the agent itself after successful tasks, via a tool; the conversation worker after a correction led to success | forever, bi-temporal |

## Processes

- `yuki-memory`: a separate background process (started by the tray app, which itself starts at logon - see `docs/UI.md`, "Start with Windows"; its own entry point) that reads the PC's power history (see "Timeline: PC off and asleep") and runs the watcher, the journal worker, the conversation worker (Yuki's own exchanges, see "Conversation memory"), the coach (check-ins and reminders, see "Nudges and to-dos"), the weekly review (see "Weekly review"), the call worker (both sides of calls and a game's voice chat, see "Calls: both sides") and the portrait worker. A crash here must never take Yuki down.
- Yuki (the agent) reads memory directly from the store (same machine) through tools; no network hop. An MCP server for other agents comes later.

## Watcher (capture)

- **Triggers are OS events**, not polling: `SetWinEventHook` for `EVENT_SYSTEM_FOREGROUND`, `EVENT_OBJECT_NAMECHANGE` (title changes), `EVENT_OBJECT_FOCUS`; plus a slow adaptive backstop re-read of the foreground window (e.g. every 45–90 s while the user is active). Debounce so a burst of events yields one capture after the window settles (condition: title/tree stable), never a fixed sleep.
- **Page changes without a title change** (reels, feeds): the timeline re-reads the page's address within `PAGE_ACTIVE_REPROBE_S` (2.5 s) of a content name or value change of the process in front (the address bar's value is one), and when the address changed it asks the watcher for a capture (`Watcher.request_capture(hwnd, "page")`, a message to its hook thread), rate-limited per window by `min_gap_s["page"]` (2 s). Measured 2026-09-24 night 2: 5 reels scrolled, 4 captures with distinct captions (before: 1, the backstop).
- **Empty first read retried once**: a read after a `foreground`/`start`/`resume`/`page` trigger that found nothing (or under `retry_below_chars`, 24), or a window that did not answer (`window_busy`, also after `title`), is read again once the window settles: on its next hooked event, when the process behind a UWP frame appears (the hooks are then re-registered for it), or at the latest `retry_wait_s` (5 s) later. Windows Settings: empty at launch, then captured 1 s later (before: nothing at all). Known limit: WinUI apps (Settings) raise no WinEvents for in-app navigation (UIA events only), so later page switches there are seen by the backstop only.
- **A browser tab still loading is not another tab**: when the window's selected tab and its title name none of the pages found (they are hidden tabs' Documents, which Chromium keeps in the tree), nothing is read (`window_busy`); reading the whole window then would read the hidden tabs as if they were on screen.
- **What is read**: the foreground window (and, since 2026-09-29, everything else that can be seen: next bullet), through the extraction layer `yuki/memory/extract` (`extract(hwnd, now=, user_names=, app=, timeout_s=1.3)`, never raises): one structured, visible-only UIA read, turned by per-app profiles (`extract/profiles_default.toml`, data) or generic structure into an `Extraction`: `kind` (conversation | email | page | document | list | terminal | generic), `thread_scope` (the Slack channel/DM, the Gmail subject, the page URL), and either `messages` (chats and mail: sender, `is_me`, time label as shown, absolute `at`, a stable `fingerprint`, a `content_key`) or the main `body` with UI chrome removed (`dropped_chars`). Sidebars, toolbars, conversation lists and the composer are never content. The read budget is `capture_budget_s` = 1.3 s.
- **Everything visible, not only the window in front (2026-09-29, the user's decision: "use the same capture we have for apps and expand it to everything that shows up on my screen"; no pixels)**. `WatcherSettings.visible` (on; `--no-visible` turns it off):
  - *What is on screen* is measured by `yuki.perception.visibility.layout()`: every visible, uncloaked, unminimised top-level window on every monitor in Z-order, with the share of it no window above covers (16 px grid; ~9 ms p50 in the service). A window counts from `visible_min_share` (3%). It is measured again 0.3 s after a window is shown, hidden, (un)cloaked, (un)minimised or moved (system-wide `SetWinEventHook` for those events only; ~10-25 a minute measured), on every foreground change and at the backstop - never on a timer of its own.
  - *Background windows are read on change only*: when one first becomes visible, when its title changes, or when something inside it changes (a name-change hook per process of the visible background windows, at most 8; a process sending more than 300 events a minute is unhooked for 10 min - Task Manager sent ~900). A read waits until the window's events have been quiet 1 s (capped at 6 s); the gap between two reads of one window starts at 15 s and doubles each time a read finds nothing new, up to 5 min; a window just read while it was in front is not read again when it goes to the back. Budget: 4 background reads and 1.5 s of reading a minute (reads waiting in the queue count), never while the user is idle, locked, paused or a full-screen app is in front.
  - *The same read, gates and store*: the structured read (`extract(..., hidden=...)`, 1.0 s budget) with the parts wholly under other windows left out (`prune_snapshot`, `Layout.covered`), the same privacy gates (apps, windows, private windows, addresses, a password field on screen, masked lines), the secret filter and the same threads and deltas. Captures are stored with `trigger` `visible:<shown|title|content|refresh>` (**visible, not in front**; the journal sees the trigger in its source line) or `popup`. A window whose last read was privacy-skipped after reading (a blocked site, a sign-in page) is not read again while its title is the same (also the window in front, for its focus / content / backstop triggers: a Meet call in front was re-read on every focus change, 0.5-2 s each).
  - *Popups, menus, dialogs and notification toasts*: a top-level window that appears (shown or uncloaked) that is not a user window (untitled or tool-styled: a menu, a flyout, a toast, a drop-down) is read once 0.5 s later if it is still there (0.8 s budget, at most 6 a minute), and again only when it is shown again. A popup of another process that names an app the user blocked (a line or control equal to a blocked app's name, the user's own list) is dropped whole. Captures with text are stored like any other (`trigger` `popup`), except a popup whose own element is a UIA `ToolTip`, `Menu` or `MenuBar` (a tooltip or a menu is what is on screen, not something that happened): those go to Now only; every popup read goes to Now's recent popups. Toast capture has not been seen live yet (no notification arrived during the measurements).
  - *Opaque windows* (content coverage under 5%: `extract` reports `stats.coverage`, the same measure as the tree's) are remembered for 10 min: their title changes and content events are followed without a UI Automation read (a Warp window's spinner changed its title every second: 509 empty reads in 3 h before), their title goes to Now, and the app's own data when a reader exists (`yuki.memory.now.APP_DATA_READERS`: Warp's saved tabs, read-only, at most every 5 s) plus the titles its windows had lately (the timeline, 12 h, at most every 60 s).
  - *Content changes of the window in front* that change neither its title nor its focus (a chat message arriving) are a new trigger, `content`: a read after 1 s of quiet, at most every 30 s, doubling while reads find nothing new, up to 3 min.
  - Logs (content-free): every read is a `capture` record with `visible_share`, `coverage`, `opaque`, `covered_nodes`; `app_data` (found, tabs, ms); `visible_hook` (a process muted); `stats.watcher.visible` (counts per outcome, reads and popups in the last minute, windows tracked, processes hooked and muted, opaque windows, layout ms, the Now board's size).
- **Now** (`yuki/memory/now.py`): every read that passes the gates also goes, redacted, into the `NowBoard` in RAM (never written anywhere): per visible window its latest gist (the one in front up to 6,000 characters, others 1,500, the newest 12 messages of a chat), app, title, address, age, visible share, coverage, and the app's own data for an opaque one; blocked windows keep no content; popups for 15 min. The service serves it on a local pipe (`NowServer`, `\\.\pipe\YukiNow-<digest>`; ops `now`, `refresh`, `stats`); Yuki's tray reads it for the brain's per-turn situation and the voice's desk block (docs/ARCHITECTURE.md, "Perception: Now"). `--no-now` turns it off.
- **One reader with the agent (report 58, 4 Oct; in progress).** Capture reads windows with its own structured walker (`extract/uia.read_window`), which keeps unnamed containers, class names, ARIA roles and landmarks that the agent's flattened tree drops. It shares only the page finder (`perception.tree._ViewportPass`). What is shared now: `snapshot_from_tree` (used when a caller already holds the agent's read) carries the RuntimeId, the AutomationId, the app's own numbering, and the agent reader's hidden-in-place state controls as `sr_only` (never text). The rest is for the memory builder: give `_ViewportPass` a "keep structure" mode (unnamed containers plus the extra properties above), move `read_window`'s content walk onto it, and capture then sees exactly what the agent acts on (the hidden slider, the left-out counts, the same RuntimeIds).
- **Thread** = (app, `thread_scope`), not the window title. A scope equal to the URL keys the thread exactly as the URL did before, so page threads carry over.
- **Conversations and mail: messages, not text.** Each message on screen is stored in `messages` only if its fingerprint is unseen in the thread (a history on screen is stored once, however often it is re-read). An unseen fingerprint whose `content_key` the thread already has, where either reading lacks a time label, is the same message re-read (`reread`, fingerprint only). The rest are labelled against when the watcher last saw the thread (`threads.messages_seen_at`): **new** = sent/received since then (its `at` at or after the last look less 90 s, or, untimed, below the thread's newest stored message on screen; on a first visit, an `at` within 5 min of the capture); **history** = older messages being viewed. One capture row per read that stored anything carries them to the journal.
- **Pages, documents, lists: delta, not snapshot**: keep the thread's latest main text; store only what changed (line diff), exact-hash deduped.
- **List rows carry their own date**: a list row (inbox row, folder entry) that shows a date - its rightmost time label, its tooltip, or a labelled cell "Date modified: 9/23/2026 5:35 PM" - is written `[dated YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM]] <row>` (resolved against the capture time). The journal makes each row of a `list` capture its own source, dated by that date.
- **The user's names**: `Settings.user_names` (none by default: `YUKI_USER_NAMES`, or `user_names` in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\settings.json`; for example Sam, sam42) plus names an app's own UI shows as the user (its `self_name` anchor, its `me_suffixes` such as "Sam (you)"), learned per app and kept encrypted in `me_names`. **Whose a message is** (`is_me`) comes from the app's profile only (2026-09-25, no-pattern audit D): its `me_row` anchor, its `me_senders` (the app's own label for the user, e.g. WhatsApp's "You:"), or the name its `self_name` anchor shows on the same screen. There is no cross-app word list ("You", "Me", "(you)") and the configured names are not matched: in a generic chat the sender is stored as shown and the journal model, told the user's names (WHO THE USER IS) and that an app's own word for the viewer is the user, attributes it.
- **No keystroke capture.** Messages are recorded when they appear on screen.
- **Privacy gates (data, not code paths)** in a user-editable config file, applied in the watcher (never in the extractor): the page URL (`rules.check_url(extraction.url)`) before anything is stored, masked lines dropped from every message and body (`rules.is_masked`); skip password/secure fields (UIA `IsPassword`: the structured read never reads their value; the capture is skipped while one has focus, and - since 2026-09-25, `[fields] skip_when_password_present` - while any password field is on screen: a sign-in page or credentials dialog is told by the field it shows, not by "/login" or "/otp" in its address; counted during the structured read, ~1 ms for 2,000 nodes, reason `password_field`), named password-manager apps, named banking/auth sites (explicit lists, from MaxMi's `Denylist.swift`), named adult sites, private/incognito windows (each browser's own title mark), and a user blocklist of apps/domains. The word lists that guessed what a page or app is (`blocked_path_fragments`, adult `host_substrings` and `query_terms`, `blocked_process_substrings`) are no longer read (no-pattern audit C). Pause content capture while a full-screen window is in front (a game, an F11 video; the timeline still counts the time, see below) and pause entirely when the session is locked. Named meeting apps and sites (Zoom.exe, meet.google.com, zoom.us, Teams meeting paths) are blocked here like banking; the timeline keeps meeting hours only (see "Timeline: full screen and meetings").
- **Budget**: <2% CPU on average, capture < 1.3 s (the structured read; 0.2-0.9 s measured 2026-09-24), never blocks the foreground app; measure and log `capture_health` (content-free: app, trigger, outcome, reason, chars, ms, plus the extraction's `profile`, `kind`, `dropped_chars`, messages on screen / stored new, and `stats` = nodes, read/total ms, source, truncated). A window the OS says is not a user window is logged as `not_user_window:<window class>`.
- **Terminals**: GPU-drawn terminals (Warp) expose only their frame through UIA, so the watcher captures nothing from them. Instead (owner's decision, 2026-09-24) `yuki/memory/warp.py` reads Warp's own command history - see "Terminal history" below. Command output is never read.

## Secret filter

The incident (2026-09-25): a password a friend typed in a chat DM ("pass : ..." under "user id : ...", a site login) was captured, stored, sent to the journal's Haiku and later, as a source excerpt, to the audit's Sonnet. UIA masks password *fields*; text typed into chats, pages and documents, or said in calls, is plain text. Code: `yuki/memory/secret_filter.py` (detector), `yuki/memory/scrub_secrets.py` (maintenance), `scripts/secret_filter_eval.py`, `tests/test_secret_filter.py`, `tests/secret_cases.py`, `tests/secret_cases_holdout.py`.

- **Fixed formats only (the owner's decision, 2026-09-25, "option B")**: the filter parses machine formats and never guesses from words. The first version (a hand-written grammar of password, OTP, PIN, CVV and account labels in English, Hinglish and Devanagari, plus entropy gated by a "secret word nearby") broke the no-keyword rule (`docs/research/no-pattern-audit.md` A) and was deleted.
- **What it hides**, as a typed placeholder that keeps the sentence ("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=[API key hidden]"):
  - keys and tokens in their vendor's published format (prefix and length): AWS, Bedrock, Anthropic, OpenAI, ElevenLabs, Stripe (incl. `whsec_`), GitHub, GitLab, Slack (incl. webhooks), Discord (webhooks, bot tokens), Google (`AIza`, `ya29.`, refresh tokens, `GOCSPX-`), Hugging Face, npm, SendGrid, Groq, xAI, Replicate, Telegram;
  - PEM private key blocks and PuTTY key files;
  - JWTs (three base64url parts whose header decodes to JSON with `alg`/`typ`);
  - the password of a URL's userinfo (`scheme://user:X@host`, read with `urllib.parse.urlsplit`; a template such as `${DB_PASS}` is not a value);
  - the credentials of an HTTP `Authorization:` header (RFC 7235 syntax: scheme, then token68);
  - card numbers: 13-19 digits, contiguous or with one kind of separator, Luhn-valid and in a published issuer (IIN) range;
  - IFSC codes (`AAAA0XXXXXX`).
  - Placeholders: `[password hidden]`, `[API key hidden]`, `[token hidden]`, `[private key hidden]`, `[card number hidden]`, `[IFSC hidden]`, `[secret hidden]`; the ones the old detectors wrote (`[one-time code hidden]`, `[card code hidden]`, `[bank account hidden]`) are still recognised, so redaction stays idempotent.
- **What it no longer catches (accepted by the owner)**: **a password typed in a chat, a page or a document, or said in a call, is stored as it is** ("pass : X", "mera password hai X", `DB_PASSWORD=X`, `mysql -pX`, `--password X`) - this is exactly the 2026-09-25 incident's shape. So are one-time codes, PINs, CVVs, bank account numbers, and keys without a vendor format behind a label (`api key: <hex>`, `AccountKey=`, `client_secret: ...`, `?access_token=`). UIA password *fields* are still never read (`[fields]`), and sign-in pages are skipped when a password field is on screen (see Privacy gates). The fix the audit recommends is a small local secret tagger (a model), not words.
- **Entropy was measured and dropped**: an entropy test (>= 3.5 bits/char, 20+ characters, mixed classes) without the secret-word gate flagged 16 of the 167 hard negatives (every git SHA, sha256/md5, ETag, ssh public key, base64 image) and 28 tokens in a 20k-character capture with no secret, while adding almost nothing the vendor formats miss. Warp keeps its own terminal-only entropy rule (a command with a long random word is dropped whole; a git hash there too, by design).
- **Where** (every entry point, before storage and so before any model call): the watcher (`_hide_secrets`: message texts, page/document/list bodies, title, address, scope; fingerprints stay those of the screen, so re-reads still dedupe; `capture` log gains `secrets` counts by kind and `secret_ms`), call transcripts (`transcribe.py`; `Store._add_call_side` again), the agent's turns (`MemoryClient.log_turn`: request, reply and action lines), rules, know-how, portrait corrections, the acting marker (`acting.json` and `captures.yuki_request`), Warp (`TerminalRules.secret_reason`: the shared detector, then the entropy rule; the privacy file's old `secret_patterns` regexes are no longer read), and the agent/UI session logs and `requests.csv` (`SessionLogger.log` checks the serialized line in one pass and redacts the record's strings only on a hit). The agent itself keeps the words in its own context: a key the user pastes works for the task at hand; only what is stored is filtered. `context=` (the previous message) is still accepted and unused: no fixed format spans two messages.
- **Measured 2026-09-25 (fixed formats)**, `scripts/secret_filter_eval.py`; the sets mark the secrets only a label identifies (`LABEL_ONLY`, `HOLDOUT_LABEL_ONLY`: 53 of 96 and 38 of 74), which are out of scope:

  | Set, in scope | password (URL) | api_key | token | private_key | card | ifsc | secret | all |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | labelled (43 secrets, 106 negatives): recall / precision | 3/3 | 9/9 | 12/12 | 3/3 | 5/5, 1 FP | 3/3 | 2/2 | 1.00 / 0.97 |
  | blind hold-out (36 secrets, 61 negatives) | 3/3 | 12/12 | 7/7 | 3/3 | 5/5 | 3/3 | 2/2 | 1.00 / 1.00 |

  Over every case (label-only included) recall is 0.41 (labelled) and 0.47 (hold-out): passwords 0.08 / 0.14, OTP, CVV 0, bank accounts 0.40 (Luhn-valid account numbers hidden as cards) / 0. The one false positive is "Invoice no 4111111111111111": the canonical Visa test number is a card by format (accepted). Real data (a read-only copy of the database, 5,970 encrypted values, 1.5M characters): no finding (the incident's rows were scrubbed before; no false positive). Cost: real stored texts p50 0.03 ms, p99 0.5 ms, max 7 ms (a 67k-character call); a 20k-character synthetic capture p50 3.1 ms.
- **Scrub what is stored**: `python -m yuki.memory.scrub_secrets [--db PATH] [--dry-run] [--samples N]` re-runs the detector over every `*_ciphertext` text column (found from the schema), redacts in place, re-encrypts, recomputes capture/thread hashes, writes with `secure_delete` on and each row only if unchanged since read; the report is counts and redacted snippets only.
- **Other limits**: a secret spelled out in words keeps its spelling; embeddings of already-stored text are not recomputed; the encrypted model-call logs (`logs/memory/*.jsonl`, `request_ciphertext`) and older plaintext session logs keep what they had (the scrub covers the database only); what was already sent to a model cannot be recalled.

## Yuki's own actions

While the agent carries out a request with its hands (from its first action tool - launch, click, type, keys, scroll, PowerShell, open URL - until the request ends; look-only requests never count), it publishes a marker (`yuki/memory/acting.py`): `acting.json` next to the database (request text, start, lane, pid; removed at the end; entries of a dead process are ignored) and sets the named event `Local\YukiMemoryActing-<db digest>` that the service creates. The service's `yuki-memory-acting` thread (woken by the event, or every 2 s) tells the watcher and the timeline.

- Captures triggered during the window, or within `acting_tail_s` (3 s) after it, are stored `by_yuki = 1` with the request (encrypted). Timeline stretches are cut where acting starts and stops; the ones in between are `by_yuki` (migration 7, additive: `captures.by_yuki`, `captures.yuki_request_ciphertext`, `timeline.by_yuki`, `timeline.yuki_request_ciphertext`, `journal.by_yuki`).
- The journal shows such sources as `BY YUKI (at the user's request "...")` and records them as "Yuki, at the user's request '...', opened ..."; facts from them are `by_yuki`.
- The portrait leaves `by_yuki` facts and stretches out entirely (the user's request itself reaches it through conversation memory); episodes list the stretches apart as YUKI ACTING, outside the time use. Conversation memory records a request as a task, never as a taste ("asked for the soba article" is not "interested in Japanese cuisine").

- The marker is refreshed on every action of the request (`seen_at`); an entry not refreshed for `acting.MAX_AGE_S` (10 min; the longest of 108 real worker requests took 65 s) is ignored and logged once by the service (`acting_leak`), and a write that meets a sharing violation is tried again (four short waits, under half a second). On 2026-09-25 one failed end left the marker standing 53 minutes and the user's own work was tagged as Yuki's.

## Timeline: full screen and meetings

The timeline (`yuki/memory/timeline.py`: foreground stretches with app, page, present/away from the user's real input (`user_idle_s`, below), media) records two kinds of rows that carry less than the rest:

**Presence** (`user_idle_s`, `yuki/memory/userpresence.py`, 5 Oct 2026): seconds since the user's last *real* input, read the way the tray's input gate reads it: a key going down, a mouse button, the wheel, the mouse travelling the takeover distance (`ui.json` `takeover_move_px`, 40) within 0.3 s, and a session unlock. The mouse's travel is its own raw counts summed over the window (absolute devices: screen px), never the cursor, so Yuki's cursor jumps never count and turning in a game does. Yuki's stamped input (`0x594B5549`) never counts; untagged injected input (dictation) does. Read through Raw Input (`RIDEV_INPUTSINK`, a message-only window on one thread of the memory service, counts and times only, never which key or where); after the mouse showed the user is there it is not listened to for 2 s, so a 1000 Hz gaming mouse costs a few reports every 2 s. Never less than Windows' own idle time. Why: on 5 Oct the user's mouse sensor began vibrating (~23 moves/s of 0-1 px with nobody there); the old rule (3 idle-timer resets in 1.5 s = a person) held `user_idle_s` at ~0 all the time, so from ~11:00 that day the timeline wrote no `away` rows, idle-gated jobs (portrait, weekly review catch-up, call transcription's away runs, the watcher's idle pause) never ran, and picking-up cards could not see a return. Measured on the real stream (read-only): the jitter's net travel never passed 11 counts in 0.3 s (the threshold is 40), `user_idle_s` grew to 175 s over a 3-minute probe with nobody there while Windows' idle time stayed at 0-0.1 s; the reader thread used ~0.2% of one core at 22 reports/s and added under 0.5 MB to a process that already has a window thread. The old sampler stays only as the fallback when Raw Input is unavailable (`presence_facts()`, logged at `timeline_start` and on each `watcher_state idle` change); if the reader finishes starting after the 1 s wait, it takes over then (`late_start`) and the old sampler's poller stops. **Limit (UIPI):** Raw Input in the (unelevated) memory service hears nothing typed into a window of a higher-integrity process (an admin terminal, Task Manager, a tool or game run as administrator). So when Windows' idle timer saw input newer than the reader's last and the window in front belongs to such a process (its token's integrity level above the service's, or it cannot be asked: access denied, a protected process), Windows' idle time answers instead, and that input stays credited after the window leaves the front (`answering: idle_timer`, `unheard_front` count in the facts; `watcher_state` is logged when the answering source changes). The cost: with the vibrating mouse and an elevated window left in front, the user reads as present for as long as it stays there. The check is two calls (~2 us) while the same window stays in front, ~25 us for a new one, and only runs when Windows saw input the reader did not. Probe: `scripts/presence_probe.py`.

- **Full screen** (`timeline.fullscreen = 1`): a game or a full-screen video is counted, not skipped. While `[pause] when_fullscreen` is on (default), such a row has only process and app, start/end, present vs away and media - no title, no page, no title hook. When the same window showed a site just before going full screen, that host (not the path) carries over, so "watched YouTube full-screen 40 min" is known without reading the page. Entering or leaving full screen (same window, F11) starts a new stretch. Full-screen time counts in aggregates (`fullscreen_s` per activity and in the totals) and in episodes ("played VALORANT for 1h40").
- **Meetings** (`timeline.meeting` = the service name) are **measured, not listed** (2026-09-25, no-pattern audit C; the old `[meetings]` process/host/path lists are no longer read): the app in front has held the microphone (or the camera, `camera = true`) for `[meetings] hold_s` (20 s), by the ConsentStore's own start time (`yuki/memory/avsignals.py`, shared with the call worker). For an app that shows web pages, the meeting is the site that was in front when the app took the device (a browser holds the microphone for one tab; another site in front during the call is its own stretch, the meeting site coming back to front is the meeting again at once). The stretch is cut and becomes a meeting row with the app, host and service name (the app's own name - FileDescription - or the site; `[meetings.names]` renames), start/end - no title, no path; it ends `end_grace_s` (30 s) after the app lets go. The user's own `always_apps` / `always_sites` (meetings whenever in front) and `never_apps` / `never_sites` override; all empty by default. Content stays blocked by `[apps]`/`[web]`. The holders are read every 15 s (`MEDIA_EVERY_S`), a cached read unless Windows changed the ConsentStore (change notification, checked without waiting: ~0 ms; a full re-read of both devices ~24 ms, on a change or every 60 s). The meeting app using the mic counts as present without input and as `mic_s`. A meeting is its own activity ("in a meeting (Google Meet)"); `aggregate()["meetings"]` joins stretches of one service less than 5 min apart into one span ("in a meeting 15:15-16:04 (Google Meet in Google Chrome), in front 45m, microphone on 44m"). Episodes are told never to guess who was in a meeting or what it was about.
- **Watching**: time while the app in front plays media is `passive_s` (watching or listening) whatever the input says; for a web page, only when its title names the playing media (a background tab playing is not the page in front). Input alone cannot tell watching from doing: nobody touches the keyboard during a film.
- Limits: a controller-only game gives no keyboard or mouse input, so it reads as away unless it plays media. A meeting is counted only while its window (or site) is in front; the 5-minute join covers short looks elsewhere. Anything in front holding the microphone for 20 s is a meeting: a Discord or WhatsApp call, and also a game's voice chat or a dictation or recording app in front (mark those `never_apps`); the first `hold_s` of each meeting is recorded as an ordinary stretch.

## Timeline: PC off and asleep

A hole in the timeline used to be only a hole (the service not running, or `MAX_TICK_GAP_S` leaving a sleep out). Now Windows' own power history says what it was. Code: `yuki/memory/power.py`, `yuki/memory/store.py` (migration 12), `yuki/memory/service.py`.

- **Source: the System event log, read-only** (`wevtapi.dll` `EvtQuery`, one XPath over the channel; no admin rights, nothing written, cleared or subscribed to). `Kernel-General` 13 (`StopTime`) then 12 (`StartTime`) = **off**. A 12 with no 13 since the boot before it = a start after the PC stopped without a clean shutdown = **unknown**, from the last sign of life - `EventLog` 6008's "previous shutdown was unexpected" time (its UTC `SYSTEMTIME`) or the end of the last recorded stretch, whichever is later - to the start; nothing is recorded when neither is known. `Power-Troubleshooter` 1 (`SleepTime`, `WakeTime`) = **asleep** (sleep or hibernation); `Kernel-Power` 42 + 107 is the fallback when no such event covers it, and 506/507 (modern standby) are asleep too. A sleep inside an off span (fast startup) is left to the off span; a sleep after an unknown span's last sign of life moves that span's start. Spans under 60 s are dropped. `powercfg /sleepstudy` and `/systemsleepdiagnostics` need admin, so they are not used.
- **Service restart while the PC stayed on**: no shutdown, start or sleep in the log, so nothing is recorded. The gap stays a gap, now described as "nothing recorded (the PC was on; Yuki not running, memory paused or the PC locked)".
- **When it reads**: at service start (before the timeline starts), and after a sleep: the service's main loop wakes every second, and a wall-clock jump of more than 30 s between two wakes (`RESUME_JUMP_S`) is a sleep, a hibernation or a stall; it reads then and once more 120 s later (`RECHECK_AFTER_S`), because Windows writes the wake event a little after the resume. Checkpoint `source_checkpoints('power.events')` = the newest event read; each read goes back 2 days before it (`OVERLAP_S`) so a shutdown read last time still pairs with the start read now; the first read goes back to 2 days before the timeline's first row (at most 30 days).
- **Store** (migration 12, additive): power spans are timeline rows with `state` = `off` | `asleep` | `unknown`, no app, title, page or counters, and `timeline.source` = `shutdown` | `sleep` | `modern_standby` | `unexpected_shutdown` | `no_record`. A partial unique index on `(state, ended_at)` makes re-reading the log idempotent (`Store.add_power_spans` is `INSERT OR IGNORE`). `Store.power_spans(since, until)`, `Store.timeline_last_end_before(at)`. Every reader that asks `state == "present"` (all of them) already treats these as not-present.
- **Surfaced everywhere time is described**:
  - `timeline.aggregate`: `power` (the spans: kind, start, end, seconds, source) and `totals.off_s` / `asleep_s` / `unknown_s` (neither present nor away; `first_at`/`last_at` leave them out). `describe` adds ", PC off 9h05m" to the totals line and one `power: PC off Mon 23:55-Tue 09:00 (9h05m)` line per span. `sequence` emits `{"kind": "off" | "asleep" | "unknown"}` entries where it used to see away or gap. `breaks` counts them as breaks and says what each break was: `kind` (the largest part: off, asleep, unknown, away or gap), `power` seconds per kind, `away_s`, `gap_s`; `describe_break` -> `9h12m (PC off 9h05m, PC on but not recorded 7m)`.
  - The agent's `activity` tool (`MemoryClient.activity` -> `format_activity`) and MCP `get_activity`: "PC off (shut down) from Mon 2026-09-21 23:55 to Tue 2026-09-22 09:00 (9h05m)" under a line saying time in no activity and no such span was the PC on with memory not recording.
  - The coach's BEFORE: "at the PC without a break ... since 09:02 (4m); before that, a break of 9h12m (PC off 9h05m, PC on but not recorded 7m)". A break that reaches back past the coach's 4 h window (the PC off overnight) is measured whole over 36 h, once per break (cached). The coach's prompt says a PC off or asleep is a break, not missing data - this fixes the old limit where the first stretch of the day read as a return from a break of unknown length.
  - Episodes: a window's SEQUENCE shows the spans; a `PC OFF / ASLEEP` block gives the spans inside the window and the one that ended at most 30 min before it ("before this window: PC off Mon 23:55-Tue 09:00 (9h05m)"); the prompt says it is a break, and that "nothing recorded" means the PC was on.
  - Portrait: TIME USE (per day, `describe`) carries the `power:` lines and the totals; its prompt says they mark when the day at the PC ended and began. The weekly review's per-day numbers carry `off_s` / `asleep_s`.
- **Logs** (service log, content-free): `power_sync` (reason start | resume | recheck, events, spans, added, kinds, ms, cpu_ms, error), `service_resumed` (slept_s).
- **Measured 2026-09-25** (live System log, read-only): a read takes 110-130 ms wall; the EventLog service spends ~100-110 ms of CPU scanning its 20 MB log (the same for 2 or 30 days), this process 0-15 ms; +2.4 MB private after the first read. Three reads per sleep, one per start: nothing periodic. 60 days of this PC's history: 12 off spans (nightly shutdown ~01:00-04:00 to ~09:00), 38 asleep spans (July-early September; none since 2026-09-09), 14 unknown (6008 after crashes or hard resets, e.g. 2026-09-22 11:22-12:02). A read-only copy of the real database migrated 11 -> 12 in 21 ms, and its sync added the 2026-09-23 and 2026-09-24 nights.

## Calls: both sides (was "Calls: just my side")

Decided with the owner (2026-10-03, handbook rule 9): during a call Yuki records **both sides**, on this PC, and only when the user said yes for this call (or "Always" for the app - the "Always" answers given before stay, nobody is asked again): the user's own microphone (headphones only, as before) **and the call app's own audio output** through Windows **process loopback** - only that app's process tree, never the whole output, so a game, music and Yuki's own voice stay out (for a call in a browser it is every tab of that browser). A full-screen game's own voice chat (VALORANT and the like, found by the measured `gaming_with_voice`, never by name) is a **game voice session** of its own (`calls.kind` "game") with its own track labelled with the game, asked with the same per-app consent; its card goes up only at a moment it can be seen (not over the full-screen game). The microphone is recorded once per voice session, however many app tracks run beside it. Every transcribed line says who spoke (`who` user | others), on which app (`app`, `track_kind` call | game), and each of the user's lines which apps were taking the microphone at that moment (`heard_by`), so a reply to teammates in the game is not taken for talk to the friends on a Discord call. Since the owner's second decision (2026-09-24 evening, "Yuki must use as little CPU, GPU and RAM as possible"; large-v3 on the GPU during a Discord call cost FPS in games) **nothing is transcribed during a call**: the speech of every track is kept encrypted in a spool and transcribed later, when the PC is free, then deleted. *History: from 2026-09-24 to 2026-10-03 only the user's own microphone was kept - never the call's audio, never the other participants; calls of that time hold only the user's lines and are read as before. Reading both sides - the journal, the digest, the prompts, the quote checks - is report 61 (`docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/61-build-call-wording.md`); the UI and the agent/API wording are their own passes.* Code: `yuki/memory/calls.py` (worker, thread `yuki-memory-calls`: detect, ask, gate, spool; one recording thread per track), `yuki/memory/loopback.py` (process loopback capture, the capture-session watch, the process tree from a Toolhelp snapshot), `yuki/memory/transcribe.py` (the deferred transcriber: scheduler thread `yuki-memory-transcribe` and worker process), `yuki/perception/audio.py` (Core Audio facts, read-only), `yuki/ui/calls.py` (card, indicator, Stop, waiting count, Erase), `yuki/memory/journal.py` (source kind `my_call_side`), `yuki/memory/store.py` (migrations 10, 11 and 34). Knobs: `[calls]` in the privacy file (`record_others`, `game_voice`). `yuki-memory --no-calls` turns it off. Build: `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/60-build-call-others-capture.md`.

**Detecting a call** (content-free; rewritten 2026-10-03 to match the code, `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/05-call-pill-timer.md`). Who holds the microphone is read only when Windows changes the ConsentStore microphone key (`RegNotifyChangeKeyValue` on the subtree; it is written when an app starts or stops using the microphone), with a backstop read every `holders_backstop_s` (60 s); the 5 s `poll_s` wake-up in between is a cached read. The shared reader is `yuki/memory/avsignals.py` (`AvSignals`, one instance for the timeline and the calls worker); Yuki's own interpreters are never a call app (`own_executables()`). **Starting a call: the screen is the proof, the microphone only the hint** (since 2026-09-28: the Zen browser held the microphone for a tab playing YouTube, and a Meet lobby counted as a meeting). A microphone holder starts a call only when a screen of that app (and site), captured during this hold, was judged by a small model to show a joined call (`yuki/memory/callevidence.py`: the watcher hands every window's visible control names to `CallEvidence.seen`; Haiku's `judge_screen` answers `in_call`, `not_in_call` or `unclear`), or when the app is one of the user's own `[calls] apps` (empty by default). **A meeting site the privacy rules keep unread** (2026-10-05, `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/84-build-meetFix.md`: a Google Meet call in Zen was never seen, because `[web] blocked_host_suffixes` lists `meet.google.com` and the watcher never reads it): for a page skipped as `blocked_host` / `blocked_path` (also a re-read skipped as `..._before`) the watcher hands `CallEvidence` only the app, the page's host and the time, in memory. Which of those hosts are calling sites comes from the privacy file itself, never from a visit: the **shipped** list of blocked sites (`privacy_default.toml`, public, the same for everyone) is judged once as a whole by Haiku (`judge_sites`; measured 5 Oct: 27 entries, 4 calling - Meet, Zoom, both Teams hosts - 8.5 s, $0.0045), the user's `[meetings] always_sites` count by their word (`never_sites` never), and a blocked site the user added themselves never counts and is never sent anywhere. A sighting of a calling site during the hold starts a call like an in-call screen (the Ask card decides recording, its standing answer is the service's), unless a newer readable screen of the same site was judged not in a call (Teams' readable lobby after its blocked join link). Its life: held, it ends on a persistent run of readable "not in a call" screens of its site, or once the browser's newest frame is another page judged not in a call and the site was last seen `screen_alive_s` ago (one glance at a meeting page during a long unrelated hold); let go (Meet releases the microphone on mute), it lives on while the browser still plays sound and the site was seen within `screen_alive_s` (`call_alive_by` `site_sound`), else by the release grace. A full-screen game in front using its own voice chat is not a call (`gaming_with_voice`). Sound out, the camera and the timeline's meeting row are hints only; the old `hold_s` rule (microphone + sound out for 15 s) is gone and so is its setting. **Once started, the microphone hold is the session** (`CallLife` in `calls.py`, 2026-10-03): the call lives while its app holds the microphone; a new hold by the same app inside the call (a new ConsentStore `since`) is a re-grab of the same call (`call_regrab`); when the app lets go, the call lives on for `release_grace_s` (90 s), or while an in-call screen shown after it let go is at most `screen_alive_s` old (Meet drops the microphone on mute); not alive for `end_grace_s` (30 s), it ends (`mic_released`). While the app lets go, the user's microphone is not recorded (the call's row and pill go on). The screen ends a call whose microphone is still held only on a persistent, reliable contradiction: `neg_frames` (3) healthy "not in a call" sights of the call's own site spanning `neg_span_s` (120 s) with no in-call frame between (`screen_contradicts`: a "You left the meeting" page while the browser keeps the microphone); one frame never does. A frame counts only when it is healthy: at least `HEALTH_RATIO` (0.5) times the median control count of the site's last 20 in-call frames (measured 28 Sept-2 Oct: in-call reads 68-80 controls, the six half-drawn alt-tab frames that used to end calls 0-22), and an `unclear` verdict counts as neither. **Restarts**: each row keeps `hold_since` (the hold it is in, moved on a re-grab) and `last_alive_at` (written at most every 15 s; migration 26). A restarted service adopts the open row when the same app still holds the microphone with the same `since` (+-5 s) and the row was alive within 10 min (`call_resumed`; a new recording continues the row's chunk numbers and counts), or by the live rules when it was alive less than `release_grace_s` ago; any other open row ends at its `last_alive_at`, never at the restart (a PC that was off is left out of the call's time). **Heavy work waits** while a row that ended within 10 min has an app that still holds the microphone (`lingering_call`, read from the holders the worker keeps in `nudge_state('call_mic_holders')`): the transcriber never starts then (whatever `defer_during_calls` says) and the digest counts it as a call. The pill counts from the call **session**'s start (`call_status()` `session_started_at`: the first row of the same hold, else merged as `callspans` merges rows, which also joins rows of one hold whatever the gap). `call_ended` logs the hold, the gap since the app let go, the newest in-call frame's age, the negatives and their frame sizes, what was in front and whether it was full screen; a verdict applied from the cache is logged too (`call_evidence_applied`). The card's app name is the app's own (FileDescription, or a Store app's Start-menu name; `[calls.names]` renames). The card still asks every call; a false positive costs one "Never for <app>". Poll cost, measured 2026-09-25 (read-only, this PC): a cached read ~0 ms; microphone and camera holders from one process list 24 ms, on a ConsentStore change or every 60 s. Measured live, read-only, 2026-09-24: `Discord.exe` was holding the microphone and had an active capture session on the M-Audio input.

**Asking, every call.** The worker writes a `calls` row (`asking`) and sets `Local\YukiCallReady`; the UI's `CallWatcher` (the nudge watcher on that event, 20 s fallback poll) fetches `call_status()` and shows the consent card (non-activating, buttons without focus), which says what is kept - the user's microphone and the call app's own sound, turned into text later on this PC - with a yes, a no and Always / Never for <app> (the words: `yuki/ui/calls.py`, the UI wording pass). A game's voice chat is asked the same way, only at a moment the card can be seen (`ask_later` shows no card), and a game session that ended before its card could be shown gets it right after (`call_status()["pending_card"]`, report 60 section 4.1). A nudge card up makes way (back in its queue) and nudges wait (`call_consent`) while the card is up. The answer goes to `answer_call` (store, then the event `Local\YukiMemoryCall-<db digest>` wakes the worker at once). Always/Never are kept per app in `call_prefs` and answer later calls without a card (`consent_by = pref`). No answer = no recording: the card goes after `ask_timeout_s` (120 s) and answers `none`; the worker does the same if the UI is not running; a call that ends unanswered is `unanswered`. While nudges are snoozed no card is shown (`not_asked`); an "Always" app still records (the indicator shows). The owner's decision (2026-09-25): snoozing nudges never affects call recording. Confirmed in code and by a stub check the same day: the snooze is read only in `CallWorker._begin`, after the app's preference, so Always + snoozed -> `consent always, by pref` (recorded), no preference + snoozed -> `not_asked`, and a snooze set mid-call changes nothing. Memory's `paused` flag ends the call's recording within one poll, and nothing is detected while it exists.

**Headphones only.** Before the microphone is opened, and at every poll while consented, the default output devices (console and communications role) are read through Core Audio (`PKEY_AudioEndpoint_FormFactor`, `STGM_READ`, ~10 ms). Unless every one is `headphones`/`headset`/`handset` (`[calls] headphone_form_factors`) or a device the user listed in `headphone_devices` (headphones on an audio interface that Windows calls "Speakers"), the microphone stays closed (`waiting_headphones`, amber indicator) and the card says the microphone records only with headphones, so other people's voices aren't picked up through it. The call app's own sound needs no headphones (it comes from the app, not the room) and is kept while the microphone waits (report 60). Switching to speakers mid-call closes the microphone; back on headphones it reopens. On this PC (2026-09-24): output "Airpods (User's AirPods Pro)" = `headphones` (both roles); other outputs: "Speakers (4- M-Audio AIR 192 4)" = `speakers`, "VG271U (NVIDIA High Definition Audio)" = `digital_display`.

**Which microphone, and sharing it.** The input recorded is the one the call app itself records from (its active audio session, `IAudioSessionManager2`, session state only), else the default communications input, else `[calls] capture_device`. Capture is WASAPI **shared mode** only (PortAudio via `sounddevice`, `auto_convert` to 16 kHz; else native rate resampled with PyAV), so it never takes the device from the call app, Handy or anything else. On this PC: "Line 1/2 (4- M-Audio AIR 192 4)" (Handy's microphone, default console input) is 48 kHz/32-bit stereo shared and has **exclusive mode disabled** in its settings, so no app can lock it; "Headset (AirPods Pro)" (default communications input) is 16 kHz mono, exclusive allowed (Windows default) - neither Handy (cpal, shared) nor Yuki asks for exclusive. Of a stereo input the louder channel of each 100 ms block is kept.

**The speech gate.** Blocks live in an in-memory queue. 32 ms frames (one Silero window, 512 samples) under `gate_dbfs` (-50 dBFS) are silence and no model runs on them; louder frames are speech only when **Silero VAD v6** (the 1.2 MB ONNX file shipped inside faster-whisper, found without importing it; onnxruntime, one thread, no arena; hysteresis `vad_threshold` 0.5 to start, 0.35 to go on) says so. Silence before speech is dropped as it comes (0.3 s pre-roll); speech is cut into chunks at a pause of `pause_s` (0.7 s) once `chunk_min_s` (6 s) long, after a 2.8 s pause whatever the length, or at `chunk_max_s` (25 s); a chunk with under `min_speech_s` (0.5 s) of speech is dropped, never kept. Why: the live Discord call of 2026-09-24 (M-Audio line input) logged `speech_s` = `audio_s` on every 25 s chunk with the old -45 dBFS energy gate - its noise floor is above it - and Whisper then found nothing in most of them. Measured on synthetic scenes (3 sentences, 28 s of speech in 72 s):

| Background | Energy gate -45 dBFS keeps | Silero keeps | Silero CPU |
|---|---|---|---|
| room noise -56 dBFS | 26.3 s | 26.4 s | 0.9 ms per s of audio |
| hot line input, noise -38 dBFS | 72.0 s (everything) | 26.5 s | 2.8 ms/s |
| noise -30 dBFS | 72.0 s | 26.8 s | 2.8 ms/s |
| music -30 dBFS | 72.0 s | 26.3 s | 2.6 ms/s |
| clicks -25 dBFS | 26.3 s | 26.3 s | 1.5 ms/s |

Silero costs at most ~0.3% of one core while sound comes in (156 ms of CPU for 60 s of loud audio) and nothing on silence. On the 53 s synthetic call with -38 dBFS noise under it, 25.8 s (the speech) was spooled.

**The spool: encrypted, compressed, temporary.** Each chunk the gate passes, on every track (the microphone, each app's own sound; the track is in the file name and in `call_chunks.side` / `track_*`), is compressed in memory to Opus in Ogg (PyAV's libopus, 24 kbps mono; int16 + zlib if libopus is missing), encrypted with the store's key (AES-256-GCM, the key protected by DPAPI) and written as `<database folder>\spool\call<id>-<seq>-<rand>.yspool` (magic `YSP1` + ciphertext; written to `.part` and renamed, then its `call_chunks` row). Nothing is ever written in plaintext; the file has no Ogg header visible. ~11 MB per hour of speech (the 53 s test call: 76 KB for 25.8 s). Caps: audio older than `spool_max_age_days` (7) and, oldest first, anything past `spool_max_mb` (500) is deleted and logged (`call_spool_pruned`: reason, chunks, bytes); files without a row (a crash between writing and recording) are removed, rows without a file become `lost`. No and Never keep nothing; "Stop remembering this call" stops the microphone and keeps what was already spooled (as before, when it kept the text so far); **"Erase waiting call audio"** (tray) deletes every chunk still waiting. The tray shows "N calls waiting to be remembered" (and the Today panel's meta line) while any wait.

**Deferred speech to text** (`call_stt_model`, pluggable: `make_backend("faster-whisper:<model>")`). The scheduler (`TranscribeScheduler`, in the service) looks once a minute, and only while audio waits. It starts a batch only when **all** of these hold: no call row is open (a call going on, answered or not); nothing full screen in front (the timeline's fact) and Windows does not report a game, full-screen app or presentation (`SHQueryUserNotificationState`); for `defer_gpu_quiet_min` (3 min) of NVML samples the GPU stayed at or under `defer_gpu_max_util` (10%) **and** kept `defer_vram_free_mb` (2,500 MB) free - VRAM counts because a game left open holds it while idle, and large-v3 needs ~1.9 GB; the system CPU stayed under `defer_cpu_max_percent` (75% since 2026-09-26; 40% held it back all day behind builds, Chrome and editor sessions while the worker itself runs on the GPU at below-normal priority). Then it starts when the user is away (no input for `defer_idle_min`, 5 min, or the session is locked), at night (`defer_night`, 01:00-07:00), or, since 2026-09-26, with the user **present** (`defer_when_present`, on: the owner's "process it when no GPU is being used"; they turn the PC off at night and are rarely away, so 590 chunks of calls 20-22 had waited). It then runs `python -m yuki.memory.transcribe` as its own process at below-normal priority. The worker loads the model (CTranslate2 Whisper on CUDA, int8_float16; else `call_stt_fallback` on the CPU, greedy), transcribes the waiting chunks of ended calls oldest first (decrypt, decode in memory, transcribe as in "Transcript quality" below), and per chunk stores the words encrypted and **then** deletes the audio. When a call has no chunk left waiting its words become one `my_call_side` capture for the journal; when the worker exits the scheduler wakes the journal (`Store.wake`), so the call is journaled within seconds instead of at the journal's next 3-minute timeout (captures written by another process are not seen before it). The worker exits after the batch: model, RAM (~3 GB peak) and VRAM (~1.9 GB) go with the process at once. While it runs the scheduler checks every 5 s and asks it to stop after the chunk in hand (named event) when a call starts, something goes full screen, memory is paused, or, in a present run, other programs take the GPU (NVML's per-process utilisation, the worker and its child process left out, summed and averaged over `defer_others_window_s`, 30 s, above `defer_gpu_others_max_util`, 25%: a game in a window) or leave under `defer_vram_min_free_running_mb` (300 MB) of VRAM. An away or locked run carries on as a present run when the user comes back (`call_transcribe_continues`); with `defer_when_present` off it stops then (input within `defer_back_idle_s`, 20 s; not at night), as before; after `defer_stop_grace_s` (90 s) it is killed (each chunk is one transaction, so nothing is half-written), and the rest waits for the next free moment. After a batch the GPU must be seen quiet again for the full window before the next. The service itself never imports a speech model or touches the GPU. Default **large-v3**: it is the only one tested that gets Kannada and Telugu right.

Comparison, 2026-09-24, synthetic speech (Microsoft neural TTS voices, generated for the test; clean speech, so real calls will do worse), RTX 2060 6 GB shared with the user's Discord call, CPU at ~99% from other load during the run:

| Sample (length) | large-v3-turbo, GPU | large-v3, GPU | small, CPU |
|---|---|---|---|
| English (13.5 s) | exact, 1.5 s | exact, 2.4 s | exact, 22 s |
| Hindi (9.8 s) | good (2 spelling slips), 1.3 s | good, 7.2 s | poor, 22 s |
| Hindi-English mixed (9.0 s) | good, English words in Devanagari, 1.3 s | same, 4.5 s | poor, 24 s |
| Kannada (5.5 s) | wrong: taken for Tamil, Devanagari transliteration | right script, near-exact, 4.8 s | Devanagari transliteration, 31 s |
| Kannada-English mixed (4.2 s) | garbled ("NANI Client Collide") | garbled | garbled |
| Tamil (5.5 s) | good (2 slips), 1.0 s | good (1 slip), 2.8 s | good, 16 s |
| Telugu (4.9 s) | wrong: taken for Tamil, Tamil script | right script, near-exact, 4.1 s | Devanagari transliteration, 17 s |
| Load (cold) / VRAM added | 26 s / +1.2 GB | 18-34 s / +1.9 GB (peak 4.2 of 6 GB) | 8 s / none (RSS 364 MB) |

Handy's model (the owner's dictation app, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Handy`): `handy-computer/nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b-gguf`, file `nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b-Q8_0.gguf` (751 MB, in `%USERPROFILE%\.cache\huggingface\hub`), a GGUF of NVIDIA's `nemotron-3.5-asr-streaming-0.6b` (cache-aware FastConformer-RNNT, 600M), licence OpenMDW-1.1, run by Handy through transcribe.cpp (ggml, Vulkan on the RTX 2060). Its language list (Handy's log) has Hindi but **no Kannada, Tamil or Telugu**, and transcribe.cpp has no Python binding (the NeMo original would need PyTorch + NeMo), so it is not a backend here; `make_backend` refuses other names.

**Storage** (migration 10, additive). `calls(id, app, process, service, via mic|meeting, detected_at, ended_at, status asking|recording|waiting_headphones|declined|unanswered|not_asked|stopped|ended, consent yes|no|always|never|none, consent_by card|pref|quiet|ui_timeout|call_ended, consent_at, output_form_factor, output_device, headphones, recording_started_at, recording_stopped_at, stop_requested, stop_reason, thread_id, chunks, chunks_silent, speech_s, audio_s, utterances, transcribe_ms, stt_model, stt_device, captures, updated_at)` - content-free. `call_prefs(app, decision always|never, name, updated_at)`. The words are captures of kind `my_call_side` (thread per call, scope `call:<id>`, JSON `{call_id, app, start, end, final, utterances: [{at, end, text, lang}]}`, encrypted, capture TTL 30 days), written by the deferred worker once all of a call's chunks are transcribed. Migration 11 (additive): `call_chunks(id, call_id, seq, start_at, audio_s, speech_s, file, bytes, codec, status spooled|done|journaled|failed|expired|capped|erased|lost, attempts, text_ciphertext, lang, transcribe_ms, stt_model, stt_device, error, created_at, done_at, removed_at)` - cleartext metadata only; the words sit encrypted in `text_ciphertext` between transcription and the journal, then are cleared. A chunk failing 3 times is given up and its audio deleted. A service restart ends calls left open (`service_restart`); their spooled audio stays and is transcribed later. Migration 34 (additive, report 60): `calls.kind` (call | game), `call_chunks.side` (user | others), `track_app`, `track_process`, `track_kind`, `track_call_id`, the table `mic_spans`; a capture's JSON adds `kind` and `lines` - every line of every track in time order, each with `who` (user | others), `app`, `track_kind` (call | game) and, on the user's lines, `heard_by` - beside `utterances` (the user's lines only, which every reader written before 3 Oct expects). Every call reader goes through `Store.call_lines(data)` (a capture from before 3 Oct reads as the user's own lines). `calls.speech_s`/`utterances` stay the user's own; the others' speech per track is `Store.track_speech`.

**Memory** (reading both sides: report 61, 4 Oct 2026). The journal reads a call through `Store.call_lines`: one source per line of every track, labelled with who spoke - `[n] THE USER SAID <time> (in the Discord call, mic open to: Discord, VALORANT, transcribed as 'en'): "..."`, `[n] OTHERS SAID <time> (on the Discord call: the call app's own sound, everyone else on it, ...)`, `[n] GAME VOICE SAID <time> (in VALORANT's voice chat: teammates or the game's own voice lines, ...)` - under a header naming the sides present (a capture from before 3 Oct reads as the user's own lines). The prompt: record what the user said, decided, planned, promised or asked for, naming the app, and from the others only what `CALL_SPEAKERS_GUIDANCE` allows, always saying who said it; skip what is too garbled; facts in English. Call batches add `CALL_TRANSCRIPT_GUIDANCE` (the transcript is noisy: Whisper invents short repeated phrases on noise - skip them; it swaps words for commoner sound-alikes and writes English inside Hindi phonetically in Devanagari - read each line in context and check a fact's words make sense together; never build a fact on one odd word, a repeated fragment or an `UNCLEAR` line alone; a short answer reads the others' line just before it for its question) and `CALL_SPEAKERS_GUIDANCE` (model judgement with worked examples, invented names, never a word list): **attribution is absolute** - only THE USER lines are the user's words, so a friend's "I'll send you the photos tomorrow" is the friend's plan, OTHERS "can you send me the hostel link?" then THE USER "yeah sure, after dinner" is the user's commitment quoted from the user's own line, and "perfect" after "Kabir is picking you up at 7" is information, not a promise; **one track per app** - a speaker is named only when the context's call screen shows exactly one other person, the line names its speaker, or the user answers them by name right then, else "someone on the call" / "a teammate"; **games** - the user's lines go to the teammates when timing, content and "mic open to" fit the game, to the friends when they fit that talk; teammates are strangers, never kept as people the user knows, never named or asked about; the game's own voice lines are nobody's words; one fact at most per match; **worth keeping from the others** - what they asked the user, plans that involve the user, information the user needs later (a date, a link, an address, a decision), never their private matters (health, relationships, money, family) unless it involves the user. `heard_by` is shown as "mic open to: ..." ("no app": nobody on the call or in the game heard that line; a hint, since an app may keep the microphone open while the user is muted in it). Call batches use the forced strict tool `record_call_side` (the name stays): `facts` (as `record_facts`, each with its nature - a possibly misheard fact is nature `uncertain` since 2026-09-25; jokes are kept as `humor`, see "Personality") plus `commitments` (`text`, `title`, `quote`, `due`, `source`, `subject`, `nature`) - things the user said *they* will do, or agreed to do when someone asked, one per thing taken on. The quote rule applies in code **against the user's own lines only**: a commitment citing an OTHERS or GAME line is rejected whatever its quote (`cites someone else's line, not the user's words`), the quote must be a span of the cited line (`quote_in`), and only a `fact` nature becomes a to-do; reasons are logged, never words. A question (names the user said) citing someone else's line is dropped the same way, so a teammate's name is never asked about. A fact citing someone else's line keeps provenance `who: others`, `by: someone on the call` (or `a teammate or the game`) and reads "someone on the call said, not the user: ..."; a short line's quote takes the line before it as context only when the same side said it. Kept commitments are conversation facts of kind `commitment`, origin `call`, written with the batch in one transaction (`commit_call_batch`), so they reach `todos()` (`commit:<id>`, evidence "the user said in a call on <date>: "..."") and reminders, and `standing_context()` lists them under "What the user said they would do, in calls". Open call commitments are shown to the model as already recorded so none is added twice. A long call is cut into parts of about `batch_chars` (12k) characters of lines (every side), one model call each, the earlier parts' facts and to-dos shown to the later ones as already recorded, and everything committed in one transaction (a 30-minute call used to be one ~37k-token request; a longer one could outgrow the output limit and be given up after 3 attempts). The joke closer (`commitments.close_joke_commitments`) ends a call to-do only on a digest `humor` line resting on the user's own line at its moment, never on a friend's joke at the same second.

**Stopping.** The tray item "Stop remembering this call" (visible only while recording or waiting for headphones) calls `stop_call()`; the worker closes the microphone and every app track and spools their last chunks; the call stays `stopped` (no second card). The strip corner shows a small pill (the recording dot and what is kept; "Waiting for headphones" while the microphone waits - the words are the UI wording pass's) while on; it steps aside for a nudge or consent card, sits above the task strip, and hides while Windows reports a full-screen app, a game or presentation mode.

**Calls in memory's outputs** (2026-09-26; before, nothing downstream read `calls`, so "how long am I in Discord" got 41 min of window time during a long call). `yuki/memory/callspans.py` (content-free, nothing decrypted) merges the rows of one app into one call when the earlier row ended by `service_restart` and the next began within 10 min, or when the next began within 20 min whatever ended it. Measured on two days of real rows: every restart-split pair touches (gap 0 s: the new service picks the call up the second it closes the old row, also across a reboot); one meeting was 9 rows with gaps of 5 s-9 min (muting); a long voice call dropped once for 15 min. Each call keeps its real start and end (or "still going") outside the window asked about, the part inside it, the time its app was in front (present time of its own rows, meeting rows included), the other apps in front meanwhile, gaps without the microphone and the PC's power spans inside it. `MemoryClient.activity` adds `calls` (with a plain `text` line each), `call_s` on the item of the call's app, and `background` (call time behind other windows); the agent's `activity` text and MCP `get_activity` print meetings, calls and background, and say time in an app is reported three ways. Example output for a day, by app: "Discord: in front 52m ...; in a call 4h10m" and "Discord call Fri 18:00-22:10: 4h10m (no microphone 21:00-21:15). Discord was in front 1h01m of it; meanwhile a game 1h30m, a browser 1h05m." (~90 ms for a day). The request-origin line names a microphone user that is a call memory knows (`current_calls`, the calls table only): "Discord.exe, on a Discord call since 18:00 (2h10m)" instead of Windows' microphone start after a reboot. The coach gets the same line (`coach_calls_text`).

**Timeline digests: what happened on a call, when** (2026-09-27; "what happened on Friday's Discord call, timeline wise" got four lines: the journal keeps a call as a handful of facts and nothing could read the call itself). Code: `yuki/memory/calldigest.py` (worker, thread `yuki-memory-calldigest`, and the command), `store.py` (migration 19), `api.py` (`calls_in`, `call_timeline`, `call_transcript`), `callspans.merged_calls`, `yuki/agent/{tools,memory,prompt,subagents}.py`, `yuki/voice/mouth.py`. Tests: `tests/test_call_digest.py`.
- *What it is.* One digest per `calls` row: time blocks where the talk moved, each with a topic and short English lines of what the user said, asked, decided, planned, promised or joked about (and to whom, when the evidence names them) and, since 3 Oct 2026, what the others asked of the user or planned with them, each dated by the transcript line it rests on; plus a one-line gist of the call. Lines carry the journal's natures (a joke is `humor`, a possibly misheard line `uncertain`) and whose line they rest on: `who` (user | others), `app`, `track_kind` (the report 61 contract; a line without `who`, from an older digest, is the user's). A bare verb is the user; a line resting on someone else's line names who said it ("Someone on the call asked the user for the hostel link"). A game's own voice chat with no call (`kind` game) gets a few lines at most, a match during a call one line at most, the others' private matters none. `speech_s` stays the user's own speech.
- *Who decides.* Code does structure: it reads the call's `my_call_side` captures through `Store.call_lines` (every track), leaves out a line whose words equal one of the 4 before it on the same track within 5 min (Whisper's loop on one recording's noise: identity, not words; the same words on another track are no loop), cuts a long call into ~10k-character parts at the longest silence in each part's back half, packs small calls into one request, numbers the lines and labels each with who spoke (`USER`, `OTHERS on <app>`, `GAME on <game>`; the user's with "mic open to: ..." from `heard_by`), marks silences of 2 min or more, and checks the answer (existing utterance numbers, blocks in order and inside one call, lines inside their call); every time comes from the utterances. Haiku 4.5 (forced strict `write_call_timeline`) decides the block boundaries (topic or activity changes, long silences), what each line says, translation from Hindi/Devanagari and other languages, noise and natures, and whose words each line reports (`speaker`); the prompt carries `CALL_TRANSCRIPT_GUIDANCE` and `CALL_SPEAKERS_GUIDANCE`. A line the model says reports the user's words but pins on the line they answered is moved onto the user's own nearest line, and the reverse (`rest_on`, within 6 lines: plumbing on the model's label and the lines' metadata; the 4 Oct check pinned "Agreed to send the hostel link" on the friend's request in 2 of 2 runs before it); the line's stored `who` follows the line it rests on, never the model. A later part of a call (or the next row of a restart-split session) sees the gist so far and the last three blocks as EARLIER, for continuity.
- *When.* Once every capture of an ended call is journaled (the journal goes first), checked every 5 min; never while any call row is open, something is full screen or memory is paused. A call that gains captures is digested again; a failing one is given up after 3 attempts. `python -m yuki.memory.calldigest --backfill [--calls 16,17] [--force] [--dry-run] [--db]` runs the same pipeline over every call that still has its words; `--show <id>` prints one.
- *Storage* (migration 19, additive): `call_digests(call_id, app, started_at, ended_at, speech_s, utterances, kept, capture_ids, status running|ok|empty|failed, gist_ciphertext, blocks_ciphertext, model, requests, tokens, cost_usd, latency_ms, attempts, error, claimed_by, claimed_at, ...)`. Encrypted, never pruned: the exact words go with the 30-day capture TTL, the digest stays. Cost counts toward `memory_cost_today_usd`; model calls are logged in `logs/memory/calldigest-YYYYMMDD.jsonl` (usage, cost, latency in the clear; request and response encrypted).
- *Reading it.* `MemoryClient.calls_in(since, until)`: the calls and meetings of a window, rows of one session merged (`callspans`' rule), each with app, start and end, time on the call, the user's own speech, whether it was recorded, per-row state (timeline written, audio waiting, not recorded and why) and gists. `call_timeline(call, since, until)`: the whole session's blocks in order (any row id opens its session), cut to a window when asked. `call_transcript(call, since, until, page)`: the exact lines (every side since 3 Oct; how they are labelled and filtered is the agent/API wording pass's), 60 a page, loops left out and counted on the line they repeat, unclear lines marked. `recall` hits heard on a call with a digest carry `call: "call:<id>"`, and the agent's recall text then says to open that call.
- *The agent.* Tools `calls` (list) and `call_timeline` (the timeline, or `words: true` for a page of exact words), also given to the read-only helpers; results are fenced as memory text. The prompt: a question about a call or meeting is answered from these, with the timeline and its times; whose words each line is, kept straight (the agent's wording: its own pass); typed answers give the timeline line by line, spoken ones the two or three moments that matter and offer the rest. The voice mouth hands such questions to the brain (`tell_yuki`), with a worked example.
- *Measured 2026-09-27* (real calls, Haiku 4.5 list prices): calls 16-17 (15 min of the user's speech, 201 utterances): 2 requests, 13.4k in / 3.4k out tokens, $0.030, i.e. **about $0.12 per hour of the user's speech**; a busy day of calls (1-1.5 h of speech) costs ~$0.15. The system prompt plus tool is ~2.2k tokens, under Haiku 4.5's 4,096-token cache minimum, so the cache breakpoint does not take effect; the saving is the stored digest. Backfill of every call still holding its words: see the handbook's status for the run.
- *Speech said to Yuki during a call* (the user's microphone; only the user's own lines are ever marked - the call app's sound never carries speech to Yuki, and `yuki_spans_for` reads only the user's lines' times): found from the voice turns in conversation memory (outcome `voice`), by time only - a turn is logged when it closes, so the words it answered ended up to 20 s before it (`YUKI_TURN_LEAD_S`; 2-17 s measured on 25 Sept 14:10-14:12), and the wake word comes up to 25 s before the voice session's start (read from our own `voice-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS-...` request id). A turn voice judged said to Yuki (`addressed = yuki`) leaves its words out of the digest; a turn with no judgement (before voice v3.5) or a wake only marks them YUKI LISTENING, and Haiku leaves out what was plainly to the assistant; `addressed = others` changes nothing. The exact transcript marks them ("said to Yuki", "maybe said to Yuki"). On the real calls: 64 utterances in 8 calls.
- *Answer shape.* `call_timeline` returns the call in stretches (`calldigest.stretches`: blocks less than 20 min apart, a stretch over 90 min cut at its longest pause), each a heading line with its time range and topics, then its moments (a block's time and topic) with the dated details under each; the tool description and the prompt (with a worked example) say a written answer keeps that shape. The pronoun rule: lines leave the user out or say "the user", never he/she.
- *Meetings with no call row* (the timeline's meeting rows, an app in front holding the microphone, no `calls` row of that app or service overlapping): `calls_in` lists them as "[meeting, no transcript]" with their hours.
- *MCP.* `get_calls` and `get_call_timeline` (read-only; `words` for the exact transcript) mirror the agent tools, with the same text.
- *Limits.* Speech to Yuki is found by timing, so call talk within 20 s before a Yuki turn is left out too (or marked, for turns of unknown addressee); a block's topic can be vague on a noisy stretch; the agent's own reply can still use "he" for the user.

**Context from what's around the call** (2026-10-01; the user's choice "use what's around the call": their own half of a call is thin - "yeah do it", "send him that link"). Code: `yuki/memory/callcontext.py` (new), `calldigest.py`, `journal.py`, `store.py` (migration 25, `captures_around`), `api.py`, `links.py`/`related.py` (the digest's recall text), `yuki/agent/memory.py`. Tests: `tests/test_call_context.py`. **No new capture source**: the pack reads no audio and no transcript, no pixels - only screen text the watcher already stored, links seen and threads of work. Since both sides are recorded (3 Oct) it is still the only evidence of *who* was on the call: an OTHERS line is given a name only when the call screen shows exactly one other person then, or the line itself settles it.
- *The pack* (`callcontext.build` / `for_call`, plumbing on times, windows and sizes, 4-20 ms a call on the copy): for a call's span, each item with its local time: **CALL APP** (the call app's own windows from 15 min before the row began to its end - voice channel view, participants or tiles, the call's chat, captions - lines already shown not repeated; conversations from their stored messages, only new ones or ones dated around the call), **OTHER SCREENS** (every other window seen during the call, one line per window or page: app, title, site, first-last time, a head of its text; marked beside / popup / put there by Yuki), **LINKS** (pages first opened, links shared, during the call), **MESSAGES AROUND** (chats and mail in the 30 min before and after), **THREADS OF WORK** (touched during the call), **NAMES** (the user's alias corrections). Caps per section, ~7k characters in all (3.4-5.5k measured on 7 real calls). A long call's parts each get their own span; the joining screens and the chats before go with the first part only, the chats after with the last.
- *Digest.* Each call part's request carries `CONTEXT AROUND CALL n` inside the untrusted fence, before the utterances. The prompt: resolve "him", "that link", "the thing on screen", "yeah do it" only when the context makes it plain at that time, else keep the words or make the line uncertain; every line rests on a transcript line (never a line that only describes the screen); with exactly one other person on the call screen at that moment the OTHERS lines are that person's; what someone *wrote* on screen (the call's chat, captions) may be quoted only as on-screen text; spell names as memory does. `write_call_timeline`'s `calls` items add `people` (`name`, `how`: `on_call_screen` | `call_chat` | `likely` - screen evidence only: a fourth kind, "the user spoke to them by name", gave Claude, a colleague read aloud from a page and friends only talked about on 3 of 4 calls, so a name the user only says stays in the lines) and `where` (app, server/channel or meeting title). Checked in code (`parse_people`): a name must occur in the context the model read (the commitment-quote rule), one of the user's own names is never a participant, one entry per name with its strongest `how`, at most 12. Stored encrypted in `call_digests.context_ciphertext` (migration 25, additive); `calls_in`/`call_timeline` return `people` and `where`, and the agent's and MCP's call texts print "Where: ... | With (read from the screen around the call, never from their audio): ...". `CallDigestWorker(context=False)` / `--no-context` is the old digest.
- *Journal.* A call batch (`record_call_side`) gets the same pack per part after its sources, and `CALL_CONTEXT_GUIDANCE` (call batches only; screen batches keep their prompt and cache): never a fact source - every fact must be carried by the call line it cites, nothing recorded because it was on screen - only to resolve who and what the user's words refer to (a commitment's text may name them; its quote stays the user's words). The windows seen during the call are offered to the thread-of-work candidates as the batch's own (`offered_threads(windows=...)`).
- *Backfill* (Yuki's own pipeline): `python -m yuki.memory.calldigest --last N --force [--rejournal] [--portrait skip] [--dry-run] [--db]` (`--last N`: the N most recent ended calls that still hold their words; `--rejournal`: first `yuki.memory.rejournal` over those calls' `my_call_side` captures, then the digests); `--show-context <id>` prints one pack, no model.
- *Measured* (copy of the real memory, 1 Oct; the Discord calls 39, 40, 42, 44 of 29-30 Sept, 69 min of the user's speech, Haiku 4.5; before = the stored digests): **where** came out on 3 of 4 (server and voice channel from the window title); **who** from the call screen: call 44 the friend streaming in the voice channel (right), call 39 the user's own tile under a handle memory does not list as theirs (wrong: the identity check compares whole names), calls 40/42 only "likely" names from the chats around; references ("the other person", "him") were mostly left as said - the model keeps the words when two or more people were on screen - and the lines otherwise match the old ones (343 against 341 lines); nothing anyone said was invented. The alias spellings (Varun, Komal) were used in one of three readings. Journal (call 44 re-journaled): the call attached to the same thread of work as before (the windows during the call offered it first); one of 20 facts came from the screen alone (a stream's resolution). A first version counted the context in the request packing and put a full part of one call into another call's request: 26 minutes of call 42 got no lines; packing is by utterances again, the context capped on its own (`pack_requests(max_context)`). Cost: the digest $0.1365 -> $0.156 for the four (9 requests, 27k characters of context): **+$0.005 a call (+14%), ~$0.135 per hour of speech**; re-journaling a call with the pack $0.043 (call 44, 3 parts). CPU: the pack is built inside the deferred digest and journal steps (nothing periodic), 4-20 ms a call, a few MB at most.
