- *Limits.* Screen text is what the watcher kept: a voice panel read only beside the window in front, or not at all while a game is full screen, shows nobody; captures are deltas, so a participant list is only as complete as what changed on screen; a pack reads at most 900 captures of its span (the earliest). Only Discord calls were in the data: Meet, Zoom and Teams screens (participant lists, in-call chat, captions) go through the same pack but are unmeasured. The user's own tile under an unlisted handle can be taken for someone else. Resolution is the model's reading; a wrong "him" is possible where two people were on screen at that moment (the prompt says keep the words then). The journal still sometimes writes a fact from the screen during the call (on call 44, 1 of 20 after the stricter guidance).

**Logs** (service log, content-free): `call_detected` (app, via, service, headphones, form factor, asked), `call_consent` (answer, by, seconds to answer), `call_headphones`, `call_recording_start` (device, how opened, VAD on), `call_chunk` (audio s, speech s, bytes spooled, codec, encode ms, VAD CPU s), `call_recording_paused`/`resumed`, `call_recording_stop` (chunks, silent chunks, silence dropped, spooled bytes, VAD windows and CPU), `call_ended`, `call_transcribe_waiting` (the gate that holds it, logged when it changes), `call_transcribe_start` (why: away, locked, night or present; GPU load and free VRAM; chunks, seconds), `call_transcribe_continues` (an away run the user came back to), `call_transcribe_stop` (done, or which interruption; chunks left), `call_transcribe_killed`, `call_spool_pruned`. The worker's `logs/memory/transcribe-YYYYMMDD.jsonl`: model, device, load ms, `transcribe_hint` (terms, tokens, ms - never the words), per chunk ms, segments, `dropped` (by reason), `unclear`, language code and probability, `decodes`, `tokens`, `max_new_tokens`, `timed_out`, GPU load; the worker's totals add `chunks_timed_out`, `segments_dropped`, `utterances_unclear`. The journal log adds `utterances` (every side's lines), `users_lines`, `unclear`, `parts`, `commitments` and `commitments_rejected` (reasons only, e.g. "cites someone else's line, not the user's words"); the digest's `call_digest` log line adds `call_kind`, `others` (transcript lines from someone else) and `others_lines` (digest lines resting on them). UI log: `call_status`, `call_consent_shown`, `call_consent_answer`, `call_stop_requested`, `call_audio_erase_requested`.

**Measured 2026-09-24 evening, deferred** (temp database, the 53 s synthetic call with -38 dBFS noise under it): during the call the service process spent 0.8 s of CPU in all (VAD 0.16 s, Opus 88 ms median per chunk) and never loaded ctranslate2 or faster-whisper; 4 chunks, 76 KB spooled. With the gates open, the worker ran 36 s (model load 19 s, 4 chunks 1.1-8.4 s), peak RSS 3.2 GB and ~2 GB of VRAM, then exited: VRAM back to the desktop's 1.7 GB, spool empty, one `my_call_side` capture with the same words as when it was transcribed live (Opus at 24 kbps changed nothing). Interruption: 8 chunks waiting, the user "came back" after the first chunk - the worker stopped 11 s later at the chunk boundary with 2 done, 6 left; the next free moment finished them and journaled the call. Caps: an orphan file, a 9-day-old chunk (`expired`) and 2 chunks over a halved size cap (`capped`) were removed; Erase removed the rest. Gate paths: user present, GPU busy, full screen, night-while-present (runs) all as specified.


**Transcript quality (2026-09-25).** A first night of real calls (4 calls, 74 min of speech, 1,323 utterances: 755 Hindi, 562 English, a few taken for kn/ta/te) showed four faults: Whisper's loop on non-speech (breathing, clicks, game sound) - "कर दो" 88 times in all, in runs of up to 7, "अजय को" 41, "झाल" 32 (14% of utterances sat in runs of one phrase); sound-alikes ("praises" as "prices", which the journal then stated as fact); English words inside Hindi transliterated into Devanagari ("सो यूजिंग क्लॉड", "कोवर्क"); and one chunk that took 301 s. What the worker does now (`FasterWhisperBackend.transcribe`, `screen_segments`, `vocabulary_hint` in `calls.py`; knobs `stt_*` in `[calls]`):
- **Language first, one decode.** Whisper's language detection (on the speech, VAD) picks the most likely of `languages`, then the chunk is decoded once in it; it used to be decoded a second time whenever Whisper's first guess was outside the list. Beam 5, `condition_on_previous_text=False`, faster-whisper's VAD.
- **Bounded decoding.** Temperature fallback `[0, 0.4, 0.8]` (was six steps to 1.0, each hot step sampling 5 candidates); each decode writes at most `stt_tokens_per_s` x the chunk's seconds (per language, ~2x what speech needs: en 8, hi 28, ta 26, kn 60, te 52; Indian scripts cost many tokens), so a loop stops there instead of at Whisper's 448; a guard around CTranslate2's `generate` starts no new decode after `stt_chunk_limit_s` + `stt_chunk_limit_rtf` x seconds (10 s + 1x) and keeps what was decoded (`timed_out` in the log).
- **Kept only when Whisper's own probabilities support it** (`screen_segments`): dropped when the no-speech probability is over 0.2 while the average log-probability is under -0.8 (unsure there is speech and unsure of the words), when the text still compresses over 2.4 after the fallback (a loop), or when it is an exact repeat of the previous segment of the call (followed across chunks), at most 12 tokens and unclear. A kept segment is `unclear` under -0.6 log-probability or over 0.4 no-speech; the utterance carries `unclear`, `lp` and `nsp` (numbers only), and the journal sees `UNCLEAR`.
- **Vocabulary hint from memory** (`hotwords`, built once per batch, 40-70 ms): words memory writes with a capital in at least 2 texts of the portrait, the conversation facts and the last 14 days of the journal, that Whisper's own tokenizer needs 2+ tokens for in every casing (it does not know them; a word it knows needs no hint), ranked by texts x (tokens - 1), up to `stt_vocab_tokens` (40). From the real memory: "Claude, Corvana, PostHog, Varun, Raghunathan, OpenSchool, Fable, Komal, Aster, Ravi, Vercel". Only for `stt_vocab_languages` (en, hi): with Latin names in its prompt Whisper translated Kannada speech into English (CER 0.05 -> 0.52). 20 tokens found fewer names; 80 tokens lowered Hinglish accuracy and made noise produce text more often.
- **Hinglish**: a style example per language (`[calls.stt_prompts]`, hi: Devanagari with the English words in Latin script) as the previous text, so English words in code-mixed speech come out in Latin script. Forcing `en` with a romanized prompt romanizes everything (English-word recall 0.81) but also pure Hindi, and without the prompt `en` translated a Hindi sentence into English: not used.

Measured on synthetic clips (Microsoft neural voices via edge-tts - the ElevenLabs key lacks the text_to_speech permission; 21 sentences clean, 18 with desk or game noise under them, 21 "hard" ones (quiet, room echo, duller mic, noise), 34 noise clips; Opus round trip; large-v3 on the RTX 2060):

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Noise that passes the VAD (reversed speech, babble, short fragments; 16 clips): clips with any text | 100% (6.3 words) | 44% (2.4 words), all `unclear` |
| Plain noise (keys, mouse, breathing, game, fan; 18 clips) | 0% | 0% |
| Hinglish: English words in Latin script (clean / noisy / hard) | 23% / 51% / 11% | 78% / 87% / 29% |
| Hinglish: memory's names right (Claude, OpenSchool, Fable, ...) clean / noisy | 14% / 25% | 57% / 75% |
| Hindi CER clean / hard; English CER clean / hard | 0.026 / 0.065; 0 / 0.22 | 0.033 / 0.058; 0 / 0.12 |
| Kannada / Tamil / Telugu CER (clean) | 0.05 / 0.06 / 0.08 | same (no prompt, no hint) |
| Hint words heard where they were not said (speech and noise) | - | 0 |
| All 98 clips: time; slowest clip; decoder tokens | 250 s; 32.4 s (a 5 s hard Telugu clip: 7 decodes, 3,381 tokens); 10,909 | 171 s; 5.4 s; 4,612 |
| A synthetic call through gate, spool and worker (217 s, 22 chunks) | 67-88 s | 62-63 s |

The stall: chunk 75 (25.0 s, Hindi) came back as "इस समय भाग भाग भाग ..." (one segment): a decoding loop. Each attempt ran to Whisper's 448-token cap (Devanagari costs ~3 tokens a syllable), failed the compression-ratio check and was retried at the next temperature with 5 sampled candidates, through all six temperatures - and twice over when the first language guess was outside the list: 300 s of GPU. Chunks 43, 78, 79 and 123 (1.2 s of audio, 28 s) were the same loop, shorter. Reproduced: the 5 s hard Telugu clip above took 32 s (7 decodes); a 25 s reversed-Hindi chunk 22-42 s before, 19-21 s after (3 decodes, the loop dropped).

Journal of a noisy synthetic call (Haiku, 4 runs each, ~$0.06 in all): the old prompt put "generic prices" into a fact in 2 of 4 runs ("gives generic feedback and pricing"); the new one in none (one fact marked unclear); neither made a fact of "कर दो", "अजय को" or "Thank you for watching".


**Measured 2026-09-24** (temp database, a 53 s synthetic call: English sentences with two commitments, a Hindi-English sentence, a Kannada sentence, 27 s of -56 dBFS room noise between them; large-v3 on the GPU; Haiku 4.5 list prices):
- Gate: 26.9 s of silence dropped unheard; 4 chunks (25.8 s of audio, 19.1 s voiced) transcribed into 5 utterances.
- Per chunk: 1.1-4.9 s (p50 3.8 s), real-time factor 0.24-0.96, CPU 1-8 s per chunk (the host CPU was saturated by other work), GPU 57-80%, VRAM peak 4.2 GB of 6. Model load at the first speech chunk: 34 s cold from disk (audio waited in memory; nothing lost).
- Journal (one `record_call_side` call): 4.1k in / 0.3k out tokens, **$0.0056**, 16 s. Facts: "In a Zoom call, the user said the launch has been moved to 2026-10-05.", "... they will book a flight to Eastvale on Friday (2026-09-26)." Commitments (quotes verified): "Send the updated deck to Varun on 2026-09-25 morning" (quote "I'll send the updated deck to Varun tomorrow morning."), "Talk to Rahul about budget approval" (quote in Devanagari). Both appeared in `todos()` and `standing_context()`.
- A long call costs about one such call per 5 minutes of talk (~$0.006-0.01 each).
- Gate paths (stub STT): speakers -> `waiting_headphones`, microphone never opened, opened when headphones came back and closed again on speakers; No -> declined, nothing opened; Never -> next call of the app declined without a card; Always -> next call recorded without a card; tray Stop -> `stopped`; pause -> recording ended within one poll; snoozed -> `not_asked`; no answer -> `unanswered` at `ask_timeout_s`; a Google Meet meeting row -> a call "Google Meet".

Known limits:
- Code-mixed Kannada-English came out garbled with every model. Hindi-English keeps the Hindi in Devanagari; since 2026-09-25 most English words come out in Latin script (78% on clean synthetic speech), not all. Names Whisper has never heard stay misheard even with the hint ("Corvana" as "कोर्वाना", "Claude" often as "cloud"). The screening thresholds were set on synthetic speech and noise; real speech is noisier, so `unclear` may be marked more often - the `lp`/`nsp` numbers now kept on each utterance let them be checked against real calls. Confident gibberish (Hindi-like words for reversed speech, log-probability -0.3) passes the screen, marked unclear only by its no-speech probability. Haiku recorded nothing from the Kannada sentence ("I'll come to the office at 10 tomorrow and send the report"), and dated the budget talk by the "Friday" that belonged to the deck.
- The CPU fallback is slow on this PC under load (16-31 s per 5-13 s chunk); deferred, it only costs time while the user is away.
- Away is hard to reach on this PC while the mouse drifts (the G402's one-count moves, see "Timeline"); since 2026-09-26 a present run does not need it. Checked on a read-only copy of the real database that day (594 chunks of 3 calls, 56 min of audio): with the user present, CPU at 50% and the GPU quiet the scheduler starts a `present` run after its GPU observation window (2 min); at 80% CPU it waits (`cpu_busy`). `tests/test_voice_speakers.py` covers the gates with stubbed facts (present run, each blocker, a call starting, a game in a window, an away run the user comes back to, present runs off).
- The model load (18-46 s) is not interruptible; a user who comes back during it waits for the load and one chunk (up to ~1 min of GPU work) before the worker exits.
- Which input the call app uses comes from its active audio session; an app that records through a helper process may not be matched, and then the default communications input is used.
- The consent card and the call worker do not know the call's topic or people, by design. Since 3 Oct 2026 the others' words are stored (the call app's own sound, one track per app; a game's voice chat), encrypted like the user's, and read only as theirs (report 61). Since 2026-10-01 the digest names who was on the call *as the screen around it showed*, from screen text Yuki already kept.
- Presence, the coach and nudges read a call only through its digest (labelled lines, the others' private matters already left out), never the raw transcript: `captures_between` and the other generic capture readers leave call transcripts out or show only the user's lines, on purpose (report 61 section 3).

## Terminal history (Warp)

- Source: `%LOCALAPPDATA%\warp\Warp\data\warp.sqlite` (WAL). `WarpReader` in `yuki-memory` opens it every 3 minutes, read-only (`mode=ro` URI, `query_only`, never `immutable`), then closes it. It reads only table `commands`, and only `id, command, exit_code, start_ts, completed_ts (UTC text), pwd, shell, git_branch, session_id, is_agent_executed` - never `blocks`, never output.
- Checkpoint `source_checkpoints('warp.commands')` = the last `commands.id` consumed. The first read starts at the newest command, so earlier history is not journaled. A command without `completed_ts` waits up to 10 min for its exit code; the checkpoint moves only over a contiguous run of taken rows. While memory is paused, `[terminal] enabled = false` or Warp is a blocked app, the checkpoint moves past new commands without reading them.
- Secrets: first the shared detector of every entry point (see "Secret filter"; reason `secret:<kind>`), then the rules below. Secret rules are data (`[terminal] secret_patterns`: case-insensitive regular expressions matched against the command, its folder and its branch). They cover secret-named assignments and env exports, `setx`/`SetEnvironmentVariable`, config-set of such names, secret-named flags with a value, `Authorization:`/bearer/API-key headers, URLs with credentials, known key shapes (sk-, ghp_, github_pat_, xox*, AKIA, AIza, ABSK, JWTs, private keys) and passwords on the command line. A high-entropy word test (`entropy_min_length` 24, `entropy_min_bits` 3.5) catches the rest; it also drops git hashes and GUIDs. A matching command is dropped whole: never stored, never sent to the model; only its count and the rule index are logged.
- Kept commands go to the journal as a new source kind `terminal`: one thread per folder (`threads.kind = terminal`, scope = the folder), one capture per folder and pass (JSON `{"commands": [...]}`, encrypted, 30-day TTL), written with the checkpoint in one transaction. The journal prompt shows each as `[n] COMMAND <time> in <folder> on branch <b>, pwsh, exit <code>`. It tells Haiku to summarise sequences ("The user ran the Yuki test suite in C:\src\yuki on branch main; it failed twice, then passed."), never to list commands one by one, to skip routine navigation, and never to record a secret.
- Terminal *time* comes from the foreground timeline (Warp in front), not from Warp's timestamps: most rows never get a `completed_ts` (193 of 264 on 2026-09-24).
- **Warp's saved windows and tabs (2026-09-29)**: for a Warp window measured opaque, `warp.read_session()` / `window_data()` read, read-only in the same way, only Warp's session tables - `windows` (position, size, active tab, full screen, vertical tabs open), `tabs`, `pane_nodes`, `pane_leaves` (the vertical-tab title Warp keeps) and `terminal_panes.cwd` - never `blocks`, never a command or its output. The live window is matched to its saved row by its rectangle (12 px, DPI scale tried); the live title marks the tab showing. Titles and folders holding a secret are left out (the same detector and entropy rule). The result goes to Now only, never to the store: "the LLM OS tab" resolved to tab 1 of the window at (1920,261) on 29 Sept's desktop. `[terminal] enabled = false`, a blocked Warp or pause turn it off. The saved state lags the live one by up to a few minutes.

## Store

- One SQLite database under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\memory\memory.db` (WAL mode). Content columns encrypted with a key protected by Windows DPAPI (`CryptProtectData`, current user). Metadata (timestamps, app, URL host) cleartext for filtering.
- Tables (shape borrowed from MaxMi): `threads(id, app, title, url, scope, kind, messages_seen_at, first_seen, last_seen)`, `captures(id, thread_id, at, trigger, kind, profile, delta_ciphertext, chars, hash)`, `messages(thread_id, capture_id, fingerprint, content_key, sender_ciphertext, is_me, time_label, at, text_ciphertext, first_seen, status new|history|reread, journaled)` unique on (thread_id, fingerprint) - fingerprints and content keys stored as keyed HMACs; past the 30-day TTL a message keeps only its fingerprint -, `me_names(name_key, app, name_ciphertext, first_seen, last_seen)`, `journal(id, at, thread_id, app, fact_ciphertext, importance)`, `journal_vec` (local embeddings). Search = vector similarity + metadata filters (time, app, URL host); keyword queries decrypt the journal rows inside the requested time window and match in memory (the journal is small — facts, not raw text). No plaintext full-text index. `portrait_facts(id, kind, subject, text_ciphertext, valid_from, valid_to, source_ids, confidence)`, `knowhow(id, app, task_kind, text_ciphertext, valid_from, valid_to, source_request)`, `open_loops(id, person, text_ciphertext, status, opened_at, resolved_at)`, `health(...)`, `timeline(..., fullscreen, meeting, mic_s)` and `source_checkpoints(name, value, updated_at)` (migration 5, additive). Migration 6 (additive) adds conversation memory: `conversation_turns`, `conversation_facts`, `session_summaries`, `conversation_batches` and the vector tables `conversation_turn_vec`, `session_summary_vec`, `conversation_fact_vec` (see "Conversation memory"). Migration 7 (additive) adds the `by_yuki` marks; migration 8 (additive) adds the coach's `todos`, `nudges`, `nudge_checkins` and `nudge_state` (see "Nudges and to-dos"); migration 9 (additive) adds `weekly_reviews` and `weekly_review_vec` (see "Weekly review"); migration 10 (additive) adds `calls` and `call_prefs` (see "Calls: both sides"); migration 11 adds `call_chunks`; migration 12 (additive) adds `timeline.source` and the partial unique index `timeline_power` for the PC's power spans (see "Timeline: PC off and asleep"). Migration 13 (additive) adds `questions` and `question_vec` (see "Questions for the user"). Migration 14 (additive) adds `journal.nature`, `journal.superseded_at` / `superseded_by`, `journal_batches.supersedes` and `portrait_facts.aspect` (see "Personality: what is true and what is a joke"). Migration 15 (additive) adds `portrait_audits` (see "Portrait audit: connect the dots"). Migration 16 (additive) adds `conversation_facts.nudge_id` (feedback on a card), `nudges.reply_ciphertext` (the user's reply to a card), `nudge_checkins.reason_ciphertext` (the coach's why, for every outcome) and `journal.milestone` (see "Nudges and to-dos"); a copy of the real database migrated 15 -> 16 in 15 ms. Migration 17 (additive) adds provenance, the AI-tool taint, portrait importance and vectors, standing requests, Yuki's persona and the memory diary (see "Fast memory, provenance, dreaming"). Migration 18 (additive) adds correction passes, name aliases, commitment owners and turn addressees (see "Corrections everywhere"). Migration 19 (additive) adds `call_digests`, the per-call timeline digests (see "Calls: both sides"). Migration 21 (additive) adds the app's short labels: `portrait_facts.summary_ciphertext`, `conversation_facts.title_ciphertext`, `todos.title_ciphertext`, `knowhow.title_ciphertext` and `knowhow.summary_ciphertext` (see "Short labels for the app"). Migration 22 (additive) adds `conversation_facts.value_ciphertext` and `keyterms_ciphertext` for the user's own resources (see "The user's own sites and accounts"). Migration 23 (additive) adds the threads of work (`work_threads`, `work_thread_events`, `work_thread_vec`, `journal.work_thread_id`), the links seen (`links_seen`, `link_vec`) and `call_digest_vec`; migration 24 (additive) adds `journal.attention` and `links_seen.attention` (see "Connecting the dots"). A copy of the real database migrated 22 -> 23 in 0.17 s.
- **Local embeddings** (no cloud): a small CPU embedding model (e.g. `fastembed` + bge-small ONNX) — measure speed/size.
- Facts are never hard-deleted; contradicted facts get `valid_to` set.

## Journal worker

- Every few minutes (or after N new captures), batch the new captures per thread and ask **Claude Haiku 4.5 on Bedrock** to extract atomic dated facts, each with an importance 1–10. The batch is a list of numbered **sources**: one per conversation message (NEW or HISTORY, sender or "the user", its own date and time) and one per page capture. Each fact cites its source and is stored with that source's time: a history message from three weeks ago yields a fact dated three weeks ago, never "now". History may yield durable facts (who someone is, what was agreed); the prompt forbids recording it as something happening now. A message without its own time is dated by the nearest earlier timed message of the same read. The system prompt names the user ("The user is Kian, who also appears as Mafex, mafex11, 'You' or a name marked '(you)'"), and facts about the user say "the user", never their name as a third party. The calendar given for relative dates covers every source day. Reuse MaxMi's extraction prompt (`Sources/MaxMiRelay/ExtractPrompt.swift`) and its untrusted-data fences (`===BEGIN/END_UNTRUSTED_DATA_<uuid>===`) adapted to Claude. Captured text is data, never instructions.
- Sources also include **terminal commands** from Warp (see "Terminal history"): one source per command, dated by its start. Facts summarise sequences and never carry secrets.
- **Content that shows its own date** (2026-09-25; the portrait said "a friend is currently on a break due to exams" from a video description posted in June 2025): a page capture is dated by when it was on screen, but a video, post, comment, article or email can be much older. Each `record_facts` item carries `content_date` ("YYYY-MM-DD", "YYYY-MM" or ""): for a fact that reports what such content says, the date the content shows ("Jun 12, 2025"; "1 year ago" counted back from the capture), and the fact is stored dated by it (`journal.content_date()`: a month alone is its 15th, noon; ignored unless before the day the source was seen). The sentence is worded as of that date ("In the description of 'YOU WERE THE ONE', posted around June 2025, the uploader said they were on a break for exams"); what the user did with the content (watched, read) keeps the source's time and is a fact of its own. Pages are stored as deltas, so a later capture lacks the page's head (title, author, account, date): a batch holding only later captures of a page/video/document thread gets the thread's first stored capture as TOP OF THIS PAGE (first 600 characters, context, never a source; `JournalWorker.page_top`). Messages and list rows keep their own dates as before. On a copy of the real database (19 YouTube threads re-journaled, $0.13): the break claim came out dated 2025-06-15, "The user watched ..." facts stayed on 2026-09-24; the first prompt wording also back-dated some "watched" facts (2 of 19 threads), fixed by the two-facts rule.
- Log every model call with tokens and cost (reuse `Settings.pricing`).
- **Who did it, and asking instead of guessing** (both paragraphs shared with conversation memory, `yuki/memory/questions.py`): an AI agent's work (Claude Code in a terminal, an assistant in a browser or app) is the agent's, judged from the content, never the user's; a capture taken while the timeline was `away` (no real input for over a minute, drift filtered by `user_idle_s`) is marked `NO USER INPUT`. Relationships, meanings and owners are recorded only as the sources state them; the unknown goes into the tool's `questions` (see "Questions for the user"). The request carries a WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS block: the user's answers, person facts and the questions waiting, only for names that occur in the batch.
- **Windows beside the one in front, and popups** (2026-09-29; the watcher now also reads windows on screen but not in front, trigger `visible:<why>`, and popups such as notification toasts, trigger `popup`): their sources are marked `NOT IN FRONT` (the user may not have looked at it) or `POPUP` (appeared by itself). The prompt: record what such a window shows as what was on screen ("A Slack message from Varun on screen at 14:02 said ...") and a popup as an event that appeared, never that the user read, watched, did or looked at anything, nor as a sign of their interests. Each fact keeps the attention of the source it cites (`journal.attention`, migration 24: NULL in front, `visible`, `popup`; `store.capture_attention(trigger)` reads the watcher's fixed trigger format).
- **Facts come from the right source** (2026-10-01; J1589 "The user told Yuki they had just received a job offer from Google" came from the "You:" line of a local `file://` "Yuki voice samples" demo page, and J3334 "The user received an offer letter from Corvana" merged two iCloud Drive rows into a "received" event; together they raised Q28 "Has the user accepted the offer letter?"). (1) **Whose words**, structural: each `record_facts` item carries `user_said` (`no` | `to_person_or_yuki` | `to_ai_tool`); a `to_person_or_yuki` fact (the user said, told, wrote to or asked someone or Yuki something) is kept only when the cited source's author is the user by its own metadata (`provenance.source_of`: a call utterance, a message marked the user's, a command the user ran, a BY YUKI capture's request); one citing a page, a list row or someone else's message is dropped (`not_users_words` in the `journal_call` log). `to_ai_tool` is the user's own prompt in another AI tool's chat (ChatGPT, T3 Code), still model-judged as under the AI-tool taint: those apps are captured as pages, and a first version that required a user-authored source for every "asked" lost the user's real requests in 3 of 8 sampled batches. (2) **What the page is**: every CAPTURE line names its page (`from page "<title>" at <url>`), a `file` URL scheme marked "a file on this PC, not a website" (`journal.page_label`). (3) **Sample, demo and test content** (a demo script, test transcript, example data, Yuki's own generated samples), judged by the model from title, address and content, joins the AI-tool taint (`other_ai` -> `journal.tainted`, see "AI-tool taint"): at most one fact that the page was open, anything it says tainted. (4) **File listings** (a folder, cloud drive, downloads, as LIST ITEMS or inside a page): one row, one file; the listed date is the file's date; never received, sent, signed or accepted. The WHO DID IT paragraph (`AGENT_WORK_GUIDANCE`) covered agents' words only and the taint covered AI-tool prompts only; both are reused, nothing duplicated. (5) **Questions on withdrawn evidence**: the vetter (`questions.vet_message`) is told when journal entries a question was raised from were re-journaled (`journal.superseded_at`, `questions.reread_items`, following re-journals of re-journals) and shown the new reading, which a drop may cite; before this, `--revet` kept Q28 after its evidence was gone. Repair: `python -m yuki.memory.rejournal --batch <ids>` re-reads whole batches by id, then the portrait refresh and `python -m yuki.memory.questions --revet`. *Measured* (copies of the real memory, 1 Oct, Haiku 4.5): batch 1087 -> one "local test page 'Yuki voice samples' open" fact plus retellings of the sample, all tainted; 1955 -> "iCloud Drive listed 'Offer Letter - Kian', a PDF, dated 2026-09-30 19:00" and the take-home PDF as two facts (importance 7 despite the 3-5 asked); 1034 ("Leda tags" sample: "finished the MCP server", "four hours on Discord") tainted; the portrait (Sonnet) no longer mentions an offer; `--revet` dropped Q28 and the Google-offer question (Q29 -> Q30) citing the new readings. 8 other page and list batches (Downloads, F:, iCloud backup, ChatGPT, T3 Code, a font mockup page) kept their content; T3 Code's agent plan, earlier written as "the user explained ...", is now the agent's. Known limits: Haiku still retells a sample's lines in 2 of 3 readings (tainted), and the "Yuki mix" mockup page read empty twice (the guard kept its earlier facts). Cost: re-journal ~$0.007 a batch; portrait refresh $0.56; revet $0.014.

## Portrait worker

- Nightly (first idle moment after a configurable hour) and weekly: read the day's/week's journal (by id checkpoint, so facts dated weeks back by their messages are still consumed) plus current portrait facts, told who the user is; the model returns ADD / UPDATE (supersede) / INVALIDATE / NOOP operations per fact (Mem0 pattern), each citing journal ids. Apply bi-temporally.
- The first run after a weekly review (normally the Sunday 22:00 weekly run, two hours after the review) also gets the review's behaviour-pattern candidates as REVIEW CANDIDATES, with the episodes they cite added to its EPISODES. They are proposals: the run decides on them under its own validation and confidence rules, and the review is then marked as given to that run (`weekly_reviews.candidates_run_id`). The review itself never writes portrait facts.
- Render a one-page portrait text (hard cap ~1500 tokens) cached for Yuki.
- Between runs, a **loop check** keeps open loops current: at most every 30 min, and only when new journal entries or portrait facts arrived, one forced Haiku call (`review_open_loops`) sees the open loops, the journal added since the last check that is newer than their evidence (at most 20,000 characters, oldest first; the rest waits for the next check) and the portrait facts that became valid after it (corrections and the user's own facts among them). A `done` stands only when it cites an entry or fact dated after that loop's last evidence; what only Yuki said (offers, suggestions, reminders) never settles a loop nor keeps it open. Closed loops are invalidated (their `open_loops` rows `resolved`), the portrait's Open loops section is rewritten from the records without a render, and the run is accounted as kind `loops`. The full runs are also told to invalidate a loop whenever an operation on another fact rests on evidence that settles it.
- The `render_portrait` flag (written after a standing rule is saved or dropped, by Yuki or by the conversation worker) re-renders the page from the current facts as a `render` run (one call, no journal) once the flag has been left alone for 45 s, so a burst of rule changes costs one render. `render`, `loops` and `audit` runs never count as the nightly run, and (fixed 2026-09-25) no longer move where a nightly run's episodes and time use start: counting the half-hourly loop check, a nightly run saw only the last 30 minutes of episodes.
- **Time-bound, ongoing or past.** Each current fact is shown to the operations and render calls with when its evidence was last seen. The operations prompt: a status ("currently", "on a break", "working at") is true as of its evidence; one from content dated long before it was recorded is written as past with its date; work and interests say whether they are ongoing or past. The render: in Work and Interests keep current things (evidence within about the last week) apart from earlier ones, with dates, since Yuki answers "what did I do today" from this page.
- **What the portrait is for, and the admission bar** (3 Oct 2026, report `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/50-build-appA2-portrait-bar.md`). The portrait is Yuki's one-page model of the person: work, interests, people and what is pending with each, routines, preferences, personality, open loops. A fact enters only when it is (a) durable - still true in weeks, not one evening's state, (b) about the person - identity, work, relationships, goals, habits, preferences, constraints, personality, and (c) useful for helping them. Never: transient readings and metrics (quotas, usage percentages, costs, burn rates, projections, session or token counts, model mixes), an AI tool's analysis, advice or dashboard text, tooling telemetry. At most one lasting line survives from such evidence ("The user runs several Claude accounts for coding across their Mac and PC"), never the numbers; a fact mixing lasting points with metrics is rewritten to its lasting points. Numbers that describe the person's own habits across days (behaviour, routines) are fine. One text, `portrait.ADMISSION_BAR`, model judgement with worked examples (an invented version of the quota evening that reached Me on 2 Oct as the main negative, three more negatives, four positives; no word lists), shared by:
  - the **operations prompt** and the **audit prompt** (every ADD/UPDATE must meet it; the audit never refreshes a fact with a tool's newer readings);
  - the **bar gate** (`PortraitWorker._bar_gate`, after `_validate`, before the commit): one forced call (same model, no thinking, `judge_bar` tool) over the model's own ADDs/UPDATEs of each operations call and each audit call (not corrections, not open loops), with the portrait's other current facts listed by summary (not judged) so a lasting line never repeats one; a version that fails is refused (`rejected: fails the admission bar (<category>)`) or written as the judge's lasting line (its `shown`/`summary` from the judge). A failed gate call lets the versions through and logs `portrait_bar_gate_error`; log `portrait_bar_gate` (counts and categories in the clear, verdicts encrypted). Cost: one small call per operations or audit call that writes new versions (~2-4 cents with the facts list);
  - **dreaming** (`dreaming.dream_system_prompt`): retires a fact that fails the bar, within its caps; when the fact still carries durable points no other fact says, its retire gives them in `text` and the fact goes on as that (an UPDATE: old version kept as history, citations, pin and review carried), so nothing durable is lost; a plain INVALIDATE only when nothing lasting is left;
  - the **bar pass** (`dreaming.BarPass`, by hand: `python -m yuki.memory.dreaming --bar [--db PATH] [--dry-run]`): every current fact but open loops and corrections is judged (Sonnet, effort low, `judge_bar`, about 60,000 characters of facts and their newest 3 cited entries per call, the other facts listed by summary so a lasting line never repeats one). A failing fact is INVALIDATEd (bi-temporal: `valid_to` set, kept as history) and its lasting line, if any, ADDed as a new fact with the same citations, importance and `reviewed_at`. Pinned facts are never retired, only reported, unless the pass runs with `--include-pinned --pinned-ids F12,F34` (the user's yes to those facts, appClean 4 Oct; `--include-pinned` alone is refused, and a dry run without it lists the failing pinned facts with their words): then each named failing pinned fact is retired like the others (other pinned facts stay, reported), its lasting line keeps the pin, its diary line says it was pinned, and `--undo-bar` brings it back pinned; a saved plan applies its pinned decisions only with `--include-pinned --pinned-ids` again (and only the named ones). At most half the facts per pass. Each retirement writes a diary line (pass `bar`, run kind `bar`); `--undo-bar RUN_ID` takes the whole pass back (`Store.restore_fact` on each retired fact, the lasting lines ended); a retirement whose lasting line changed since (newer version, merged, ended) is left as it is and reported as `diverged`. A dry run keeps its decisions in a file (encrypted with the memory key; `--save FILE`, default in the temp folder) and `--bar --apply FILE` writes exactly those with no new judgement (facts changed since are skipped), so what was reviewed is what is written. Categories: `transient_state`, `usage_metrics`, `ai_tool_output`, `tooling_telemetry`, `not_about_user`, `not_useful`. Measured on a copy of the real memory (3 Oct): 155 facts, 3 calls, $0.21-0.27 a dry run; 13-25 would retire (most rewritten to a lasting line), 1-2 pinned facts reported.
- **Where the quota fact came from** (report 50 §2): the journal recorded each reading of a coding tool's usage panel and each of its agent's reports ("measured", "projected", "advised") as a plain fact at importance 4-8, not marked as another AI tool's text (52 of 1,740 entries from that tool were); the portrait's operations then wrote them into a work fact. The journal prompt now says a coding agent's own reports, measurements, projections and advice are the AI's text (`other_ai`) even about the user's real accounts, and that a tool's own meters are telemetry at importance 1-2, one fact per screen. Narrowed after review (A2-3): only an agent's usage, quota, limit and spend figures, its forecasts of them and its advice on staying within them are the AI's text; everything else an agent reports about the user's own work (found, built, fixed, where a project stands) stays usable. Replayed on a copy (42 batches of 2 Oct): 8 entries tainted, all usage or cost figures, none a project report.
- **What a person fact is: the person bar** (4 Oct 2026, report `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/55-build-appPeople.md`). The Me page's summary of a person is their first person fact (its shown form), and those summaries had grown into logs: what the user said on one Discord call, who shared what with whom, a student portal's term, attendance and tuition figures. A person fact is now that person's **summary**: one per person, a few natural sentences saying, as far as the evidence states it in words, who they are to the user and how they know each other, what they do (study, work, role), where they are from or live, what they are like and into and what they do together, how the user usually talks with them (the apps), and what is going on with them now that matters to the user, with its time. Never: a moment from one call or chat, who shared, sent or showed what, records and figures about them (attendance or class counts, fees, tuition, marks, grades, scores, ranks, balances, ids), the dates and clock times of single events, raw screen data, an AI tool's output; such evidence may still give one lasting point (they study engineering), without the figures or the moment. One text, `portrait.PERSON_BAR` (model judgement, worked examples: an invented version of the user's complaint as the main negative with its rewrite, one evening's help as a second negative, two good summaries; no word lists). It is the end of `ADMISSION_BAR`, so the operations and audit prompts, the bar gate, dreaming and the bar pass all apply it; two bar categories were added: `person_moment`, `person_figures`. The operations prompt's `person` kind, the render's People section, the shown forms (`titles.PERSON_SHOWN_RULE`: a person fact's shown form is the same summary said to the user, a few sentences), the people list's label (never one moment or a figure) and the linking call's route `use` (what the chat is for across days, never one moment) say the same.
  - the **people pass** (`yuki/memory/people_summary.py`, by hand): every person with a person fact (the index's contacts, their facts in the card's order; a person fact no contact cites is a person of its own) goes to the portrait worker's model (Sonnet, effort low, about 40,000 characters per call) with all their person facts and the index's lines (the relation, the chats where the user talks with them; no journal, so nothing new can come in). Per person it rewrites the summary fact under the bar from those facts (every durable point kept, nothing added) or keeps it, and marks each other person fact merged (its points now in the summary) or failing. A rewrite is an UPDATE of the summary fact (old version kept as history; citations of the merged facts added; pin, review and rating carried; new `shown` and `summary` from the same call); merged and failing facts are INVALIDATEd. **Pinned facts are never changed, only reported** (`pinned_held`); `--include-pinned` rewrites pinned summaries too, only on the user's say-so (the pin and a user origin are kept). At most half the person facts retired for failing per pass (merges not counted). Diary lines (pass `people`), a `portrait_runs` row (kind `people`), one render after a real run, log `portrait_people` (counts and categories in the clear, decisions encrypted). Commands: `python -m yuki.memory.people_summary --dry-run [--save FILE] [--show] [--include-pinned]` (prints counts only unless `--show`; keeps its decisions encrypted in a plan file), `--apply FILE [--include-pinned]` (exactly those decisions, no model call; a fact changed since is skipped), `--undo RUN_ID` (each rewritten summary gets its old words back as a new version, each retired fact is restored; a rewrite changed since is reported as `diverged`), `--no-embed` (leave the vectors to the service). Measured on a copy (4 Oct): 16 people, 1 call, $0.07 a dry run; 9 summaries rewritten, 5 more held back because pinned (all five the user's own corrected facts), 2 kept; `--apply` $0.15 (the render); undo restored every summary word for word.
  - The index's `fact_ids` name the versions current at the last link run; `contacts.person_facts_of(..., store)` follows a superseded id to its current version (`contacts.current_version`), so a summary rewritten by the nightly run or the people pass shows on Me at once. After the cited facts it adds every other current person fact whose subject is the contact's name or an alias, so a person fact the nightly run added since the last link run joins its person (on the card and in the people pass) instead of becoming a second person of the same name. The pass writes each person's diary lines right after that person's commit, so a failure partway (the database locked) still leaves what was written undoable with `--undo RUN_ID`; a rewrite turned down (empty or over 1,200 characters) also keeps the facts merged into it.

## The personal brief (2026-10-03, revamp 09 P1-P4)

Yuki knew a lot about the user and showed almost none of it: each consumer got an accidental slice (the voice thinker no portrait, the talker a cut personality block, the card writers eight old work facts). Code: `yuki/memory/brief.py`, the render in `yuki/memory/portrait.py`, report `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/30c-build-D-personal.md`.

- **Written by the render itself**: `save_portrait` has a `brief` field (no call of its own; about +1,000 output and +3,000 input tokens a render). Fields: `with_him` (register, what annoys them, what lands; folds in the persona), `people` (at most 12 from the people index: name, relation, route with the handle, how the user writes to them, what is live with them), `own_bits` (their own jokes, each citing its joke fact: the only teasing material), `now` (at most 5 dated live items, each citing a goal, thread, loop, dated plan or personal event), `routine`, `careful` (private topics, labelled by the model). The render gets a BRIEF DATA block for it: the people index with routes, counts, tone and the user's last lines; the live items with ids; their bits; Yuki's voice.
- **Checked in code** (`validate_brief`): budgets measured (whole sentences); a person must be in the index, a bit must cite a joke fact, a live item an offered id with a date in the week's window; offered ids never stay in a line's text. A render with no brief keeps the last one; a brief older than 20 h gets the nightly render even when no fact changed; the persona pass or the people index changing asks for the night's one render. Stored in `portraits.sections_ciphertext` under `_brief` (`Portrait.brief`; no column).
- **Slices** (`MemoryClient.brief_for(consumer)`, `brief_slice`): `thinker` the whole brief in the agent's cached profile layer instead of the Personality section and the persona (typed and voice); `talker` with_him, names and relations, bits and careful, at most 2,100 characters, "tone and names, never news" (the voice talker, builder 3b); `cards` the whole brief as the card writers' ABOUT THE USER (portrait facts only before the first brief).
- **Personal-ness** (migration 31, `surfaced`): every reply (the agent's turn writer) and every card (the card store, from what the writer noted with `note_card_context`) records the ids of the brief lines and memory items in its context, never text. Nightly, `PersonalJudge` reads at most 20 of the last 36 h in one Haiku call: personal, correct, generic (verdict `personal` | `generic` | `wrong` | `plain`), kept on the rows. `python -m yuki.memory.brief --show | --surfaced | --judge`.
- **Content first**: the `activity` result starts with what changed (`MemoryClient.activity_header`: threads touched with where each stands and what is open, things finished, dates ahead a week).
- **Plans ahead** (presence channel `plans`, one a day): a plan of the user's (a dated plan said on a call or told to Yuki, a goal with a day, a personal event) between 45 min and 24 h ahead, no timed to-do of theirs near it and its own "want a reminder?" card not answered, gets one card offering a reminder (todo_add).

## Portrait audit: connect the dots

Nightly runs only fold new journal entries in; nothing ever looked at an old fact again. The real memory of 2026-09-25 held a course enrolment shown on a friend's student portal as the user's own, and a stale "on a break" claim from an old video description. Code: `PortraitWorker.audit`, `audit_scope`, `_audit_evidence`, `_audit_chunks`, `_audit_message`, `_apply_audit`, `AUDIT_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, `AUDIT_PORTRAIT_TOOL` (`yuki/memory/portrait.py`), `yuki/memory/audit.py` (command), `yuki/memory/store.py` (migration 15).

- **When.** After every successful run that read the journal (nightly, weekly, refresh, first) the scheduler audits; after the weekly and the first run, every fact. The `audit_portrait` flag file asks for one now ("all" in it: every fact). `python -m yuki.memory.audit [--all | --facts 87,98] [--dry-run] [--db]` writes the flag when the service runs for the database, else audits in-process (`--facts` always in-process and leaves the checkpoint alone).
- **What is looked at** (plumbing, cheap; checkpoint `source_checkpoints('portrait_audit')` = journal id at the start, fact id and time at the end, so the audit's own versions and questions never trigger the next one): facts new or changed since the last audit, facts whose subject new journal entries name or whose threads got new entries, facts whose evidence was re-journaled, facts a question raised, answered or closed since then bears on. Nothing to look at: no call.
- **What the model sees** (Sonnet 5, effort medium, one `audit_portrait` call per group): every current fact with its last evidence date, the ones UNDER REVIEW marked with why; EVIDENCE per fact under review - its cited entries (newest 14), the other entries of their threads (6 each), the 6 most similar entries (local embeddings, `Store.similar_journal`), recent entries naming it - each with the thread it came from (the conversation, page or window and who it is with); SOURCE EXCERPTS: the first 500 characters of each capture still stored behind a cited entry (`Store.batch_captures`; not calls or terminals; at most 14,000 characters) - where an excerpt and an entry disagree the excerpt is what the screen showed ("Welcome <name> <id>" behind the entry "The user is a student with ID <id>"); RECENT JOURNAL since the last audit (first audit: 2 days; importance 3+, 30,000 characters, newest kept; first call only); the questions answered and waiting. Facts whose evidence overlaps stay in one call (union by shared entries), packed to 45,000 characters of evidence per call.
- **What it decides.** Findings (`contradiction`, `misattribution` - whose account, course, portal, document, task or obligation -, `relationship`, `stale`, `agent_work`), each with its reasoning and a decision: resolve (UPDATE / INVALIDATE / ADD, the same schema and validation as the nightly run's, superseded versions kept with `valid_to` / `superseded_by`, the audit run their provenance), ask (a question through `questions.py`, citing entries and facts) or keep. Code rules on top: an operation states a relationship ("friend", "their sister") only with a cited entry's own words for it (`relationship.words`, quote-checked like rules and to-dos); what moves to another person is what the user did with or for them, never their private records (IDs, fees, grades). Sonnet sometimes sends an array as a JSON string with the rest of the object inside it; `_repair_tool_input` reads it back.
- **Stored**: `portrait_audits(id, run_id, at, finding, decision resolved|asked|kept|rejected, fact_ids, journal_ids, new_fact_ids, question_ids, reasoning_ciphertext)` (migration 15, additive), `Store.audit_findings()`; log `portrait_audit` (content-free counts, findings and operations encrypted), runs of kind `audit` in `portrait_runs`.
- **Measured 2026-09-25** on a copy of the real database (47 facts; after re-journaling the 19 YouTube threads): the first full audit took 4 calls, 115k in / 29-35k out tokens, **$0.51-0.58**, 5 min. Both full runs re-attributed the course facts to the friend whose portal it was (the enrolment and a tuition loop invalidated, a plan rewritten without the portal, the friend's own fact updated), the reasoning citing the portal's welcome line and the user's own degree; the break claim was rewritten as "a break around mid-2025 for exams, status since unclear" (stale). Also found: a game stream that was another person's screen, a Claude-model fact contradicting its own evidence, and two spellings of one contact merged on a guess (asked). An incremental audit (new chat evidence naming the friend): 2 facts, 2 calls, $0.18. A targeted re-examination (`--facts`): 1-2 calls, $0.07-0.15. Known limits: the model wrote "a friend" instead of the friend's name in 2 of 3 runs before the relationship-quote rule, and the rule only applies to operations it proposes (a fact it leaves alone keeps its older wording); findings vary between runs.

## Conversation memory

Memory of Yuki's own conversations with the user: rules the user sets mid-chat ("call me captain always"), preferences about how Yuki talks, what the user handed Yuki for later or Yuki promised, facts the user reveals, and continuity across restarts. Design basis: `docs/research/conversation-memory.md` (Mem0 diff operations, Graphiti bi-temporal invalidation, end-of-session summaries, a small always-attached block and just-in-time recall). Code: `yuki/memory/conversations.py` (worker, in `yuki-memory`), `yuki/memory/api.py` (Yuki side), `yuki/memory/store.py` (migration 6).

**Tables (migration 6, additive).**
- `conversation_turns(id, session_id, request_id, at, user_ciphertext, reply_ciphertext, actions_ciphertext, outcome, extracted, extract_attempts, extract_batch_id, summary_id, summary_attempts, created_at)`: one row per exchange. The id is allocated by the writer from a microsecond clock, so it is known before the write lands. Kept forever: this is the raw conversation log (no capture TTL).
- `conversation_facts(id, kind rule|preference|commitment|fact, subject_ciphertext, text_ciphertext, quote_ciphertext, status active|done|revoked|superseded, valid_from, valid_to, due_at, source_turn_ids, origin model|user, created_at, expired_at, superseded_by, end_note_ciphertext, batch_id)`: bi-temporal. Nothing is deleted: an UPDATE supersedes the old version, and a revoke or done ends it, keeping `valid_to` and the words or reason that ended it. `quote` holds the user's own words for rules and preferences. `fact` is reserved: durable facts go to the journal (below).
- `session_summaries(id, session_id, started_at, ended_at, turn_count, text_ciphertext, batch_id, created_at)`. The spec's start/end columns are named `started_at`/`ended_at` because `end` is an SQL keyword.
- `conversation_batches`: content-free accounting per model call (purpose extract|summary, tokens, cost, latency, ops applied/noop/rejected, journal facts, outcome).
- Vector tables `conversation_turn_vec`, `session_summary_vec`, `conversation_fact_vec`: local 384-d embeddings, encrypted like the rest.

**Writing a turn (Yuki's process).** `MemoryClient.log_turn(session_id, request_id, at, user_text, reply_text, actions, outcome)` encrypts the exchange and inserts it through a dedicated SQLite connection that waits at most 8 ms for the write lock. If another writer holds the lock longer, the row goes to a background writer: turns queue behind it in order and the id is returned at once. It then sets the named auto-reset event `Local\YukiMemoryTurns-<db digest>`, which wakes the worker across processes. It never raises; it returns 0 if the turn could not be stored. Measured 2026-09-24: 0.5 ms per turn (2.3 ms for the first, which opens the connection); with the database locked, 10.5 ms, then 0.3 ms for each queued turn.

**Extraction (worker, Haiku 4.5).**
- *When it runs.* When at least 3 turns are pending, or the oldest pending turn was queued 60 s ago. A new turn wakes the worker at once; otherwise it waits on a condition, with a timeout set to the next due time.
- *Batches.* Pending turns are batched per session, at most 12 turns and 16,000 characters each.
- *What the model sees.* The current active items (C<n>), items that ended in the last 14 days (never to be re-added), the last 12 journal facts from conversations (so none is repeated), up to 4 earlier exchanges of the session as context only, the new exchanges numbered [n], and a calendar for relative dates.
- *What it returns.* One forced, strict `record_conversation_memory` call. `operations` are ADD / UPDATE / INVALIDATE (`end_as` done|revoked) / NOOP, each citing exchanges. `journal_facts` are atomic third-person facts, each with an importance and an exchange.
- *Grammar size (hotfix, 5 Oct 2026).* Bedrock compiles a strict tool's schema into a grammar with a size limit. Release 2 added `for_yuki` as a fifth inline number list and every extraction failed with "400 - The compiled grammar is too large" (35 turns given up after 3 attempts). Measured on the real service: the schema before it had no room for even one more string field; any inline array costs more than a field, and descriptions cost nothing. The four exchange-number lists (`operations.exchanges`, `knowhow.exchanges`, `not_for_yuki`, `for_yuki`) are now one `$defs` entry (`exchange_numbers`) referenced by each: the same JSON and descriptions, and it compiles with room for one more field. `tests/test_hotgrammar_schema_budget.py` holds every strict tool in `conversations.py` to that measured budget (fields, inline arrays, references, enum values); grow it only after one real call on the new schema. `python -m yuki.memory.grammar_requeue` (dry run by default; `--apply` with an undo file; `--undo FILE`) gives the turns given up by that 400 back the attempts it took, from the worker's own logs; run it once the fixed build is installed.
- *Validation, in code.*
  - Rules and preferences need a quote that is a span of the cited exchange's user text, after normalising case, whitespace, quote marks and dashes. A paraphrase or an inferred rule is rejected.
  - Revoking a rule or preference needs the user's words too.
  - Only a commitment can be `done`.
  - The target must be active. Evidence older than the item is rejected. Each item gets one operation per call.
  - A commitment's quote is dropped when Yuki, not the user, said it.
  - `due` ("YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM]") becomes `due_at`.
- *Durable facts go to the journal,* dated by their exchange (app "Yuki", thread `yuki:conversation`, kind `yuki_chat`). The portrait worker consumes them like any other fact. There is no parallel fact store.
- *One transaction* writes the batch row, the item changes, the journal facts, the turns marked extracted and all vectors.
- *Failures.* A failed call bumps `extract_attempts`; after 3 the turn is given up. After a failure the worker waits 5 min.

**Session summaries.** A session's exchanges split where 30 minutes passed without one. A run has ended when a later run of the same session exists, when 30 minutes passed since its last exchange, or when a later exchange belongs to another session id. Each ended run gets a 2-4 sentence, past-tense, third-person summary (forced strict `save_session_summary`). The model sees the exchanges (the latest 24,000 characters) and, as context, the session's previous summary. The summary is embedded and stored. Two known limits: two Yuki front ends running at once with different session ids would split each other's runs, and the summary of the last session appears only once it has ended, so `resume_context` right after a restart shows its exchanges without a summary.

**What Yuki attaches (hard budgets in code).**
- `standing_context()`: the active rules and preferences, each in the user's own words with the date it was said and its id (`- [rule:12] ...`), and the open commitments with due and asked dates and their to-do id (`- [commit:5] ...`). Hard cap 1,200 characters (~300 tokens). Over the cap, the oldest items are dropped whole. An item is cut only after a whole sentence, and dropped if even its first sentence does not fit. It returns `""` when there is nothing. Yuki attaches it on every request as its own block, the last thing before the user's words (see "Yuki integration").
- `resume_context(within_hours=6)`: when the newest exchange is at most 6 h old, a header (the session span, the number of exchanges, how long ago it ended), that session's latest summary, and its last 6 exchanges, each with the user's words, Yuki's reply and one line of actions and outcome. Messages are cut after whole sentences, or shown as "a long message of N characters" when the first sentence alone is too long. Hard cap 4,000 characters (~1,000 tokens), dropping the oldest exchange first. It returns `None` otherwise.
- `remember_rule(text, source_turn=None, supersedes=None)`: stores the rule at once in the user's words (origin `user`). It supersedes an active rule or preference with identical words or embedding cosine of at least 0.85, or the active one whose id is given in `supersedes` (a restatement in other words scores far lower: 0.30 for "Call me captain in every reply, even short ones" against "Always address the user as babe", 2026-09-24). It writes the `render_portrait` flag.
- `revoke_rule(ref)`: takes the id the standing context shows (`rule:12`; `[rule:12]` or `12` too). The model picks the rule the user withdrew; no text is matched (2026-09-25, no-pattern audit F: the old "every word of the phrase is a substring" pick, then embedding cosine >= 0.45, is gone). It marks the rule revoked and returns its id, or 0 when `ref` is not an active rule's id. A revocation writes the `render_portrait` flag. The agent's `forget_rule` tool passes the id in its `text` field.
- `recall(...)` now also returns `kind: "chat"` hits, one exchange each (`The user said: "..." Yuki replied: "..." Yuki did: ...`), and `kind: "session"` hits (summaries, with `until`), each dated. Both use vector plus keyword search with RRF, as for facts and episodes, and are left out when `app` is given.

**Portrait.** The render gets a RELATIONSHIP DATA block: the active rules and preferences in the user's words, and up to 8,000 characters of the last 14 days of session summaries, newest kept. It writes a short Relationship section on how the user likes Yuki to talk, and on running themes that span more than one session. That block feeds this section only: every other section still comes from the facts. A new or ended rule, or a new summary, since the last render re-renders the portrait on the next run; a rule change also re-renders it within a minute (the `render_portrait` flag). With no rules the data has no rules heading and the section says nothing about rules.

**Feedback on cards** (2026-09-26). Kind `feedback` (migration 16, `conversation_facts.nudge_id`): what the user said back to one of the coach's cards, tied to it (`card` in the operation, N<id> of the CARD line under the exchange), or about the cards in general. The CARD line comes from `cards_for`: the card whose Reply was pressed at the exchange's start (`Store.nudge_replied_near`, the two measured times), else the newest card shown in the 30 minutes before an exchange that came with a card in brackets. Validation in code: every quote must be in the user's own words (`own_words`: the card or note Yuki put in brackets is not theirs); an exchange that answers a card (Reply pressed) changes only feedback - a rule or preference it proposes is stored as feedback on that card, and an operation on a rule, a plain preference or a commitment is rejected ("a reply to a card changes only feedback"); rules the user states there reach memory through the agent's `remember_rule`. The prompt: a complaint that Yuki went quiet revokes, in the complaint's own words, what cut the coach down and records that they want it back. "Then don't mention it" is a rule about what Yuki just said. An answer goes only onto a question that was asked (status `asked`), and onto the question of the card the exchange answers. Relative days said late at night use THE USER'S DAY (`journal.user_day_line`: when they sat down after their last long break), here and in call transcripts. Measured on the copy (Haiku): the 15:37 "no messages from you" re-extracted revoked the gag (preference 13) in 3 of 3 runs with the final prompt; the 11:07 "stfu" gave feedback on card 12 in 2 of 3 runs, and Haiku's attempts to revoke the em-dash rules with "stfu" were all rejected by the card-reply rule (Sonnet 5 got both right in 5 of 5 at $0.024 a call against $0.010; not switched).
- `python -m yuki.memory.conversations reextract --turns ID[,ID] [--db PATH] [--dry-run] [--log-dir DIR]`: runs the extraction again over exchanges already extracted (items only: journal facts, questions and answers are not written twice); the changes are the pipeline's own, validated as always.

**Pause.** The `paused` flag does not stop conversation memory. It stops screen capture. Turns the user types to Yuki are still stored and extracted, just as the journal worker finishes what was captured before a pause. Revisit this if the user wants pause to mean "remember nothing".

**Costs** (measured 2026-09-24, synthetic two-session history, list prices):

| Call | Tokens (in / out) | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Extraction, 5 exchanges | 3.7k / 0.3k | $0.0052 |
| Extraction, 3 exchanges | 3.6k / 0.2k | $0.0046 |
| Summary | 1.7-1.8k / 0.1-0.16k | $0.0022-0.0026 |
| Portrait render with Relationship (Sonnet) | 2.1k / 0.24k | $0.0066 |

Logs: `logs/memory/conversations-YYYYMMDD.jsonl`, with usage, cost, latency and stop reason in the clear and the request, response and operations encrypted. Status cost (`memory_cost_today_usd`) includes these calls. `yuki-memory --no-conversations` turns the worker off; turns are still stored.

## Nudges and to-dos

**The coach since 2026-09-26** (`docs/research/coach-and-answers-review.md`, and the owner's decision the same day: no praise; session-aware break nudges). This paragraph supersedes what follows where they differ.
- *No praise, no generic lines.* The coach writes `nudge` cards only: a well-timed, personal break nudge after a long session ("you've been deep in the Yuki voice code since 11, it's 1:40, go grab lunch"), and rarely a call-back when a work session has genuinely turned into something else. Old `praise` rows still count toward the budget. Since 2026-09-29 a plain observation (something keeps failing, a call's recap, a merge that leaves something open, the screen meeting memory, a follow-up, a thread left open), maybe ending in one offer, is not the coach's but an observation card (kind `notice`, "Observation cards" below); the coach's prompt says so, and still bans praise.
- *Two models.* Haiku 4.5 decides (`coach_decision`: `say` none | nudge, `is_break_suggestion`, `detail` - the one concrete thing -, `evidence` - the ids of the lines it rests on: `J<journal id>`, `NOW`, `BREAK`, `CALL` -, `mentions`); Sonnet 5 writes every card the user sees (`write_card`, and the welcome back, reminders and questions), checking the detail first and free to say none. Code checks both answers the way it checks to-do mentions: an id the request did not offer, or no detail, is `suppressed:ungrounded`; a call-back that is not on a drifted session, or cites no journal fact from that session, is `suppressed:no_content`. Bedrock refuses `strict` for Sonnet 5, so it is sent to Haiku only.
- *Sessions* (`activity_sessions`): the dominant app over a stretch with hops shorter than `session_excursion_min` (5) folded in, and a short interlude between two stretches of the same app folded back; what a session is, is the model's judgement from the journal and the portrait. Triggers: `session_change` (a long session gave way: a pause), `drift` (a new session after work held `callback_after_min`, 15, with journal facts: once per session), `moment` (a journal fact the journal's own model marked `milestone`: something finished, shipped, merged, fixed or sent), `calm` (the first calm moment after a long full-screen stretch), `periodic`. No look before `break_consider_min` (45) at the PC; a break nudge only once one is due - `break_after_min` (90) at the PC without a break of `break_min`, the session with its short hops folded in or sessions back to back (`break_due`; the [BREAK] line says whether one is due and from when, and the prompt defers to it) - once per session and `break_gap_min` (60) apart, checked before the model (a look that could only end in a break nudge is not made before then: it could only say none) and on its answer (`suppressed:break_too_soon`, `suppressed:break_again`, the latter also for a drift look). Until 2026-09-29 the answer's guard used `break_consider_min`: on the 28 Sept replay three break nudges came at 45-46 min at the desk; with the fix one came, at 13:47 after 1h31m. Cards `card_gap_min` (10) apart. The message gives SESSIONS, CALLS (`yuki.memory.callspans.coach_calls_text`), the [BREAK] line and the portrait's routine; the prompt says a routine is only what the portrait says.
- *Time at the PC from presence* (`observe`): the start of the current presence session over 36 h, never the lookback window's edge. A stretch under `break_min` of presence is `settling`: nothing is said (the 2026-09-26 18:14 "4 hours straight" came 2 minutes after 15.5 h away). The 36-hour content-free scan is cached (`wide_rows`: settled rows re-read every 5 min, the fresh tail every tick): `observe` 8 ms.
- *Doubtful stretches* (`doubtful_spans`): a page run of 30 min or more with one title and no journal fact from its app loses its page and title before the coach sees any block ("input was going elsewhere"). Measured 2026-09-25 16:43-18:30: 1h47m of input on an unchanged ElevenLabs settings page; `%APPDATA%\Deskflow\Deskflow.conf` shows this PC shares its keyboard and mouse with a second computer, so they were most likely driving that one.
- *Feedback, not gags* (`coach_facts`, `feedback_text`): what the user said back to a card - conversation facts of kind `feedback`, and older preferences whose exchange replied to a card (`Store.fact_card`) - is a COACH FEEDBACK block after RULES, each tied to the card it answered; a sharp reply to a call-back means fewer, surer call-backs, never silence. RECENT CARDS carry the user's reply in their words (`nudges.reply`, from the UI, or the exchange the reply became) and tell "shown while at the PC" from "faded while away or in full screen".
- *One card on return.* The welcome back (Sonnet) waits for a settled calm moment and carries whatever fell due meanwhile (DUE NOW); items it carried are marked reminded (`nudge_state reminded:<ref>:<due>`), so no reminder card follows it. Questions wait for `question_gap_min` of presence after the user sat down.
- *Reminders and call plans.* A to-do or Yuki's commitment is reminded at its time; the writer says none for a vague item (`suppressed:vague`, never again). A plan the user said on a call (source `call`, labelled "the user's own words on a call") is never scheduled on its own: one `confirm` card asks whether they want a reminder while its moment is at least `confirm_lead_min` (30) ahead; a yes reaches the agent, whose reason line says to add the to-do.
- *Meetings that survive mute* (`NudgeWorker._meeting_held`): within `meeting_hold_min` (30) of the latest meeting row, the meeting is held open while its app still has an active audio render session (Meet drops the microphone on mute: 9 separate mic spans on 2026-09-25 09:00-09:46, and a reminder in a 70-second hole).
- *Replay, 2026-09-26* (the 25-26 Sept moments, on copies of a copy of the real database, real models, $0.50 over two rounds): no call-backs at 10:31 and 11:07, no praise at 13:35, no break line in the middle of the settings page, a game break nudge late at night at the session change, and one welcome back on 26 Sept instead of five cards. Per call: a look $0.0088 (7.9k in), a look with the writer $0.031 (17.8k in), a welcome back $0.020, a reminder or plan question $0.0037. A day like 25 Sept: 23 looks (a stub run over the whole day), about 6 reaching the writer: about $0.40-0.45 a day, against $0.52 for the old all-Haiku coach.

The coach (`yuki/memory/nudges.py`, thread `yuki-memory-nudges`) looks at what the user is doing **right now**, set against what they did before, and writes short messages for the UI. There are four kinds: `praise` (encouragement or an acknowledgement), `nudge` (a call-back, a call-out or a break suggestion), `reminder` (an item that is due) and `resume` (picking up after the PC was off or asleep; see *resume* below). A fifth kind, `review`, is not the coach's: it is the teaser of a new weekly review (see "Weekly review"), delivered through the same table and event. Code decides only *when to look*: presence, gates, budget and rate, all plumbing. *What to say, if anything,* is Haiku's call, through a forced strict `coach_decision` call with `reason`, `say` (none, praise or nudge), `text` (null for none) and `mentions` (the to-do ids the text names).

**When it looks.** Every 15 s (`tick_s`) the worker reads the timeline. It uses the user's rows only: `by_yuki` stretches are left out.
- *transition*: the site or app in front changed (`timeline.group_of`, site grouping) and the new activity has held for `transition_hold_s` (90 s), so a flicker never counts. Coming back after a break of `break_min` (5 min) or more is also a transition. Transitions go first: a periodic look waits while a new activity is still being held.
- *follow_up*: after a `nudge`, the next `follow_ups` (2) transitions may speak inside the budget, but only to praise ("good, keep going"). A nudge there is suppressed (`suppressed:follow_up_nudge`), so the same drift is never called out twice.
- *periodic*: a backstop every `periodic_min` (15 min).
- *reminder*: a user to-do or a commitment with a due time. It fires at that time, or at the first allowed moment after, once per item and due time (`nudges.ref` + `due_at`). A date with no time is reminded at `all_day_at` (09:00) that day, and nothing is sent more than `reminder_late_max_h` (24 h) late. A small Haiku call (`write_reminder`) phrases it in the user's register; if that call fails, the item's own words are used.
- *resume* ("picking up", kind `resume`): the user is back after **time away from the PC** - the PC off or asleep (the power history above), the PC on with nobody at it (the timeline's `away` rows; `user_idle_s` filters mouse drift), or nothing recorded at all - of at least `resume_after_h` (3 h), or it is their **first presence of a new day** (days start at `resume_day_start`, 04:00) after at least `break_min` away. Code: `resume_gap` and `presence_sessions` (content-free, `Store.timeline_states`: nothing decrypted): over the last 36 h the user's presence is cut into sessions (presence less than `break_min` apart is one session), and a session is *settled* once it holds `break_min` of presence, so a glance, a mouse bump or drift never ends a gap; a gap runs from the end of one settled session to the start of the next, whatever filled it. The latest qualifying gap is the one offered (a short break after coming back does not hide it), its key is `resume:<start of the return>` and its `resume_within_min` window runs from that return. Fixed 2026-09-25: before, only off/asleep spans counted, so a night with the PC on gave no welcome back; on the real timeline of that night (blips of drift every 5-20 min from 05:07 to 08:43) the gap is 05:16-08:56 (3h40m, "PC on, nobody at it 3h39m"), found at 09:02. The `skipped:window` of 08:00 that morning (check-in 21) was the old code deciding the 2026-09-24 morning's PC-off span, never decided before, at its first tick out of quiet hours: not this morning's return. Cost: one scan of ~1,000 content-free rows (~10 ms) at most once a minute (`RESUME_RESCAN_S`). Then `NudgeWorker._resume`. It is sent once they have settled into something since coming back (`transition_hold_s`, the transition-hold idea), at a calm moment: the usual gates hold it (quiet hours, snooze, pause, lock, a meeting or full screen in front, Yuki acting, away), and so does any open call (`calls` row of `CALL_OPEN_STATUSES`: a Discord call too, not only a meeting row) and a reminder written in the same tick (one card at a time). It waits for such a moment for `resume_within_min` (60 min) after the user came back; later it is skipped (`skipped:window`). While one is due and waiting, no other check-in is made; once sent it counts toward the budget like a praise, so the next transition within `budget_min` is `skipped:budget`. **Once per gap**: `nudge_state('resume_gap')` = `resume:<end of the span>`, written when it was sent, said none, was suppressed, skipped, or failed 3 times (`skipped:errors`; a failed call is retried after `min_gap_s`); it survives a service restart (the boot case). A gap that does not qualify (a short break) is marked at once, so later ticks cost one indexed query. A service restart while the PC stayed on writes no span, so it never triggers one.
  - *Its own prompt* (`RESUME_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, forced strict `welcome_back`: `reason`, `say` resume | none, `text`, `mentions`). Outside the fence: the trigger line (how long they were away and what the PC did meanwhile: off or asleep spans, on with nobody at it, nothing recorded; when the user was last at it; when they came back; how long they have been on what NOW shows; "first time at the PC today" on a new day) and the RULES (`rules_text`). Fenced: NOW (the run since the return, its journal facts), BEFORE THE GAP (the user's last `resume_before_min`, 90 min, at the PC: time per site with titles, the sequence, the last thing in front, episodes, journal facts, never `by_yuki`), TO-DOS (open, with ids; an item reminded in the last hour is left out, it is already on screen), RECENT MESSAGES (the last four, without those reminders). The prompt: name what they were in the middle of, specifically; if NOW is something else, do not call them back; a to-do only when genuinely open and relevant (due today, overdue, or tied to the work), at most one, with its id in mentions; never invent an item, deadline, plan or progress; the off time plainly with the real numbers, and nothing guessed about what the user did meanwhile ("no nap"); friend texting, one or two sentences, their rules; no mention of Yuki's data, "the gap", reminders; no offers of help; none when there is nothing real to say. A mention not on the list is suppressed (`suppressed:unknown_mention`).
  - *Knobs* (`[nudges]`): `resume_enabled` (true), `resume_after_h` (3), `resume_day_start` ("04:00"), `resume_within_min` (60), `resume_before_min` (90).

**Never while**:
- memory is paused, or the session is locked;
- a meeting row is in front, or one ended less than 5 min ago;
- a full-screen row is in front;
- Yuki is acting on the desktop;
- the coach is snoozed (`snooze_nudges`);
- it is quiet hours (00:30-08:00);
- the user has not been present (active or watching) in the last `presence_min` (5 min).

Reminders wait through the same gates.

**Budget**: at most one praise, nudge or resume per `budget_min` (30 min). Reminders and follow-ups are exempt; a resume is not held back by the budget (after hours off there is nothing in it), but counts in it. A transition takes priority over a periodic look: praise written at a periodic look does not hold a transition back, but a nudge does. A check-in the budget blocks is recorded as `skipped:budget` and makes no call.

**Rate**: calls are at least `min_gap_s` (90 s) apart, with at most `max_calls_per_hour` (12).

All these knobs live in the `[nudges]` table of the privacy file. The defaults are in `privacy_default.toml`, and a user file without the table gets them.

**What the coach sees.** Two things sit outside the fence: the trigger, and how long the user has been at the PC without a break. Everything else is fenced as untrusted data with a per-request nonce:
- NOW: the activity in front (app, site, title, how long, active or watching), with the journal facts and the latest two captures since it began. Journal facts lag a few minutes; captures do not. For a transition, it also shows the run just before.
- BEFORE: the last `lookback_min` (2 h): time per site with titles, the run-by-run sequence, time since the last break, episodes and journal facts (never `by_yuki` ones).
- TO-DOS, with ids, due dates (today, tomorrow or overdue) and evidence.
- The portrait's `work`, `interest` and `behaviour` facts.
- After the fence, not inside it (inside, they read as data to ignore): the user's active rules and preferences, in their own words (nicknames, tone), read fresh for every call. Every message, reminders included, follows each of them.
- The last 24 h of nudges with the user's reactions, and a count for the last 7 days.

The prompt gives principles, not rules:
- Judge whether something is related from its content, never from the site name.
- When the user switches from work to something unrelated, call them back and name the work.
- Acknowledge a return to work.
- When a long stretch (about 45 min) ends at a natural break, praise it.
- Never interrupt a stretch that is still going. The one exception is a break suggestion after about 90 min without a break.
- After long drift, be firmer: give real numbers and name the to-do due soonest.
- Never invent numbers or obligations.
- Be blunt if needed, never insulting.
- Say less after dismissals, and most of the time say nothing.

A text that names an id not on the list is suppressed.

**Storage** (migration 8, additive):
- `todos(id, text_ciphertext, due_at, due_all_day, status open|done, created_at, done_at)`.
