- `nudges(id, at, kind, trigger, text_ciphertext, reason_ciphertext, inputs_ciphertext, ref, due_at, checkin_id, shown_at, reaction shown|dismissed|replied|snoozed|expired, reacted_at)`. `inputs` is an encrypted JSON summary of what the decision rested on: the activity and its length, the previous activity, the time since the break, and the mentions.
- `nudge_checkins`: content-free accounting of every check-in (trigger, outcome `none|praise|nudge|reminder|suppressed:*|skipped:budget|error`, tokens, cost, latency), written in one transaction with its nudge.
- `nudge_state`: the snooze.

A praise, nudge or resume not shown within `deliver_within_min` (15 min) expires; reminders never do. `nudges.kind` gains `resume` (`NUDGE_KINDS`; `ref` = `resume:<span end>`, `due_at` = when the user came back, `inputs` = the spans, off seconds, last presence, return, new day, NOW and mentions). The UI shows it like any nudge (card heading "Welcome back", light blue; `resume` in the overlay's history label), `nudge_status()["today"]` counts it, and `reaction_summary` lists it. After writing a nudge the worker sets the named auto-reset event `Local\YukiNudgeReady`, which it creates and holds open, so the UI wakes at once.

Logs go to `logs/memory/nudges-YYYYMMDD.jsonl`, with usage, cost and latency in the clear and the request and response encrypted. The service log gets one content-free `nudge_checkin` line per check-in. The cost counts toward `memory_cost_today_usd`.

**The to-do list.** `MemoryClient.todos` merges three sources:
- open loops from the portrait, as `loop:<open_loops.id>`; evidence is who asked, where and when;
- open commitments from conversation memory, as `commit:<id>`; evidence is the user's words;
- to-dos the user added with `add_todo`, as `todo:<id>`.

`complete_todo(ref)` takes an id only (`todo:3`, `commit:5`, `loop:12`, also in brackets as the lists show them): the model picks it from the `todo list` result or the standing context, and lists first when it lacks one. No text is matched (2026-09-25, no-pattern audit F). What completing does depends on the source:
- a to-do becomes done;
- a commitment ends as `done`;
- a loop becomes `done` and its portrait fact is invalidated. A user correction ("the user said this is done") is also stored, so the render follows it and later runs do not re-open the loop from old evidence, and the portrait is refreshed.

**Reactions feed back only through existing paths.** The coach reads them for its own next decisions. The portrait render's RELATIONSHIP input gets one line of counts (last 14 days, per kind and reaction) for its Relationship section, as an observation, never a rule.

**API** (`yuki/memory/api.py`, used by the UI and the agent):
- `todos(include_done=False)`
- `add_todo(text, due=None) -> "todo:N"`
- `complete_todo(ref) -> id | None`
- `pending_nudges()`: unshown nudges, oldest first, as `{id, at, kind, text, reason}`
- `ack_nudge(id, "shown"|"dismissed"|"replied"|"snoozed")`
- `snooze_nudges(minutes)`: 0 clears the snooze
- `nudge_status()`: `{quiet_until, last_nudge_at, today: {praise, nudge, reminder}}`

`yuki-memory --no-nudges` turns the coach off.

**Measured 2026-09-24** (temp databases, synthetic timelines, Haiku 4.5 list prices):

| Call | Tokens (in / out) | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| Coach check-in | 3.0-3.6k / 110-190 | $0.0035-0.0045 (mean $0.0037) |
| Reminder | 1.2k / 42 | $0.0014 |

There is no prompt caching: the prompt is shorter than Haiku 4.5's minimum cacheable prefix. At the PC, expect 5-8 calls an hour (a periodic look every 15 min, minus budget skips, plus transitions). That is about $0.20-0.30 for a 10-hour day. The hard ceiling of 12 calls an hour comes to about $0.45.

Scenarios:
- 50 min of coding, then a chat: praise, "Nice, 50m on the nudge worker. Enjoy your lunch."
- Reels after coding, with a to-do due tomorrow: a nudge naming the work and the form; 30 min later, "Boss, 32 min on reels - back to store.py. The application form is due tomorrow."
- A YouTube talk on LLM agents while building Yuki: none (twice), then "good, back to it" on return.
- In a meeting: no check-in.
- A commitment due at 15:00: "Boss, call Asha about the flat now." at 15:00, once.
- Two drifts 10 min apart: one nudge, plus a "Good, back on it." follow-up; the second drift is `skipped:budget`.
- 25 min in Warp: none.
- Warp, then an unrelated video held for 2 min: a call-back naming the Warp work.
- Back to Warp after 20 min of reels: "Good, back on it".
- 1h50 of unbroken focus: a break suggestion.

**Resume, tested 2026-09-25** (offline: 51 checks on temp databases with synthetic timelines and a stub model - overnight off -> one message, never twice, not again after a service restart; not settled yet -> waits; 1 h off the same day -> none; a new day after 1h50 off -> sent, says so; straight into full-screen VALORANT -> nothing, then sent at the first calm settled moment; a game for 70 min -> `skipped:window`; a Discord call open -> waits, sent when it ends; snoozed or quiet hours -> nothing; three `APIConnectionError`s -> retried after `min_gap_s`, then `skipped:errors`; an unknown mention -> suppressed and the gap marked; asleep 4 h -> sent; a gap with no power span -> nothing; a transition 6 min later -> `skipped:budget`; a reminder due at the same moment goes first, alone, and its item is left out of the resume's to-dos). Live, 9 Haiku 4.5 calls in three rounds while the prompt was tuned (temp databases; the owner's "call me boss" rule): 2.4-2.8k in / 130-160 out tokens, **$0.0031-0.0035 each**, 2.6-3.3 s (one cold call 20 s). Final round:
- Off 8h40m overnight, back into Gmail, the application form reminded a moment earlier: "You were in nudges.py fixing the resume trigger's budget handling last night, boss. What's in the inbox?"
- Same, back straight into nudges.py: "Welcome back, boss. You were in nudges.py fixing that resume trigger budget—let's pick it back up."
- Asleep 4h15m after reading a paper, back into Spotify: "You were deep in the Attention Is All You Need paper on arXiv, boss—section 3.2 on multi-head attention. Spotify's going now if you're taking a break."
- Earlier rounds repeated the just-reminded to-do ("You've got the application form reminder - that's due today"), said "before the gap", offered help ("let me know if you want to jump back in") and read a PC asleep as "before the nap"; the prompt and the input were changed for each (the reminded item is now left out of the input in code).

Known limits:
- Haiku's wording varies from run to run. It usually uses the nickname, but not always, and a call-back is sometimes less specific than "the memory tests in Warp". The resume message sometimes adds a small aside about NOW ("What's in the inbox?").
- Solved 2026-09-25 by the power history: the first stretch of the day used to follow hours with nothing recorded, which the model could read as a return from a break of unknown length; now the break says "PC off 9h05m".
- The resume window runs from the user's return, so a return during quiet hours (before 08:00) usually expires before quiet hours end.

## Questions for the user

Memory records what it sees; some of it only makes sense with something it does not know - who a person is to the user, what an unfamiliar name is, whose task or material something is. The real memory of 2026-09-24/25 guessed these wrong (a relative's chat name read as a friend; Fable, a Claude model, as a team workspace; study notes made for a friend read as the user's own study). Now the model that does the understanding asks instead. Code: `yuki/memory/questions.py` (shared prompt paragraphs, the item schema, WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS, the phrasing prompt), `yuki/memory/store.py` (migration 13), `yuki/memory/nudges.py` (asking), `yuki/memory/api.py` + the agent tool `answer_question` (answers), `yuki/ui/nudges.py` (the card).

- **Found where the understanding happens.** The journal's `record_facts`, conversation memory's `record_conversation_memory` and the portrait's `update_portrait` each return `questions`: `subject` (as shown), `kind` (person | meaning | ownership | fact), a neutral third-person `question` with no guess inside, brief `evidence`, `why` and an `importance` 1-10, citing their source (journal: the source, whose facts' journal ids become the evidence ids in the batch's transaction; conversation: the exchange; portrait: journal ids and current facts - contradictions and guesses it did not make). The fact itself keeps the neutral observation.
- **Dedupe** (plumbing): the subject's HMAC (casefolded) or a "subject: question" embedding cosine of at least `QUESTION_DUPLICATE_COSINE` (0.70; measured: rewordings 0.73-0.78, different people at most 0.64) is the same question - merged while open or asked (seen +1, the higher importance), reopened if it expired unasked, otherwise `known`. A subject the user already stated a fact about (a user-origin portrait fact) is `known`. The models are shown the answers and the waiting questions too (WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS / QUESTIONS WAITING).
- **Asked like praise.** The coach asks the most important open question (importance >= `question_min_importance`, 5; evidence at most `question_max_age_days`, 7 days old) at a calm moment: the usual gates (pause, lock, snooze, quiet hours, a meeting or full screen in front, Yuki acting, away), no call open, no card written in the last `min_gap_s`, at most one per `question_gap_min` (20 min) and `questions_per_day` (6, days from `resume_day_start`), within the call rate; after three failed calls in an hour it waits. A small Haiku call (`ask_question`, the user's RULES after the fence) phrases it like a friend texting, with the evidence in a few words and no guess or options inside, or says none when memory already knows it (`dropped`). The card is kind `question`, `ref` = `question:<id>`; the question is `asked` in the same transaction. A card that expired unshown puts the question back to `open`; a shown one is never asked again. A question check-in does not move the coach's periodic look.
- **Answers become memory.** Answer on the card (it opens the overlay) or reply in the overlay: the request carries `[Replying to your nudge (kind: question, id question:<n>) ...]` and the agent calls `answer_question` with the user's exact words (checked to be a span of the request; a paraphrase is refused), or `skipped` / `never`. The card's Skip and "Don't ask" call `skip_question` directly. An answer is a portrait correction of origin `user` (`Asked "Who is Sam to the user?" (about Sam; memory had seen: ...), the user answered in their own words: "he's my cousin"`), the question `answered`, and the `render_portrait` flag written: the render run first folds pending corrections (`PortraitWorker.fold_corrections`, one operations call over the current facts only, effort low), so the wrong fact is superseded by a user-origin version within about a minute. Conversation memory also records answers given in ordinary chat (`answers`, quote-checked) as a safety net.
- **Downstream prefers the user's words**: user-origin facts outrank inferences in the portrait (as before); the journal and conversation memory see all the user's answers and the memory that bears on what they read (below); the portrait may not state an answer to a waiting question, and drops a relationship, meaning or owner no journal entry states when it updates a fact. The portrait and the episodes describe an AI agent's work and time as the agent's, directed and reviewed by the user.
- **Chat follow-ups**: the agent's prompt allows one short follow-up about someone or something unknown that matters, only where it fits, never mid-task.
- **Why none were asked on the first day** (2026-09-25, the table empty from 07:06 to 10:18): the asking side was fine; nothing reached it. (1) The evidence that mattered (a friend's portal, a group chat, the calls) was journaled before the feature went live, and nothing looked at it again. (2) The 15 `record_facts` calls after 07:06 were small batches (Zen, Settings, Chrome, PostHog) with `questions: []`. (3) The one portrait operations call since (10:07) left `questions` out entirely: it was optional in a schema Bedrock cannot make strict for Sonnet 5. (4) A call's own side could never ask. Fixes: `questions` is required in `update_portrait`; the portrait audit re-examines old facts and asks (above); a call's own side asks about names the user says (`CALL_QUESTION_GUIDANCE`: "Who is <name> to the user?", only a name clear enough to write in a fact, never about what the others said; `record_call_side` gains `questions`, stored in `commit_call_batch`'s transaction, and gets WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS). On the copy after the audit: "Who is <name> to the user?", "What is <handle>'s relationship to <name>?", "Is <A> the same person as <B>?", all importance 5; the coach's `ask_question` phrased them ("Who's <name> to you? You helped them with something on their portal today."), $0.003 each. A nickname in one garbled call utterance was read as "someone the user calls <nickname>" and not asked.
- **Storage** (migration 13, additive): `questions(id, at, created_at, kind, subject_ciphertext, subject_key, question_ciphertext, evidence_ciphertext, reason_ciphertext, importance, origin journal|conversation|portrait, source_ids, fact_ids, status open|asked|answered|skipped|never|expired|dropped, seen, nudge_id, asked_at, answered_at, answer_ciphertext, answer_fact_id, updated_at)`, `question_vec`. Knobs: `[nudges]` `questions_enabled`, `question_gap_min`, `questions_per_day`, `question_min_importance`, `question_max_age_days`.
- **Measured 2026-09-25** (temp databases; list prices): offline 61 checks on stub models. Live on synthetic inputs: a family chat, the Claude usage page with "Fable" and study notes "to explain to a friend" each gave a neutral fact and a question (Fable asked in the last 3 of 5 runs); a Claude Code session streaming into a terminal with no input was written "Claude Code worked on ..."; Sonnet dropped a seeded "Sam is a friend", invalidated "the user is studying for an exam" and rewrote a "Fable workspace" fact as a model. Costs: journal batch $0.005-0.006 (about +900 input tokens a call for the guidance and schema, ~+$0.16 a day at the 181 calls of 2026-09-24), card phrasing $0.0022-0.0025, answer fold + render $0.022, portrait refresh $0.041-0.049. Footprint: no new thread or process; the question check costs 0.01 ms a tick with nothing open, 0.15 ms with 40 open; WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS takes 2 ms to build; migration 12 -> 13 on a copy of the real database 15 ms.

- **Since 2026-09-26**: questions are paced by the user's presence, not the clock (one per `question_gap_min` minutes at the PC, counted from the last question or from when they sat down: never on top of the welcome back); the writer (Sonnet 5) sees THE USER'S WORK AND INTERESTS from the portrait ("What is Corvana?" was asked of its founding engineer); an answer is saved only onto the question that was asked.

- **Connecting the dots before asking (2026-10-01).** The journal asked "Has the user accepted the offer letter from Corvana?" (Q28, importance 8; an offer letter in iCloud Drive) of the user who already works there as founding AI engineer (portrait fact 347, their own answer to Q7, ~20 Corvana facts). Three causes: WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS was picked by a substring match of fact subjects in the batch text; it never held model-origin work, interest or routine facts; and nothing ran a question past memory before storing it. The decrypted original request showed the user's own answer *was* in the block and Haiku still asked, so better context alone was not enough. Now:
  - **WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS** (`known_text(..., about=, embedder=)`, every finder: journal pages and calls, conversation memory, the threads backfill) is a **core profile**, always given (`core_items`: the user's work facts, most important first; *all* their own answers, the closest to the text first - by ranking alone "corvana is where i work" fell below the cap; the key people; corrected names), then **what else memory holds that bears on it**: the whole memory ranked by meaning against the text the model reads (`related_hits`, up to four 1,000-character queries spread over it, on the shared `RelevantIndex` - `relevant.shared_index`, one copy of the vectors with the screen recall; cosine + importance + recency, no model call; per-kind floors and most: portrait 0.45 / 6, resources 0.45 / 2, journal 0.55 / 4, the batch's own window's facts left out), then the waiting questions, the closest first. No word matching anywhere; `KNOWN_CHARS` (3,000) still caps it (core 60%, waiting 15%). The portrait's own calls (`with_facts=False`, no `about`) are unchanged.
  - **Vetting** (`vet_new`, before `commit_batch` / `commit_call_batch` / `commit_conversation_batch` / `add_questions`, for every producer: journal, calls, conversation memory, the portrait and its audit, the threads tidy): each candidate is ranked against memory ("subject: question evidence", the shared index) and one Haiku call per `VET_BATCH` (5) questions (`vet_questions`, strict) decides each: `drop` (memory answers it or makes it pointless - the friend test: would someone who knows everything in this memory find it odd to ask?), `rephrase` (asked wrongly but something real is still unknown: the better question, open, no guess, no candidate answers) or `keep`. Plumbing on ids only (`parse_vetting`): a drop must cite a shown item that is not the question's own evidence, a rephrase must carry the new question, anything malformed keeps the question; an error keeps them all (fail open). Logged `question_vet` (where, decisions with the memory ids they rest on - `F` portrait fact, `J` journal, `R` resource, `A` answer, `N` alias - kind, importance, cost; no content).
  - **Re-checking the waiting ones**: `revet` (open and asked): a dropped question becomes `dropped` with the portrait fact that answers it in `answer_fact_id` (`Store.drop_question`; a dropped subject is `known` to the dedupe, so it is not asked again), a rephrased one is superseded (`Store.supersede_question`: status, card and evidence kept). It runs after a successful portrait run, at most every `REVET_EVERY_S` (6 h; logged `question_revet`), and by hand: `python -m yuki.memory.questions --revet [--dry-run] [--db PATH]`.
  - **Measured on a copy of the real memory (2026-10-01)**: the exact batch-1955 request re-sent as it was asked the Q28 question in 1 of 5 samples; with the new block, 0 of 5. Q28 vetted alone: dropped 3 of 3 (citing A7, the user's answer, and F347); in a batch of ten it was kept (the shared core got trimmed and the judgement diluted), hence all answers in the vet's core and batches of five. `--revet` over the 10 waiting questions: 4 dropped as already known (Q28 the offer letter; Q25 "What is T3 Code?" - F363 describes it; Q24 the M-Audio AIR 192 - F460, the user's audio interface; Q26 T3 Connect), 6 kept (Q19, Q20, Q27 and the three threads-tidy questions). Costs: the journal's known block grew from a median 0-229 to ~3,000 characters (+~1,000 input tokens, +$0.001 a batch, ~+$0.2 a day at 214 batches); a vetting call $0.004-0.008 (1-5 batches a day carry questions); a re-check of ten $0.016 (at most four a day); embedding the block's queries ~0.25 s warm per batch on the service's CPU.

## Personality: what is true and what is a joke

The owner (2026-09-25): a long Discord call showed who they are better than any screen - how they talk, what they laugh at, the stories they tell - and Yuki should use it: joke with them, tease them, bring things up, ask about them. But memory must know what is true: the call's first journal stated jokes as facts ("needs a pilot who earns millions so they don't have to work"), banter or mishearing as fact ("unlimited Claude Opus accounts") and a misread number ("known them for 2 years"). Jokes are kept, labelled for what they are. Code: `yuki/memory/personality.py` (natures, the shared journal paragraph `NATURE_GUIDANCE`, `split_personality`), `journal.py`, `conversations.py`, `portrait.py`, `nudges.py`, `questions.py`, `rejournal.py`; readers in `review.py`, `episodes.py`, `api.py`, `agent/memory.py`, `mcp/server.py`; use in `agent/prompt.py`, `voice/mouth.py`.

- **Every journal item has a nature** (migration 14, `journal.nature`; NULL = a fact written before): `fact` (plainly told or shown as true), `trait` (how they think and behave, their humour, values, tastes, habits, how they talk - drawn from how they talk and what they say about themselves), `story` (an anecdote they told), `humor` (a joke, bit, tease, boast or banter: kept as humour, never a fact) and `uncertain` (possibly misheard or ambiguous: never a fact anywhere). Commitments keep their own to-do path. The journal (`record_facts`, `record_call_side`) and conversation memory (`journal_facts`) return it per item (strict enum). The prompt: the nature is a label, not a filter; humour and uncertain items carry it in the sentence ("joked that", "said what sounded like"); a claim that would be surprising if true is a fact only when plainly serious and clearly heard. The call guidance (`CALL_TRANSCRIPT_GUIDANCE`, "IT IS TALK AMONG FRIENDS") now keeps jokes as humour instead of leaving them out, records how they talk as traits, and says a number must be in the words of the cited utterance, a short answer ("2 years I guess") answers a question nobody can hear, and the language an utterance was "transcribed as" says nothing about the languages the user speaks.
- **Readers label or filter by nature** (`nature_mark`, `usable`): the coach (NOW, BEFORE, the resume message), episodes and the weekly review leave `uncertain` items out and mark jokes "[joke/banter - humour, not a fact]"; `recall` hits carry `nature` and the agent's recall lines and MCP `search_memory` mark it (its instructions say a joke or an uncertain item is not a fact); the loop check never closes a loop on a joke or an uncertain item.
- **Portrait: personality facts and a Personality section.** A new fact kind `personality` with an `aspect` (`portrait_facts.aspect`): `talk`, `cares`, `joke` (a running joke, always written as one), `story`, `people`; each ends with where and when it showed. Validation in code: an ADD/UPDATE may rest on `humor` entries only if it is a personality fact, never on `uncertain` ones; either may still support an INVALIDATE; a personality fact needs its aspect. A fact whose journal evidence was all re-journaled is marked "[rests on re-journaled entries ...]" and may be invalidated citing nothing. The render (`save_portrait`) writes the Personality section in its own field (at most 250 words, 2,000 characters): how they talk, what they care about, running jokes and bits, stories they tell, how they are with different people, each with its date; code places it under `PERSONALITY_HEADING`, before the Open loops, so the voice mouth can take it whole (`split_personality`; the mouth's 3,000-character cut of the rest no longer loses it).
- **Using it.** The agent's prompt, the mouth's prompt and the coach's prompt (which now gets the personality facts in ABOUT THE USER) say, as behaviour: joke back in their style, bring up a story or something they care about when it fits, ask about them now and then; tease only with what the user joked about themselves; never about health, body, family or money troubles unless they bring it up in that moment; their jokes are never facts or reasons to act; a task always comes first; most replies carry no joke. Questions gain kind `personality` (the story behind a bit, what got them into something, whether an uncertain item was real or a joke), found by the portrait (at most one a call) and phrased warmly by the ask call.
- **Re-journal** (`python -m yuki.memory.rejournal --thread-kind my_call_side --since ... [--until] [--batch ID,ID] [--db] [--dry-run] [--portrait auto|now|flag|skip]`): the captures still stored (30-day TTL) are journaled again by the same pipeline (`JournalWorker.rejournal`), whole batches only; in the new batch's transaction the earlier batches' facts are superseded (`journal.superseded_at`, `superseded_by` = the new batch, `journal_batches.supersedes`), kept as history and left out by every reader; the facts being replaced are not shown to the model as already recorded; a call's commitments stay as they are. A reading with no facts where there were some writes nothing, is read once more, and otherwise the earlier facts are kept (Haiku answered one noisy 13k-character call with empty lists in 2 of 6 readings). Then the portrait refresh: the `refresh_portrait` flag when the service runs for that database, else a run in-process.
- **Measured 2026-09-25** on a copy of the real database (the 5 call threads of 2026-09-24/25, 7 captures, 43 facts before): 94 facts after (plus 11 for the last call read alone), e.g. "[humor] The user joked that the coffee machine is their real manager", "[story] The user once missed a train by reading the wrong platform", "[trait] The user switches languages mid-sentence"; a hedged length of time now reads as hedged ("roughly 2-3 years ago", where the transcript wavered between the two); a boast came out `uncertain` for its garbled part and `fact` for its clear part. Re-journal $0.22 (Haiku, 18 calls), portrait refresh $0.16-0.23 (Sonnet). Known misses: Haiku's natures vary between readings (the humour line was `uncertain` in 1 of 5), traits repeat across a long call's parts, and "Tamil" still appeared once from the recogniser's language labels.

## Admission: memory that cleans itself (report 76 item 5, 5 Oct 2026)

Why: 44 corrections in 7 days, 21 of them "just wrong, forget it", the call-related ones all from jokes, teasing or misheard lines taken as facts, and personal sensitive facts stored until the user removed them. Code: `yuki/memory/admission.py`; `journal.py` (the `sensitive` mark, the UNCLEAR rule, the judge call), `store.py` (`NewFact.held`, `_hold_journal`), `presence.py` (the keep card), `api.py` (`recap_keep`, `held_facts`, `resolve_held`), `ui/nudges.py` (the card's rows), `ui/web_data.py` / `web_window.py` / `web/pages/me.js` ("Waiting for your yes"), `portrait.py` (one line in `ADMISSION_BAR`, the confirmed mark), `calldigest.py` (one paragraph). Build report: `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/93-build-memClean.md`.

- **Held facts** are written with their vector and provenance and superseded at once by a `hold:<reason>` batch in the same transaction (no migration), so no journal reader sees them: `call` (offered on the call's keep card), `sensitive` (needs the user's explicit yes; never on a corner card), `drop` (judged not meant; never offered). Unanswered, a held fact stops being offered `HOLD_DAYS` (7) after it was held (its hold batch's time, not the fact's own date: an old statement read today is offered); the rows stay, superseded, like any retired fact. A re-journal re-points the unanswered held facts of the batches it replaces, so they are not offered twice; an answered one stays put, and its answer is carried to exactly one new fact: the one it meets on the same call line (call, line time, whose line), paired one to one by the stored embeddings, a tie going to a fact held or admitted alike, then to the same place on the line (`Store._match_line_facts`), so a plan admitted beside a claim answered Not true stays in memory. A card or window row written before a re-journal answers the held fact that took its place (`admission.moved_held`); when the new reading is not held the answer comes back `moved` and the card points to the Me page.
- **Structure** (metadata only): a fact citing someone else's line is never admitted on its own, whatever its label (the judge's drop keeps it out, else it waits); a fact citing a line the recogniser marked UNCLEAR is `uncertain`, and an `uncertain` fact is held, not stored, unless it carries the journal's misheard guess (the "Maybe misheard" list asks about those). A work op or a question resting only on held facts is left out (`unheld_links`).
- **Sensitive** (model judgement, `SENSITIVE_GUIDANCE`; on a call the mark holds the fact, never the user's own to-do: `SENSITIVE_COMMITMENTS_GUIDANCE`, in both journal tools as a strict `sensitive` enum; screen facts also give `sensitive_shown`, the second-person line the window shows): sexuality, health, the user's own money accounts, where their passwords or seed phrases are, legal trouble. The portrait's admission bar takes such a fact only from what the user told Yuki, or from a journal row marked `confirmed_by_user` (a Keep). The call digest keeps the user's sensitive matters without the detail, and the recap card's writer never puts them on a card.
- **A call's facts** get one more forced Haiku call (`admit_call_facts`): `admit` (the user's own plan or current doing, plainly in their own line), `ask` (waits for a Keep), `drop`; ties go to `ask`. No network: the batch is read again (no attempt spent); any other judge failure fails the batch while attempts remain; only the last attempt stores unjudged (every claim waits; logged as `admission.held_unjudged`). Its cost and usage count toward the batch.
- **The keep card**: the presence `recap` channel, when the call has waiting lines with the judge's second-person wording - "From your call with Tara: 3 things I'd keep", each with Keep / Not true / It's a joke (`inputs.keep`; no "It's a joke" on someone else's line, and the answer is taken as Not true there). No model writes it; one per call; every coach gate holds it (games, calls, quiet hours). Keep and It's a joke write a new journal row (the portrait reads it as new); Undo takes it back.
- **Waiting for your yes** (the window's Me page, `web_data.held_view`, action `resolve_held`): every unanswered call and sensitive hold of the last 7 days, said to the user, with Keep / Not true / It's a joke and a 5 s Undo. The private surface for a sensitive fact's explicit yes, and the fallback for call lines the card never showed (beyond the top 3, short calls, games, late digests, unjudged batches).
- **Measure** (counts only, on a copy): `python -m yuki.memory.admission --measure --db COPY [--days 7] [--good 40]`.

## Weekly review

Once a week, and when the user asks, memory looks back over the last seven days: what the week was about, how the time went against the week before, focus and drift, what got done, what is still open, and one or two suggestions for next week. Code: `yuki/memory/review.py` (thread `yuki-memory-review`), `yuki/memory/store.py` (migration 9), `yuki/memory/api.py`.

**The numbers, in code** (`week_numbers`). Code computes every number. The model only reads them.
- *The period.* Seven review days, each from 04:00 to 04:00 local time, so a late night counts with the evening it belongs to. The last day runs until now. The seven days before it are the comparison.
- *Per day:* time at the PC (active = with input; watching = no input while the app in front played media), away time, meetings (hours only), full-screen time (games, full-screen videos), switches per active hour, the longest uninterrupted stretch, when the day at the PC started and ended, and the top five sites or apps.
- *For the period:* time per site or app with its split by day (top 12), the six longest stretches (meetings left out), back-and-forth pairs, meetings as spans with microphone time, and full-screen time by app or site and day.
- *Around the time use:* to-dos opened, completed and overdue (user to-dos, commitments and open loops, with ids), open-loop expiries, the open list now, nudges by kind with the user's reactions, and conversation sessions with Yuki (a session's exchanges split at 30 idle minutes).
- *Left out:* Yuki's own stretches (`by_yuki`) are not the user's time. Only their total is given, as a note.
- *The change* against the previous week (`compare`): hours at the PC, active, watching, meetings, full screen, switches per active hour, days at the PC, average start and end, and every top site or app that moved by 10 minutes or more. It is computed and given to the model as text, so the model never does the arithmetic.

**The narrative** (Claude Sonnet 5 on Bedrock, adaptive thinking, effort medium; one `save_weekly_review` call).
- *Input,* fenced as untrusted data with a per-request nonce: the numbers; the week's episodes (at most 60 and 24,000 characters, newest kept); the journal's most important facts (highest importance first, up to 8,000 characters, shown oldest first; no `by_yuki` facts); the portrait's work, behaviour, interest, person, routine and preference facts with their ids; and the user's standing rules in their own words.
- *Output:* the parts `about`, `time`, `focus`, `done`, `still_open`, `suggestion_1`, `suggestion_2` (may be empty), a `teaser` for the card, `mentions` (the to-do ids the text names) and `behaviour_candidates`.
- *The prompt's rules:* evidence only, and every number as given; never a character label; never a guess at a meeting's people or topic, or at what was played in full screen; pending items only from TO-DOS; the user's register and rules (nickname, tone); about 250 words.
- *Strict.* `strict` is sent. Bedrock refused it for Sonnet 5 again on 2026-09-24 (`tools.0.custom.strict: Extra inputs are not permitted`), so it is dropped for the rest of the process after the first 400 (about 4 s, no cost). Every field is then checked in code.
- *Suggestions* are two string fields, not an array. Without strict, Sonnet sent the array as one string in 2 of 2 runs.

**Validation, in code.**
- Every part must be a non-empty string, with at least one suggestion.
- `mentions` must name only ids on the to-do list. A wrong type or an unknown id gets one corrective round (a `tool_result` with `is_error`). If the second answer is still wrong, the run fails and is retried 30 minutes later.
- A candidate must cite at least one episode that was in the request. Its `fact_id` must be null or a current behaviour fact. There are at most four.
- A candidate whose cited episodes all fall on one day has its confidence capped at 0.3, the portrait's own rule. The model's figure is kept as `confidence_asked`.

**Storage** (migration 9, additive).
- `weekly_reviews(id, at, finished_at, week, period_start, period_end, trigger scheduled|demand, model, text, sections, teaser, numbers, candidates, candidates_run_id, nudge_id, calls, tokens, cost_usd, latency_ms, stop_reason, outcome running|ok|empty|error, error)`.
- The text, sections, teaser, numbers and candidates are encrypted. The accounting is in the clear.
- `week` is the ISO week of the period's last day (`2026-W38`).
- `weekly_review_vec` holds the review's local embedding, for recall.
- A week with under 30 minutes at the PC is stored as `empty`, and no model is called.
- The cost counts toward `memory_cost_today_usd`.

**When it runs** (`ReviewScheduler`).
- *The slot:* by default Sunday 20:00, from `[review]` in the privacy file (`weekday`, `at`). The review runs at the first moment after the slot that the user has been idle for `idle_min` (5 min).
- *Missed slots:* a slot missed while the service was down runs at its next start, but only within `catch_up_h` (36 h) of the slot.
- *On demand:* `MemoryClient.run_weekly_review()` writes the `run_weekly_review` flag file. The service wakes, deletes the flag and runs at once, even while memory is paused.
- *Pause:* while memory is paused, no scheduled run starts.
- *Retries:* a failed run is retried after `retry_min` (30 min).
- *Order:* the review runs before the 22:00 weekly portrait run, which takes up its candidates.

**Delivery.** The teaser becomes a nudge of kind `review`. It carries `ref` = `review:<id>` and a reason that says where the full text is. It is written through `Store.record_checkin` with a model-less check-in (trigger `review`, left out of the coach's rate, look and budget counts), and then `Local\YukiNudgeReady` is set.
- *When:* the user must be present (input within `present_s`, 120 s) and the session unlocked, within `deliver_within_h` (72 h). So a review written while the user was idle waits for them to come back.
- *Extra gates for a scheduled review:* memory's pause, the coach's quiet hours and snooze, and `[nudges] enabled`. A review the user asked for skips these.
- *Expiry:* `pending_nudges()` never expires a review, as with reminders.
- *In the UI:* the card's Reply opens the overlay as for any nudge, with the teaser and reason as context.

**API and surfaces.**
- `MemoryClient.weekly_review(which="latest")`: `which` is `"latest"`, an ISO week (`"2026-W38"`) or a date in that week. It returns `{id, week, trigger, start, end, written_at, text, sections, teaser, summary (a few computed lines of key numbers), numbers, candidates, candidates_given_to_run, nudge_id, model, cost_usd}`, or `None`.
- `MemoryClient.run_weekly_review()`.
- `recall` returns `kind: "review"` hits, dated by the review period. They are left out when `app` is given.
- The tray item "This week's review" opens a read-only glass panel in the style of Today's list. It shows the parts under headings, the suggestions, and "By the numbers".
- The MCP tool `get_weekly_review(week?)`.
- `yuki-memory --no-review` turns it off.

**Logs:** `logs/memory/review-YYYYMMDD.jsonl` records usage, cost, latency, whether strict was used, and the stop reason in the clear, and the request and response encrypted. The service log gets content-free `review_run`, `review_delivered` and `review_missed` lines.

**Measured 2026-09-24** (temp database, synthetic two weeks, Sonnet 5 list prices). The synthetic week had a heavy-focus Monday (1h50 unbroken in VS Code, a meeting), a drift-heavy Wednesday (48 switches, 20.2 per active hour, 2h49 of Instagram reels, a meeting), a meeting on Thursday, a short Friday, a weekend game in full screen (5h10, until 01:10 counted with Saturday), to-dos opened and closed (one overdue), and nudges dismissed and replied. The previous week was lighter.

| Call | Tokens (in / out) | Latency | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| Review, one call | 11.5k / 2.1k | 24 s | $0.044 |
| Review with one corrective round (before the two-field fix) | 25.3k / 3.8k | 39 s | $0.088 |
| Weekly portrait run taking the 2 candidates (ops + render) | 13.0k / 4.2k | - | $0.069 |

What the run produced:
- The review cited the real figures: 31h54 at the PC against 13h27 (+137%), watching 7h04 against 1h24, 5h10 of a game, and Wednesday's 48 switches.
- It named only listed to-dos. The overdue Q3 deck came first.
- It proposed two candidates. One refined the existing behaviour fact F6; its asked confidence of 0.35 was capped to 0.3, since its episode fell on one day. The other was new, at 0.3.
- The teaser: "31h54m at the PC this week (nearly 3x last week) and Varun's deck is still overdue, boss."
- The weekly portrait run then updated F6, citing episodes from four days at 0.4, and added the morning-stretch pattern at 0.3. The candidates were marked as given to that run.

Known limits:
- Small wording slips: "tripled" for 3.4x, and "afternoon block" for one that began at 10:35.
- The portrait's candidate uptake is the model's call.
- The card's title for `review` comes from the UI (`yuki/ui/nudges.py`). It shows "Yuki" until that module names the kind.

## Fast memory, provenance, dreaming (OpenClaw-inspired, 2026-09-26)

Design basis: `docs/research/openclaw-memory.md`, `docs/research/openclaw-harness-features.md` (top 15). Code: `yuki/memory/relevant.py`, `provenance.py`, `dreaming.py`, `persona.py`; changes in `store.py` (migration 17), `journal.py`, `conversations.py`, `portrait.py`, `api.py`, `personality.py`, `yuki/agent/{memory,loop,prompt,tools}.py`, `yuki/mcp/server.py`. Tests: `tests/test_openclaw_memory.py`.

**Migration 17 (additive).** `journal.source_ciphertext` (provenance JSON), `journal.tainted`, `portrait_facts.importance`, `portrait_fact_vec`, `standing_requests`, `yuki_persona`, `memory_changelog`, `knowhow.origin`. A copy of the real database migrated 16 -> 17 in well under a second.

**1. Fast memory mid-chat** (OpenClaw's trigger injection). Before every reply (typed and the voice thinker: both go through `Agent.run`), the user's words are embedded once by the shared embedder (the service's, over its pipe; the same vector the know-how lookup uses, cached per text) and ranked against every current portrait fact, journal fact, open or done commitment and episode, of any age: `score = cosine + 0.06 x (importance - 5)/5 + 0.05 x 0.5^(age/14 days)` (portrait facts evergreen). Importance is written once by the model that writes the item (journal: per fact; portrait: the operations tool's new `importance`; episodes: the highest journal importance inside them; dreaming moves it). Uncertain and AI-tool (tainted) journal facts never go in; a near-duplicate of a picked item (cosine 0.90) is left out; at most 4 items. The previous message of the conversation is searched too, 0.05 lower, so a follow-up ("elaborate", "what was that?") keeps its subject. The block goes in the request's messages right before the user's words (with the commitments), never in the system prompt: it changes every message and the cached core/profile/rules layers must not. `MemoryClient.relevant()`, `MemoryAccess.context(..., previous=)`, `relevant_block_text()`.
- *Threshold, measured on a copy of the real memory* (19 real-style messages): chit-chat ("hi", "ok", "what's up", "minimize discord") never above cosine 0.37; true hits 0.48-0.86 (CLI setup 0.61-0.66, Hemanth 0.81-0.86, the pitch 0.50-0.62). `MIN_COSINE = 0.48`.
- *Latency, the service's embedder over its pipe*: embedding p50 9 ms, p95 67 ms; ranking + decrypting the picks 1.6 ms p50 (index refresh 0.5 ms: only new rows are loaded, liveness by id queries); the agent's whole memory lookup p50 28 ms. The first lookup of a process builds the index (1,171 rows) in 143 ms. New portrait facts (a correction a minute ago) are embedded at the next lookup.

**2. Deep recall.** `recall` takes `with_time_use` (needs `since`): the facts plus the measured time use of the same window (apps, calls with real start and end, episodes), to reconcile in one answer; the tool description and the prompt say when (when, how long, a day or stretch of time, "where did I say"). No classifier. Replay: "what did I do last Friday afternoon" -> one `recall` call (Fri 12:00-18:00, with time use), answer naming the merged PRs, the outreach emails, the ElevenLabs stretch and "Discord call was running most of the afternoon".

**3. Provenance.** Every journal fact stores where it came from, from the source the journal model cited: kind (`call` | `message` | `command` | `list` | `page` | `chat`), who said or wrote it, a short quote (a short call utterance gets the one before it), where (the call's app, the channel or conversation, the page), and the source's own time (`provenance.source_of`, `chat_source`). Rows written before are recovered by `python -m yuki.memory.provenance` (also run once by the portrait scheduler at start): the batch's captures are still stored, the sources rebuilt, the fact's own stored time names its source, embedder similarity breaks ties. Copy of the real memory: 1,040 rows, 996 by time, 16 by similarity, 28 chat, 0 missing, 25 s. Every recall hit, fast-memory item, portrait fact (its earliest cited entry with words, "and N more") and MCP `search_memory` result carries the line (`provenance.describe`): `Discord call, Fri 25 Sep 04:26; the user's own words: "... CLI access will connect over MCP over Chrome ..."`. The prompt: asked what something is or to elaborate, say where and when it came from and what was said, never repeat the summary; to elaborate, recall it. A user explaining where something stands ("I'm waiting for Varun's approval") is `correct_memory`.

**4. Dreaming** (`dreaming.py`, after the nightly/weekly/first run and its audit; `python -m yuki.memory.dreaming [--db] [--dry-run]`). One Sonnet call sees every current fact with what code measures (evidence entries and days, last evidence, journal entries of the last 14 days close to it, look-alike pairs at cosine 0.80) and decides merge (citations are the union; merged-away versions point to the merged one), retire, promote, demote, rate. Code caps it: at most 8 changes a night, at most 15% retired, never a user-origin fact, a correction or an open loop (those are only rated), merges within one kind. Each applied change writes a diary line (`memory_changelog`, pass `dream`; `MemoryClient.memory_diary()`) for the future Yuki window; the page is rendered once at the end of the nightly pass. Copy of the real memory: retired F128 ("duplicates the richer, user-authored F53 covering the same Warp/YouTube switching pattern") and rated the unrated facts; $0.13-0.15 a night.

**5. AI-tool taint.** `record_facts` items carry `other_ai`: the fact's content comes from a prompt the user typed into, or an answer they read from, another AI tool (judged from the content by the journal model, no site list). Stored as `journal.tainted`: `personality.usable()` leaves it out (coach, episodes, weekly review), the portrait's operations and audit see "other AI tool or sample text" (sample, demo and test content joined it 2026-10-01) and code rejects an ADD/UPDATE resting only on it, fast memory skips it, recall marks it. Older rows are untainted until re-journaled.

**6. Separate budgets.** `save_portrait` has a `profile` field (People, Preferences, Relationship: at most 350 words, 2,400 characters) apart from `text` (Work, Interests, Routines, Behaviour) and `personality` (2,000); each is cut to its own budget, so work never squeezes out who the user is.

**7. Yuki's persona** (`persona.py`, nightly after dreaming; `python -m yuki.memory.persona`). One Sonnet call reads the last 7 days of exchanges (numbered T1..Tn: 16-digit ids got mis-copied), card replies and the rules (never restated) and proposes at most 3 changes to a few "how Yuki talks" lines. Every add/update quotes the user's own words from a cited exchange (checked like a rule's quote); at most 6 lines of 180 characters, 900 in all; bi-temporal (`yuki_persona`); diary pass `persona`. It goes into the agent's profile layer (`prompt.profile_text(..., persona)`) and to the mouth through `MemoryClient.yuki_persona()` / `MemoryContext.persona`. Copy: "Doesn't say 'Done' or 'Scrolled' until the action actually happened - he keeps catching Yuki claiming success on things that didn't work." (the user: "No, you did not scroll, bro."); one other proposal rejected by the quote check; $0.03.

**8. Standing requests.** The agent's `watch_for` tool (add | list | cancel) saves "tell me when Varun replies" with the user's own words (checked against the request). While any is open, the journal's own `record_facts` call gets OPEN REQUESTS after the fence and a `request_matches` field (no keyword prefilter; no open request = the call is exactly as before). A match writes one card (kind `reminder`, `ref` = `request:<id>`, the model's one line), gated in code in one transaction: the request open and not expired (14 days default), its cooldown (30 min), its fire budget (1, or 5 when asked "every time"), at most 6 request cards a day. Copy: a synthetic Slack DM from Varun -> "Varun replied on Slack: approved, go ahead with the scope — he'll send the final scope by tomorrow noon", request `fired`.

**9. Know-how from corrections.** The prompt and the `done` tool say to call `remember_how` after a correction led to success; in stub-driven replays the voice thinker (low effort, thinking off) still did not (0 of 3), so the pipeline carries it: the conversation worker finds runs of consecutive user messages close in meaning (cosine 0.60, the shared embedder) where Yuki acted - the user asking again - and one focused Haiku call (`record_knowhow`) records what worked and what Yuki's first steps did instead, quote-checked, deduplicated against the app's entries (`Store.knowhow_duplicate`), origin `conversation`. Replay of the 20:56-20:58 session: 4 of 4 saved ("click and drag the volume slider on the YouTube player itself, not the system media keys ... Not: media_key (volumedown) reduced the system volume"; the big extraction call alone managed 2 of 3, then 0 of 4). It comes back on the next "Reduce the YouTube volume" through the know-how lookup, and the thinker then goes to the player. Known limits: the stored action lines are one-line summaries, so "clicking the volume button mutes; hover to reveal the slider" is not recovered, and there is no pointer-move action to hover with; the voice mouth reads memory with an empty query, so relevance-ranked know-how does not reach its hands yet.

**Cost** (the owner's target: an ordinary day about $1). 26 Sept spent about $5.2 (journal $1.53 over 183 calls, portrait $2.61, conversations $0.48, coach, episodes). Changes:
- the journal's and conversation extraction's fixed prompt + tool (5.5-5.7k tokens, above Haiku 4.5's 4,096 minimum) carry a cache breakpoint; measured on the copy, a call reads 5,669 cached tokens. Replaying 26 Sept's calls: journal $1.53 -> $0.77 (23 cache writes, 160 reads), conversations $0.48 -> $0.29;
- the incremental audit reviews only facts with genuinely new evidence (new, corrected, a new version that moved in meaning below cosine 0.90, or new journal entries within cosine 0.62; no subject-substring or same-thread rules), at most every 20 h unless a run folded a correction; replayed at 26 Sept's two audits: 39 -> 29 and 36 -> 22 facts;
- a correction asks for the fold + render instead of a full refresh (26 Sept: one correction cost a $0.34 refresh and a $0.71 audit);
- one render per nightly pass (run, audit, dreaming); the render flag waits 5 min and renders at most every 30 min (26 Sept: two pairs of renders 1-3 minutes apart).
Estimate for a day like 26 Sept: about $2.9 (from $5.2), dreaming and the persona included (~$0.17). Not yet at $1: the rest is the nightly operations and audit (~$0.95) and the journal's many one-source batches (128 of 160 calls had at most 2 sources), which could be batched across threads.

**Standing block fix.** One 1,200-character cap covered rules, preferences, coach feedback and commitments; five long rules pushed the "don't nudge me" preference (C13) out, so at 22:15 "you can nudge me again" found no id to withdraw (the extractor revoked it as the backstop). The user's instructions now have their own 2,400 budget (commitments keep 1,200), `revoke_rule` takes `preference:N`, and the prompt says any withdrawal, in any words, is `forget_rule` with that id in the same turn. Replay on a copy: `forget_rule rule:13`, "Done, nudges are back on."; the extractor alone also revokes it.

**To run after deploying.** Nothing by hand is required: at its next start the service migrates to 17 and the portrait scheduler embeds the portrait facts and backfills provenance (`python -m yuki.memory.provenance` does the same on demand); the first nightly pass rates the unrated facts and starts the diary and the persona. Re-journaling (`python -m yuki.memory.rejournal`) would mark AI-tool text in older rows.

## Corrections everywhere, to-dos, talk not meant for Yuki (2026-09-27)

From the user's review of the Yuki window. Code: `yuki/memory/corrections.py`, `store.py` (migration 18),
`conversations.py`, `nudges.py`, `questions.py`, `api.py` (block at the end of `MemoryClient`), `portrait.py`
(scheduler), `yuki/agent/{prompt,memory,tools}.py`, `yuki/memory/knowhow_backfill.py`. Tests: `tests/test_corrections.py`.

**Migration 18 (additive).** `correction_passes` (one row per user correction: queued | applying | done | failed |
skipped, what changed, cost), `name_aliases` (a wrong or other form of a name and the right one, from a
correction), `conversation_facts.owner` (a commitment is Yuki's, "yuki", or the user's own to-do, "user"),
`conversation_turns.addressed` (a voice turn said to "yuki" or "others"; since report 77b also "unanswered", a
call turn the next words cut off before Yuki answered, until the extractor judges it).

**A correction applies everywhere, within minutes.** `add_correction` and a question's answer queue a pass in the
same transaction (a loop marked done does not). The portrait scheduler runs queued passes first on its next tick
(the render flag the correction writes wakes it): the portrait's own fold, then one Sonnet call that reads the
user's own words (parsed from Yuki's fixed "in their own words:" format) and names the terms and aliases, local
candidates (embedding neighbours plus items containing those terms) from journal facts, portrait facts,
commitments and to-dos, user to-dos, episodes, open questions and persona lines, one Sonnet call that rewrites or
retires the ones the user's words contradict, then bi-temporal supersedes (journal: a new row with the old time,
thread, provenance and taint; conversation items keep their quote), a diary line each (pass `correct`), one render.
Validated in code: a candidate id, the words it calls wrong are in the item, a rewrite grows the item at most
x1.35 + 40 characters, an alias only when an applied rewrite carried it out. A later correction's words win.
`MemoryClient.correction_status(id)` -> updating | done | failed | none. Measured on a copy with the 13 real
corrections of 26-27 Sept: Haiku misread two ("gradient bang" renamed "Discord" from a "that's right") and
rewrote items no correction was about; Sonnet: $0.02-0.09 a correction, "not barun, its varun" fixed the journal
entry and the to-do, and recorded Barun -> Varun. Known limit: Sonnet still reads a casual "hes" as a pronoun fix.
Aliases reach WHAT MEMORY ALREADY KNOWS (journal, calls, extraction) and `MemoryClient.speech_names()`.
Run by hand: `python -m yuki.memory.corrections --since 2026-09-26 [--dry-run] [--db]`.

**To-dos, not promises.** The extractor gives each commitment an owner; a call plan is the user's by
construction; older rows are sorted once by one Sonnet call (`ConversationWorker.sort_owners`, which also ends a
commitment whose words were not said to Yuki as `wrong`). `todos()` rows carry `owner` and `label`, source `chat`
for the user's own to-dos from a chat. `end_todo(id, "done" | "not_needed" | "wrong")`; ended items leave the list,
the coach and reminders, and a wrong capture is shown to the extractor so the like is not captured again. The
user's own plans are asked about before any reminder, like call plans.

**Talk not meant for Yuki.** `log_turn(..., addressed=)`: a turn said to others is stored and never extracted (a
batch of only such turns costs no call). The extractor also judges from the content (`not_for_yuki`), and nothing
resting only on those exchanges becomes an item, answer, question, know-how or journal fact.

**A call turn nobody judged (report 77b).** In a call, a turn whose next words came before Yuki said or did anything
is stored `addressed = "unanswered"`, not "others": only the talker's `stay_quiet` is a judgement that words were for
the call. The extractor reads such a turn (marked SAID IN A CALL, NEVER ANSWERED, with worked examples) and names
the ones said to Yuki in `for_yuki`; one left out counts as said to the call (its words never become an item). Its
verdict replaces the mark in the batch's transaction (`commit_conversation_batch(addressed=)`). When a batch's newest
turn is unanswered, the session's next exchanges are shown after it as LATER IN THIS CONVERSATION (context only), and
a live batch ending in one waits up to `max_wait_s` for its next exchange (`held_until`), so the first half of a
request is judged with the rest. Until judged, every reader treats it like "others": search, call-digest spans, the
to-do check. Before 77b such turns were stored as "others" (23 all time to 5 Oct, 15 of them followed by a request
Yuki answered); `python -m yuki.memory.addressee_backfill [--db] [--voice-logs] [--dry-run | --apply [--undo-file F]
| --undo F]` marks them "unanswered" from the voice log's own rows and requeues them for the worker to judge. The dry
run opens the database read-only; `--apply` changes only rows still as planned and writes an undo file first;
`--undo` restores the rows the worker has not judged yet.

## The user's own sites and accounts (2026-09-28)

The user had Yuki open his portfolio by voice (the name misheard first, then "no it's example dev", then example.dev opened)
and memory kept nothing. Conversation facts of kind `resource` (a standing kind, migration 22) now hold the user's
own sites, accounts and emails, repos and project links: `subject` what it is, `value` the identifier, `keyterms`
the words speech recognition must hear right, all encrypted.

- **Extraction** (`conversations.py`, model-judged in the prompt and schema): only the user's own things, never
  passwords or codes; the spelling that worked (the page Yuki's actions show opened, the text typed, the user's
  correction) wins over a misheard first try, and a correction in the same exchanges leaves only the corrected
  one. UPDATE for a new one, INVALIDATE revoked when not theirs. Code checks the value against the exchanges
  (letters and digits, case ignored); a value no exchange spells (only dictated) is kept with the note "Spelled
  as heard ..." and is not given to speech recognition. The journal prompt also records the user's own sites and
  accounts a screen shows as theirs.
- **At request time**: `standing_context()` lists them on every request under their own heading and budget
  (700 characters); fast memory (`relevant.py`) indexes them as kind `resource`, evergreen, embedded as
  "subject: value" ("open my portfolio" -> cosine 0.53 against 0.30-0.41 for the full sentence).
  `MemoryClient.user_resources()` lists them; `speech_names()` adds the confirmed ones' keyterms after the
  corrected names. A correction rewrites a resource's text and drops its old value and keyterms.
- **Re-extraction** of 13:30-13:32 on the real database (backup `backup-20260928-resources`), $0.026: resource 31
  "The user's portfolio website is example.dev (https://www.example.dev/, a page titled "<Owner Name>")."
  (keyterm exampledev) and resource 32, the dictated email sam@example.com (as heard).

## Short labels for the app (2026-09-28)

The Me and Yuki pages showed each item as its first sentence cut off, which read as machine-made. Memory now keeps a
model-written label per item (migration 21, encrypted like the text; code `yuki/memory/titles.py`):

| Item | Field (API / attribute) | Words |
|---|---|---|
| Portrait fact of any kind, people included | `summary` in `portrait_facts_with_evidence()`, `PortraitFact.summary` | at most 8 |
| Open loop (an `open_loop` fact) | its fact's `summary`; `title` of its `todos()` row, `OpenLoop.title` | 3 to 6 |
| Commitment, the user's to-do from a chat or call, a to-do the user added | `title` in `todos()`, `ConversationFact.title`, `TodoItem.title` | 3 to 6 |
| Know-how | `KnowHow.title` (what it does), `KnowHow.summary` (the way that works) | 3 to 6 / at most 8 |

`None` until written; the window falls back to the text. Labels are plain notes (no "the user", no tool names, ids,
dashes or parentheses, nothing the item does not say) and are never cut: code keeps a label only within its word
bounds (counted by whitespace, 2 words at least) and otherwise asks again.

- **At the source**, in the calls that already write the item: the portrait's operations (`update_portrait` and
  `audit_portrait` items carry `summary`: nightly, weekly, refresh, fold, audit), the conversation extractor
  (`title` on commitments; `title` and `summary` on know-how, also in the focused know-how pass), the journal's
  call-side commitments (`title`), and the correction pass (the judge's `label` for a rewritten P, C or T item: a
  corrected item gets the label of its new wording). Dreaming's merges and the agent's `remember_how` / `add_todo`
  write none.
- **The batched pass** (`Titler`, one forced Haiku call per 40 labels, portrait runs of kind `titles`, logged in
  `portrait-<date>.jsonl`) fills the rest: the portrait scheduler runs it when the user has been idle 2 minutes, at
  most every 5 minutes, and right after a correction pass. A label over its limit is asked for once more in the same
  pass, told its length outside the data fence; an item that fails 3 times waits for the next start.
- **Backfill**: `python -m yuki.memory.titles --backfill [--dry-run] [--db PATH]`; `--redo` rewrites every label.
  On a copy of the real database (74 facts, 4 know-how): 82 labels, 4 calls, $0.027, 23 s. The real database
  (2026-09-28, backed up first to `backup-20260928-titles-2`): 82 of 82 labels, then a `--redo` after a prompt
  fix, 11 calls and $0.07 in all.
- **Cost**: the added fields are ~250 input tokens on calls that already run (about 20 Sonnet portrait calls a
  day: operations, folds, audits; the correction judge; the cached Haiku extractors) and ~10 output tokens per
  item; the pass sees only what arrived without a label (~$0.004 a call). Estimated $0.03 a day, at most ~$0.05.

## Connecting the dots: threads of work, whole-memory recall, the screen (2026-09-29)

The user: "It captures everything, stores it, and connects the dots, and brings it into the conversation like a
personal Windows assistant ... if the user is asking about something it connects the dots from the recorded
workflow" and "20 mins is too less, it should connect dots from the entire memory it has." Design basis:
`docs/research/human-like-review.md` §5 Phase 3 as changed by §6a. Code: `yuki/memory/workthreads.py`,
`related.py`, `screenrecall.py`, `links.py`, `before.py`, `routines.py` (new); changes in `store.py` (migrations 23
and 24), `journal.py`, `relevant.py`, `api.py` (block at the end of `MemoryClient`), `embed_ipc.py` (extra ops),
`service.py`, `episodes.py`, `conversations.py`, `portrait.py`, `nudges.py` (the resume data moved to `before.py`),
`yuki/agent/{memory,tools,untrusted}.py`, `yuki/mcp/server.py`. Tests: `tests/test_phase3_dots.py`. No model runs
on a request's path for any of it.

**Migration 23 (additive).** `work_threads(id, title, kind, status active|paused|done|merged|dismissed, state,
open, entities, first_seen, last_seen, importance, origin, merged_into, events)`, `work_thread_events` (every
start, continue, pause, end, reopen, glimpse, merge, close and rename: when, from which capture thread, where the
thread stood after it), `work_thread_vec`, `journal.work_thread_id`, `links_seen(url_key HMAC(how, url), url,
title, host, app, how viewed|shared, sender, where, page_key, capture_thread_id, work_thread_id, by_yuki,
first_seen, last_seen, visits)`, `link_vec`, `call_digest_vec`. Text columns encrypted. **Migration 24
(additive)**: `journal.attention`, `links_seen.attention` (below, "Windows beside the one in front").

**1. Threads of work** (a project, task or workflow pursued over days: building a feature, a PR review, an
application, studying, helping someone). Kept by the journal's own per-batch call, no extra call:
- The batch is offered up to 8 threads (`workthreads.candidates`, plumbing on ids and the shared embedder): the
  ones its window was already part of (3), the closest by similarity to the batch's text (cosine 0.30 or more, 4)
  and the most recently active (3), as `W<n>` lines with where each stands. `record_facts` / `record_call_side`
  carry a required `work` array: `continues` | `starts` | `pauses` | `ends`, the thread, a title for a new one,
  one sentence of where it stands, at most 3 open items, the apps, sites, repos and people involved, the sources
  it rests on. The prompt (`WORK_GUIDANCE`) says what is and is not a thread (model-judged, no app or site list),
  that an AI agent's work in the user's project is that project's thread, and that work is a second job that
  never replaces a fact.
- **Where the block goes matters.** Placed between FACTS ALREADY RECORDED and the sources, Haiku took the
  thread lines for recorded facts: re-journaling 28 Sept 20:00-23:30 (32 captures) on a copy whose thread lines
  already described those captures (backfilled past them; live, a line can describe an earlier read of the same
  page) gave 0 facts on 3 threads where the old prompt gave 1-5, in 3 of 3 readings each, even when told the lines
  are not recorded facts. After the sources (inside the fence) it gave 1-5 again, 3 of 3 each. The block now
  follows the sources.
- Checked in code (`parse_work`): an existing thread only when it was offered; a new one needs a title; what Yuki
  put on screen (BY YUKI) and what was only glimpsed (below) start nothing; one op per thread, at most 2; an
  older source (a history message) never overwrites a newer state or status; a "start" within cosine 0.86 of an
  existing thread continues that one (measured on the backfill: pairs the model later merged sat at 0.68-0.82,
  distinct threads up to 0.72, so 0.86 only catches renames). The batch's facts link to the op whose sources hold
  theirs (`journal.work_thread_id`); every op is an event; all in the batch's transaction.
- **Nightly tidy** (`workthreads.Consolidator`, after dreaming and the routine miner, under the portrait worker's
  lock): one Sonnet call sees the current threads (done ones for 14 days) with look-alike pairs (cosine 0.62) and
  decides `merge` (facts, events and links move to the thread kept), `close` (paused | done), `rename`,
  `update`, `dismiss` (not a piece of work: its facts let go of it) - at most 10 changes a night - and asks the
  user through `questions.py` when a merge is ambiguous. Diary pass `threads`. About $0.10 a night.
- **Backfill** (`python -m yuki.memory.workthreads --backfill [--since] [--until] [--model sonnet|haiku]
  [--dry-run] [--db]`): the journal read in time order, 9k-character chunks, Sonnet by default (Haiku started
  104 threads on the copy, many of them videos, games and chats), checkpointed (`workthreads.backfill_at`), then
  a tidy (up to 80 changes, low effort) and the links sync. Copy of the real memory (1,923 facts, 24-29 Sept):
  59 calls, 415k in / 38k out tokens, **$1.24**, 75 threads; two tidies $0.10 + $0.09 (38 changes) -> 36 active,
  17 paused, 11 done, 8 dismissed, 3 merged; 904 of 1,923 facts linked to a thread.
- **Journal cost of the work field**, the same 32 captures (28 Sept 20:00-23:30) re-journaled with the old code
  (two runs, on a copy without threads) and the new one (on a copy whose threads were backfilled only up to
  20:00, and on the fully backfilled one): per batch input 3.7k -> 4.7-4.8k tokens (the THREADS OF WORK block),
  output 260-280 -> 470-540 (the work items, and more facts: 89-104 against 71-80 - mostly more small facts from
  a long call), cached system prompt 7.0-7.2k -> 9.0-9.5k read; **$0.0060-0.0065 -> $0.0080-0.0092 a batch
  (+30-40%)**. At 150-270 batches a day (25-28 Sept) that is **+$0.3-0.55 a day** on a journal of $0.7-2.1.
  Most work answers continue a thread (17 of 17 on the past-only copy).

**2. Whole-memory recall at request time** (`related.related`, `MemoryClient.related()`). Every typed and voice
request's memory context (`MemoryAccess.context`) now carries a "Connected from memory" block instead of the
four-item fast-memory block (the fast-memory path stays as the fallback):
- Queries, embedded once each over the service's pipe: the user's words (and the previous message, 0.05 lower),
  the screen now (its title, site, app and the head of its text; 0.04 lower, 0.10 lower when there are words:
  then the screen is context for the question) and the current thread of work (its vector; 0.05 lower when it is
  the screen's own thread, 0.20 when it is only the one touched last and there is a screen - measured: the last
  thread's items crowded the page's out).
- Ranked over every kind in one in-RAM index (`relevant.RelevantIndex`, kinds `portrait`, `journal`,
  `commitment`, `episode`, `resource`, `session`, `chat`, `knowhow`, `call`, `thread`, `link`): `score = max over
  queries (cosine - discount) + importance + recency + 0.04 affinity - 0.04 weak attention`, floors 0.48 for words
  and 0.52 for the screen and the thread (exchanges 0.60, links 0.55: short texts close to everything), +0.03 for
  an item two kinds of query found (it connects them), near duplicates (0.90) dropped, at most 3 of a kind, 8
  items (voice: 4). Affinity: items linked to the current thread (its facts and links, the episodes and calls
  around its events; id-only queries, `Store.work_affinity`) rank higher once over the floor, never in by it.
  Left out: what restates the screen (its window's facts of the last 2 h, its links), the current threads (shown
  in their own section), the thread's own facts for the thread query, and pages merely viewed that only the
  screen or thread found (another page of the same site is no connection; a link someone shared still is).
- The block: the screen line, the current threads (the screen's own marked ON SCREEN) with state and open
  items, the last 45 minutes of screens (`related.recent`, condensed, 1,800 characters; fenced as untrusted), then
  the items with provenance (where, when, who): ~4k characters (~1k tokens, ~$0.002 a Sonnet request, not
  cached: it changes every message). `block_text(voice=True)` is the compact one for the talker (~1.4k
  characters, 21 ms warm through `MemoryAccess.related_brief`).
- **Latency over the pipe** (a stand-in service on a copy, 80 requests, the machine at 98-100% CPU from other
  processes the whole time): p50 39-55 ms, p95 80-351 ms in three runs (the spikes are the embedder's, under
  contention), 16 ms CPU in the tray per request; the first `related()` of a process builds the index (~0.2-1 s),
  so `MemoryAccess.warm()` builds it at start. Before `Store.work_affinity` the thread stage alone took 58 ms (it
  decrypted every link).

**3. Screen-triggered recall** (`screenrecall.ScreenRecall`, a thread in the memory service). The store's
capture listener (`Store.add_capture_listener`) wakes it; a burst is ranked once quiet 2 s (at most 6 s after its
first capture), at most every 4 s, and not at all when the screen's text did not change. The screen is the Now
board's foreground entry (`yuki.memory.now.NowBoard`, read in-process, no polling) and else the newest capture
taken with the window in front. The cache (screen, its thread, the 12 best items with scores; RAM only) is served
on the embedder's pipe as op `screen_related` (`EmbedServer.register`), so a request never embeds the screen
itself. Every 60 s it also syncs the links seen and embeds new call digests. Measured replaying 28 Sept (196
captures, 148 rankings, 43 unchanged): 188 ms CPU a ranking (p95 313 ms, mostly the embedding), 33 s of CPU over
the day = 0.04% of one core on average, at most ~5% of one core while switching windows every few seconds; the
index (3.5k rows) adds ~6 MB, ~10 MB after the replay. Logs `screen_recall` (ms, CPU ms, hits by kind) and the
worker's counters in the service's stats line.

**4. Links seen** (`links.py`): pages from the timeline (time in front, one visit per stretch) and the capture
threads, links in chat and mail messages with who sent them (the capture's own message format), each linked to
the thread of work of its window. `links.sync` on the service's minute tick; `MemoryClient.links(query, since,
until, how)`. Copy: 628 links (first full sync 47-53 s, once; the service embeds one batch a pass).

**5. Tools.** Agent (`yuki/agent/tools.py`): `threads` (`list`, `search`, `open`: one thread whole - its
timeline, facts, the calls, chats and episodes around it, its links),
`before` (what the user was doing before a break or a time: the break nearest the moment - away, off or asleep,
10 min or more, within 14 h - and the stretch before it, rebuilt by the same code the coach's welcome back uses,
`before.stretch_lines`, moved out of `nudges.py`), `links` ("that link I was looking at": the most recent first,
or by what it was about; resolution only, sending is the user's call). MCP (`yuki/mcp/server.py`, read-only):
`get_threads`, `get_links`, `get_related`.

**6. Routines and people.** `routines.RoutineMiner` (nightly, after dreaming): the timeline's last 14 days
measured in code (days seen, breaks of 20 min or more, activity by hour, calls) and one Sonnet call (low effort)
that may ADD or UPDATE only `routine` facts, each citing the measured evidence; validated like the nightly
operations. Copy: 1 update, 2 adds, $0.03. `python -m yuki.memory.routines [--evidence] [--dry-run] [--db]`. The
portrait audit gets PEOPLE ACROSS APPS (the people of the last 14 days by app, at most 24, two example facts
each) and may link one person seen in two apps only by asking the user, never by merging on a guess.

**7. Learning from conversation.** The extractor's know-how items carry `how`: `worked` (Yuki's actions
succeeded), `taught` (the user explained a procedure or a preference for how to do something) or `failed` (what
Yuki tried that did not work: "what doesn't work" know-how, never an invented fix), stored with origin
`conversation` | `taught` | `failed`. Copy: a taught and a failed case each came out as know-how. A spoken "then
don't mention it" after Yuki raised something is still saved as a standing rule (3 of 3, typed and spoken).

**8. Episodes never tell one stretch twice.** 29 Sept 03:45-04:08 came out twice: one Haiku answer held two
episodes of the same span, and `_parse` accepted both. `episodes.non_overlapping` keeps the finest episodes
(shortest first; one covering half or more of a kept one is a retelling) and trims a small overlap off the later
one. Existing rows: `python -m yuki.memory.episodes --repair-overlaps [--dry-run] [--db]` (copy: 2 pairs).

**9. Windows beside the one in front, and popups** (the eyes read every visible window and popups since
2026-09-29; triggers `visible:<why>` and `popup`). The journal marks their sources NOT IN FRONT / POPUP and
records them as what was on screen or an event that appeared ("Journal worker"); on synthetic captures (a
document beside the window in front, a toast, a page in front) Haiku wrote "A Notion page titled '...' on screen at
12:47 showed ...", "A Gmail notification appeared on screen showing ..." and "The user viewed ...", and started
no thread. Each fact keeps its source's attention (`journal.attention`), a link seen only beside the window in
front is `links_seen.attention = visible` until it is seen in front, and the index ranks both 0.04 lower (`relevant.ATTENTION_DISCOUNT`: a
starting weight - no such capture existed before 29 Sept to measure it on). What was only glimpsed starts no
thread; it may update where a thread stands but is no touch of the user's (event `seen`: status and last seen
stay; `latest_work_events` lists only the user's own touches - which also leaves out the nightly tidy's events,
found when "the thread touched last" turned out to be the one tidied last). The screen for recall and the coach's
"on screen" excerpts are the window in front only (`Store.captures_between`, `related.screen_from_store`).

**Evaluation** (a copy of the real memory, the typed brain - Sonnet 5 - on the offline stub desktop, a fresh agent
per question; before = HEAD 2857ce7 on the copy, after = this code on the copy with the backfill; two after runs):

| Question | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| "continue where I left off" | looked at the stub windows, "nothing mid-flight" (31 s) | `before`: named the last work and the tutorial still open; offered to reopen it (36 s), or reopened it (98 s, the stub shows no page) |
| "what was I doing before lunch yesterday?" | the whole morning from `activity` + `recall` (21 s) | `before`: the stretch before the lunch break, 12:49-14:19, and the last thing in front (10-13 s) |
| "send him that link I was looking at" | could not tell (21 s) | `links`: the three most recent pages, asked which and to whom (11-12 s); nothing sent in any run |
| "what did we decide about how you should talk, on the discord call on sunday?" | right (21 s) | right (10-19 s), one run with the later revision |
| "what's the status of the ... relay work?" | "shipped" (7 s) | shipped, then the deploy workflow that failed after it and was never confirmed fixed (9-17 s) |
| "is this useful for what I'm building?" (a GitHub page on screen) | "not really" (23 s) | connects the page's architecture to the user's current agent work; the block also showed the user had viewed it on Friday (25-28 s) |

Cost per question $0.02-0.08 before, $0.02-0.19 after (the tool calls). The agent's whole memory lookup
(`lookup_ms`, with the lookups that were there before) ~180-470 ms after the first request of the process (there
is no service in the harness, so the embedder loads in-process on the first one: 2.3-3.2 s before and after).

**To run on the real memory** (after the service runs this code; it migrates to 24 at start): back up
`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\memory` first; `python -m yuki.memory.workthreads --backfill --db <real db>` (~$1.2-1.5, 10
min: threads, tidy, links, call digest vectors); `python -m yuki.memory.episodes --repair-overlaps --dry-run --db
<real db>` then without `--dry-run`. Without the backfill, threads start from the next journal batches and links
from the service's sync.

**Known limits.** Recall is similarity: a page whose README never names the user's project does not reach that
project's thread (the screen case's Pipecat thread sat at 0.50, under the 0.52 floor; 0.48-0.52 was mostly noise
on 60 sampled screens). Thread states are one sentence: detail lives in the linked facts (`threads` tool). The
attention discount is unmeasured. The voice talker does not get the block yet (the voice builder wires
`MemoryAccess.related_brief`), and the loop-breaker's failures reach "failed" know-how only once `loop.py` writes
them into the exchange.

## Observation cards: noticing, offering, never speaking (2026-09-29)

The user: "It has everything to capture and everything, still it's not a proactive agent. I want it to be
proactive 150% so it feels like a human lives in my PC", then chose **offers on cards only**: "no proactive
speaking, only messages are fine" (`docs/research/human-like-review.md` §6a). The coach's rules stand: no praise,
no generic lines, personal and evidence-based, what a problem or a session *is* judged by a model from content.
Code: `yuki/memory/presence.py` (new), `nudges.py` (wiring, the welcome back's threads and offer, feedback),
`store.py` (kind `notice`, reactions `accepted` / `less`, `milestones_since(until)`, `journal_vectors`,
`journal_written`, `journal_newest`, `call_digests_ready`), `api.py` (`offer` / `trigger` on the rows, missed
cards in the history), `service.py` (the screen recall's cache handed in), `yuki/core/attention.py` (the kinds'
channels, `load`); the UI in `docs/UI.md` ("Observation cards"). Tests: `tests/test_presence.py`,
`tests/test_presence_cards.py` (offscreen). Replay: `scripts/presence_replay.py` (a COPY only).

**What it is.** `PresenceWorker` runs inside the coach's worker, after the welcome back and the due reminders and
before the questions, so every coach gate holds it (paused, locked, snoozed, quiet hours, a meeting or full screen
in front, Yuki acting, away, not settled) plus any open call; it shares the call rate (`max_calls_per_hour`,
`min_gap_s`) and the card gap (`notice` is in `SPACED_KINDS`). At most one look a tick. A card is a nudge of kind
`notice` (`trigger` = its channel, `ref` = what it was about, `inputs.offer` = its typed offer, run by code),
written with its check-in in one transaction; every model call goes to `logs/memory/nudges-YYYYMMDD.jsonl` like
the coach's. Never spoken: nothing in `yuki/voice/*` changed.

**When it looks: measured signals only** (numbers, never words; each looks again only when its input changed):
