- `stuck` - something keeps coming back. The user's own journal facts of the last hour (written by now, from the
  window in front, not humour, not uncertain, not another AI tool's text, not Yuki's doing) compared by their stored
  embeddings: a fact at most 15 min old with 2+ others at cosine >= `STUCK_COSINE` (0.75), spanning 6+ min
  (`recurrence`). Measured on 28 Sept: 0.75 isolates the two real recurrences (a Slack bot asked the same thing three
  times, 19:11-19:20; the Corvana Agent deploy failing at 19:52, 20:27 and on the re-run at 20:46), 0.70 adds
  routine repeats (`claude` runs, API pages). A recurrence is looked at again after one newer fact and 10 min when
  the last look showed nothing, two and 30 min once a card went out (each fresh fact makes its own group, so "more"
  counts facts newer than the last look). The first 28 Sept bot cluster was journaled 25 min late, after it had
  resolved: no look, rightly.
- `recap` - a call session's digests are written (rows of one app under 30 min apart are one session; every row
  with 20 s of the user's speech has its digest), it ended within 18 h and holds 90 s of the user's speech. The
  data: the digest's gist and lines (uncertain ones left out, spread over the blocks, 6,000 characters) and the
  user's own call commitments of that span. Only the user's microphone is recorded: the prompt forbids any word of
  anyone else.
- `notice` - the journal's model marked a fact `milestone`, fresh (30 min); shown with the same threads' facts of
  the last 3 h.
- `recall` - the service's screen recall cache (`ScreenRecall.latest`, in-process: no pipe, no new embedding) has a
  hit at least 3 h old of a kind that can hold an answer or an open item - know-how, a shared link (viewed pages are
  dropped by the recall itself), a call, an earlier talk with Yuki, an open commitment, an open loop, a thread with
  open items - at cosine 0.66 (threads 0.70, talks with Yuki 0.74). Measured on 28 Sept: journal facts, episodes
  and other portrait facts near the screen were the same site or topic seen before (18 of 20 looks); a WhatsApp chat
  met an old "send Hemanth a message" request at 0.694 and came out wrong, a thread at 0.669 only told the screen
  back. The same memory is not raised again within 24 h, the same window within 30 min.
- `followup` - new facts are read once (importance 5+, fact or story; the backlog of 7 days first, then every 3 h)
  by a Haiku call (`record_events`) for the user's own dated personal events still ahead when written (an exam, an
  interview, a trip, a visit, a match); never news of something done, what a page or video showed, work meetings
  or deadlines, or an event whose day was not given (a "graduation, date to be sent" was recorded with the fact's
  own date on the first try; the prompt now forbids it). Kept encrypted in `nudge_state` (`presence_events`); asked
  1 h after the moment (an all-day event from 18:00), at most 36 h after. The look carries the rest of the
  conversation each fact came from (its capture thread, +-30 min: a correction said right after) and the two portrait
  facts closest to it (stored vectors, cosine 0.60+); the writer uses names exactly as the data spells them, a later
  correction and the portrait winning over an earlier line.
- `thread` - the thread of work in front changed within 15 min after 3+ touches of another one over 30+ min that
  still has open items; or, once a Yuki day, the most important active thread (importance 8+) with open items
  untouched 36 h-7 d. A thread is not raised again within 3 days.

**What to say: models.** Claude Haiku 4.5 judges (`observe_decision`: none | notice, the detail, the evidence ids,
maybe one offer; `OBSERVE_PROMPT`: what each trigger is for, what an offer may be, no praise, never generic, one
card per thing, RECENT CARDS with their offers and the ids they rested on) and Claude Sonnet 5 writes every card
(`write_notice`, `NOTICE_WRITER_PROMPT`), checking it against the same data and free to say none. Both get the
user's RULES and COACH FEEDBACK after the fence, like the coach. Code checks both answers:
- every evidence id is one the request offered (the look's lines, NOW, the threads' W ids, the to-dos' ids), a
  to-do named is on the list (`suppressed:ungrounded`, `suppressed:unknown_mention`);
- the card rests on the thing that woke the look (`suppressed:no_content`);
- the card's shape for its kind: a `stuck`, `recall` or `thread` card must bring **new information**: cite an id of
  a kind that is not on the screen and not the thing itself - know-how `K`, a link `L`, a call or call line `CALL`/`D`,
  an earlier chat or session `X`/`S`, a portrait fact `P`, a list item `todo:`/`commit:`/`loop:`, or a thread with
  open items `W` (`suppressed:no_new_info`; until 2026-10-03 such a card needed an offer, which forced invented
  offers about the user's own work); a `notice` must cite a line beyond the finish itself and have an offer, name a
  to-do or cite a thread (`suppressed:nothing_next`: a merge with nothing next came out as a report of what they
  did). Ids and fields only, never words.
- a typed offer is checked again in code (below); a card whose offer was dropped is not shown
  (`suppressed:offer_dropped`: its text was written around a button that no longer exists).
- Sonnet is sent without `strict` (Bedrock refuses it): a missing `say` is read from whether it wrote a text, and an
  id list written as one string is parsed as the JSON list it was meant to be (`nudges._id_list`; seen once).

**Typed offers: no button ever starts an agent** (2026-10-03, `yuki/memory/offers.py`,
`docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/06-proactive-do-it.md`; the user: "that is my work, I'll do it. The agent is not
supposed to work on my behalf unless I say it"). Until then an offer was one model-written sentence that the card's
Do it handed to the full-power agent as the user's own instruction; of the 13 offers written 29 Sept-2 Oct six were
the user's own work (T3 Code, PR replies, Slack reviews), and the one real click pulled T3 Code over Chrome and was
cancelled after 22 s, then recorded as "it was useful". Now an offer is a typed object
`{do, label, steps, refs, text, due}` whose `do` is a **closed capability list** (a product boundary like
`ACTION_TOOL_NAMES`, not a rule about words): `todo_add` (one item on the user's Yuki list; Undo ends it as a wrong
capture), `show_memory` (what Yuki holds about a thread, a call, a link, a know-how, shown in the card), `bring_back`
(open again one http(s) page the user already had - a link, or a thread's latest page; Undo brings back the window
that was in front), `draft` (a ready-written reply shown in the card with Copy; never typed or sent anywhere), `ask`
(a suggestion put **unsent** in the ask bar; nothing runs until the user presses Enter, and then it is their own
request with the strip and Stop). The judge picks the kind and the ids (`offer_do`, `offer_refs`, strict); the writer
keeps that kind or drops it and fills the rest (`offer`, `OFFER_SCHEMA`). Code validates twice - when the card is
written (`validate_offer`: the kind, steps, ids the request offered, refs that resolve to something to show or open,
the text and due a kind needs, never a `todo_add` for an item the list has) and at the click (`act`: the offer's
30-minute time, the refs re-resolved, the `[nudges] offers` setting, and a second press does nothing) - and the card
row carries the typed fields plus the fixed "Won't:" line per kind (`WONT_LINES`, UI copy) and `expires_at`. The
click is run by code: no model call, no agent (`MemoryClient.offer_act` / `offer_undo` / `offer_outcome`). Prompts
(`OFFER_RULES`, shared by the judge, the writer and the welcome back): offers only for Yuki's own job; never to do,
open, read, check, run or finish the user's own work (code, repos, files, documents, designs, reviews, tests, their
apps' sessions) nor to send, post or follow up a message - putting an item of it on their list, showing Yuki's notes
or drafting a reply someone is waiting on does not touch the work; none when another tool already gave the next step;
card text never names internal ids (titles instead; ids only in evidence, mentions and refs). `[nudges] offers =
"off"` makes cards inform only. A free-text offer stored before 3 Oct gets no button. Yuki's own words never become
the user's: a request that began with a Do-it bracket (before 3 Oct) or a card's suggestion sent unchanged
(`[Yuki's own suggestion ...]`) has no user words for the conversation extractor (`conversations.yuki_words_request`;
on 1 Oct an offer was stored as "The user replied to this card: ...", conversation fact 41).

**Budgets and feedback** (`yuki/core/attention.py`): per-kind day budgets - notice 3, stuck 3, recap 3, followup 2,
recall 3, thread 2 - and all of them together through `presence` (two an hour, 25 min apart); `presence_per_day`
(12) caps the day, `LOOK_CAPS` the model looks per kind (recap 4, stuck 6, notice 6, recall 6, followup 4, thread
3). Histories are loaded from the stored cards each pass (`Attention.load`), so a restart resets nothing. Less of
these halves that kind's budget per press over 14 days, an offer that ran and was kept takes one back (a press that
was then undone or stopped takes nothing back; `channel_budgets`; a kind at 0 is closed - the framework reads a 0
budget as none at all, so the worker closes it itself). **Offers are learned per (channel, kind)** over 14 days
(`offers.offer_scores`, numbers only): Less of these -1, Not now on a card with that kind of offer -0.5, a request sent
from an `ask` suggestion and stopped within a minute -1, Undo -1, closed unsent -0.5, ran and kept +1; a kind at -3 or
below loses its button on that channel until the signals age out (the card can still come as information). The models
read what really happened as COACH FEEDBACK (`card_feedback_lines`, `offers.feedback_lines`: "pressed ..., then Undo:
not wanted", "declined 3 bring_back offers ..."), never "it was useful" for a stop; `card_reaction` says it for
`accepted` and `less` too. Never a mute of the other kinds.

**The daily start** (the welcome back, `RESUME_SYSTEM_PROMPT`): its request now carries THREADS OF WORK (the threads
touched in the last 3 days, those of the last stretch before the gap marked; W ids as evidence); it may name the one
open thing that matters most today and end with one typed offer (`offer`, the same closed list; resume cards also
have Less of these). It never greets or says they
are back (the heading says Welcome back), and it must rest on something beyond NOW: a plain "Hey, welcome back."
(28 Sept 18:14, one run) is now `suppressed:ungrounded` - the user's "no generic lines". It still folds whatever fell
due; a call recap comes as its own card after it.

**Nothing vanishes unseen.** A notice not shown within `presence_deliver_min` (30) expires, but `recent_nudges`
keeps it (`missed: true`), so the overlay's and the window's history show it ("missed while you were away") and
nothing shows it as new.

**Evaluation** (the final code, replaying real days on a COPY of the memory - v22 copied 29 Sept 14:52, migrated to
24, threads backfilled up to 28 Sept 04:00 with the tidy and after it without, $1.14 - with real models; the store
answers as memory stood at each minute, `scripts/presence_replay.py`; coach, welcome back and questions included):

| Day | Cards in all | Observation cards (useful / moderate / noise) | Welcome back | Coach | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mon 28 Sept 08:00-00:30 (a full work day) | 8 | 4 (2 / 2 / 0) | 2 | 2 break nudges | $0.42 |
| Sun 27 Sept 08:00-00:30 (a crash, YouTube, two gaming calls) | 2 | 1 (0 / 1 / 0) | 1 | 0 | $0.13 |
| Tue 29 Sept 08:00-14:50 (meetings, away; a night of calls before) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | $0.10 |

28 Sept, the shipping code: 09:16 welcome back (the harness revamp's build failure still open, or the MCP server
since the pitch is close; offer to reopen it in Warp); 11:20 thread (a PR waiting since Friday on a colleague's
answers and a review; offer to check); 13:02 the coach's break nudge; 18:14 welcome back (a test
for a demo still open for today, offer to add it to the list); 18:27
follow-up (a relative's application day, from a chat of
24 Sept: personal, but not strictly the user's own event); 19:53 notice (a PR merged, another open, and a fix
needs a redeploy - the exact command; offer to draft the reply a colleague is waiting for and show it); 20:30
stuck (deploy-beta keeps failing on a credentials error, twice now; offer to open the failed run and show the logs);
20:57 the coach's break nudge. Held back, rightly: three social call recaps, the 11:16 merge of #2794 and the 19:50
opened PR (nothing next), a 21:32 repeat of the colleague's reply, a stale thread that was someone else's application,
a follow-up of a "review at 11 on Saturday" too vague to ask. The two coach nudges came at 45-46 min at the desk: the
coach's guard used `break_consider_min` (45); fixed the same day (the coach paragraph above): the replay after the fix
has one break nudge, 13:47, "You've been on the work project in Warp since 12:16, 1h31m straight - go stretch
for a bit." The follow-up named the wrong place: the event's fact has the relative's word, the next line of the same chat the user's
correction, and the portrait has the corrected name; the follow-up look now carries the rest of that conversation
and the closest portrait facts, the writer spells names as the data does (a correction wins), and the card after the
fix uses the corrected name and asks how it went (offer: draft a message to the relative and
show it). While the prompts were tuned the models varied run to run (six earlier 28 Sept runs gave
4-10 cards); the stuck card and the "merged, and there is a next step" notice came out in nearly all. Noise seen and
fixed on the way: restated finishes (the card-shape check), a WhatsApp chat tied to an old request (recall bars), a
card telling the screen back (offers required), "Hey, welcome back." (NOW is no thing), a graduation asked about on
the day the fact was written (events need a stated day), someone else's application (the thread guidance). The
replay's known leaks: threads' open items and the screen recall's index are today's, questions answered later read
as known.

**Cost**: 28 Sept $0.35-0.42 in all (observation looks ~$0.13-0.17, the coach $0.07, welcome backs $0.03, questions $0.02,
the event scans $0.10 - of which about $0.08 is the one-time catch-up of 7 days of facts; steady state 4-5 scans a day,
~$0.03). A look without a card is one Haiku call (~$0.007); a card adds Sonnet (~$0.02).

**Footprint** (the coach's worker ticking every 15 s through 28 Sept 08:00-24:00 on the replay store, stub models):
the presence pass adds 2.7 ms of CPU a tick on average (10 s over 16 h, ~0.02% of one core; most ticks read one
indexed row per detector and stop), no thread, no process, no GPU, no new embedding (it reuses the journal's stored
vectors and the screen recall's cache). Private bytes: +38 MB over the run, of which 33 MB is OpenBLAS's one-time work
buffer taken by the first matrix product (the recurrence check) - the live service already holds it (the screen
recall's index search allocates it) - so the net is a few MB, as without the pass (Python's own live allocations at
the end: ~1 MB, tracemalloc).

**The routine card's `run_skill` offer (phase R, 10 Oct 2026).** One more typed offer, written by code only: the
"Your usual" card of a learned skill with an active routine (see "Routines from moments and the 'Your usual' card"
below). `run_skill` is in `offers.CODE_ONLY_DOS`: no model-written card can carry it (the writers' schema and the
judge's enum are built from `MODEL_DOS`, `validate_offer` refuses it, `allowed_dos` returns it only for the routine
channel), the only writer is `RoutineLane._write` through `offers.routine_offer`, and `act` checks it again (its
skill, its routine, its own expiry) and runs nothing itself: it answers `running`, and the tray runs the skill through
the engine (`origin = "card"`, under the desk lease, the game read again per rung). It never sends a message and never
joins a call (the engine refuses a card-origin run whose end commits), never offers the user's work, and its Undo puts
back the window that was in front. States the window and the thread show: Running, Done, Didn't work (`failed`), Not
ready yet (`not_ready`: the skill has too few checked runs), Busy (`busy`).

## The revamp's memory modules (3 Oct 2026)

Seven modules and six migrations from the revamp (build reports `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/24`-`33`; the loop that uses them is in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "The loop, end to end"). Rules for all of them: tables are additive (migrations 26-31 at the end of this section); content is encrypted like every other column; model calls go through the portrait worker's `_call` (logged to `logs/memory/`, costed, response sealed) unless said otherwise; numbers are computed in code, and strings are only compared with stored strings (whole-word spans, quotes, ids; no word lists); nothing is hand-edited. Costs were measured on DB copies and offline replays; no live night has run yet.

| Module | Stores | Runs | Command |
|---|---|---|---|
| `contacts.py` | people, routes, route preferences | nightly after the portrait audit, and only if memory changed | `python -m yuki.memory.contacts` |
| `practice.py`, `practice_learn.py`, `practice_clean.py` | run traces, procedures, habits, pitfalls, style notes, an evidence ledger | traces on every request; `PracticeWorker` every 30 s; the miner nightly; the clean-up pass by hand | `python -m yuki.memory.practice_learn`, `python -m yuki.memory.practice_clean` |
| `checkins.py` | goals and their events, check-in questions | the card worker, every 15 s pass | `python -m yuki.memory.questions --revet` |
| `offers.py` | typed offers on card rows, what became of them | when a card is written and when its button is pressed | none |
| `brief.py` | the personal brief (in the portrait row), the personal-ness log | the nightly render; the judge nightly | `python -m yuki.memory.brief` |
| `recheck.py` | a corrected version of a weekly review; retired portrait facts | by hand, Yuki's own pipeline | `python -m yuki.memory.recheck` |
| `commitments.py` | ends to-dos that later evidence settles | the conversation worker, every pass (gated to every 30 min) | `python -m yuki.memory.conversations check-commitments` |

### People and routes (`yuki/memory/contacts.py`, migration 28)

- **Stores.** `contacts` (name key = HMAC of the casefolded name; name, relation, aliases with how each was seen or heard, tone, fact ids: encrypted), `contact_routes` (service in clear = URL host or app key, kind dm / group / channel, chat name, handle, the capture threads it spans, evidence journal ids; `contact_id` NULL for a group chat the user is in), `contact_prefs` (the user's lasting route choice with their quote). **No counts are stored**: they are read from `messages` by SQL when asked.
- **Written by** `Linker.run(kind="refresh" | "rebuild")`. It gathers the chat threads with a message in the last 60 days (at most 90, busiest first) with senders and counts both ways, person facts, aliases (including mishearings such as "Himanth") and the people call digests saw. One Sonnet call (effort low) groups threads into chats and people. `validate` checks the answer against stored strings: a name or alias is a whole-word span of a sender, chat name, alias, person fact or digest name; a relation is a cited person fact's own words; a DM is theirs; a group needs them writing there or a cited journal entry; a handle is a sender or the chat's name; never the user's own names. Ambiguous merges become questions through the questions pipeline. A rebuild retires every live row (kept with `ended_at`). The nightly refresh makes no call unless a message, person fact or alias was written or retired since the last run (by write time: a person fact's `valid_from` is its evidence date, often days before it was written).
- **Read side** (no model, SQL, a few ms): `people`, `resolve`, `named_in`, `rank_routes` (the live preference first, then the user's own messages with a 10-day half-life, a group scaled by the person's share; margin between the top two, a close call below 0.35), `card`, `samples` (the user's last own messages per route, decrypted at read time), `directory` (at most 900 characters), `rhythm` (messages a day for 14 days: both ways in a DM, only what they wrote in a group; days before Yuki first read a chat are `None`, not zero), `recent_chat_contacts`, `call_contacts`, `prefer_route`, `identify`, `tags_in`.
- **API.** `MemoryClient.contact(ref, near)`, `people_list(limit)`, `people_directory()` (a string), `prefer_route(ref, route, words)`, `frame_facts(words, said, screen, apps)`. Agent tool `about`.
- **Commands.** `python -m yuki.memory.contacts --rebuild | --refresh | --show [--dry-run] [--db PATH] [--log-dir DIR]`. `--show` prints private content: never paste it anywhere.
- **Cost.** One Sonnet call, $0.11-0.16 per link run. On the real copy: 61 chat threads → 19 people, 32-34 routes. Reads: `contact` 3 ms, `directory` 31 ms, `people_list` 51 ms with rhythm. Known: counts include `reread` rows (review M10); the window recomputes `people_list` every minute (M11).

### Practice memory (`yuki/memory/practice.py` + `practice_learn.py` + `practice_clean.py`, migration 29)

- **Stores.** `runs` (one row per step: tool, `app` as app key in clear, target / input / why / result encrypted, verdict, ms; step 0 is the request itself: the user's words, outcome, measured signals, the playbooks `injected` and `cited`, where learning stands; pruned at 30 days), `playbooks` (kind **procedure** | **habit** | **pitfall** | **style**; intent, apps, body, "instead", family, origin, sources; confidence and state derived; bi-temporal `valid_from` / `valid_to` / `superseded_by`), `playbook_vec` (intent vectors), `playbook_events` (the ledger: observed, observed_day, ran_ok, ran_trouble, taught, user_corrected, contradicted, injected, cited).
- **Written by** the agent's `_record_run` and the voice hands' `record_hand_trace`, both on the background writer; learned by `PracticeWorker` (thread `yuki-memory-practice`, 30 s tick): `Learner` (Haiku: distiller and post-mortem on measured triggers; every step cites trace steps that ran ok, a pitfall cites failed steps or the user's own words, a quote must be a span of the user's words, no window id or coordinate in any text; an ADD within cosine 0.85 of a live object of its kind supersedes it and takes its ledger), `Intake` (the old know-how rows become objects **on probation**: never shown, confidence capped at 0.35, until a run confirms them or the user presses Save), `Miner` (Sonnet, nightly after 22:00 with the user idle 10 min, no call, nothing full screen: routes from the people index, sites by dwell, Warp commands per folder, app surfaces; recurring 3+ times on 2+ days; unsure ones become teach-back questions, origin `practice`, answered later as `taught`).
- **Confidence is computed, never asked of a model**: `(S+1)/(S+F+2) × 0.5^(days since last evidence / half-life)`, half-life 60 days (procedure, pitfall), 180 (habit, style); weights observed 1, ran_ok 1, taught 3, ran_trouble -2, user_corrected -3, contradicted -3. States candidate (one source, shown "tentative, seen once") → active (two independent sources, or taught) → trusted (3 cited clean uses) → stale (past a half-life: "verify first") → retired (confidence under 0.30 with 2+ negative events, or forgotten).
- **Retrieval** (`MemoryClient.practice`, ~7 ms): the request's meaning against each object's intent with app names taken out of both sides; the apps in play are the apps named in the words (whole words of stored app names), the open windows and the caller's apps. Ways at or above 0.35 cosine when scoped to an app in play, else 0.45, ranked by cosine × confidence, at most 2 procedures and 1 habit or style note; the pitfalls of the apps in play always come, at most 3.
- **API.** `MemoryClient.record_run(run)`, `record_hand_trace(...)`, `practice(words, apps, open_apps)`, `workflows()` (the Playbook page: procedures, lessons, proposals with uses, last use, confidence), `workflow_edit(ref, action, words)` (save, edit in the user's words (re-distilled by the worker), disable, enable, forget; a `question:N` ref answers through the questions pipeline), `run_ledger(since, until, limit)` (the Activity page: each request with the user's words, acting steps, cited playbooks), `control_record(app, tool, control)` (expected-unverifiable lookup for the finish gate), `sent_messages(app, since)` (send confirmation).
- **Commands.** `python -m yuki.memory.practice_learn --intake | --learn | --mine [--force] | --tables | --show | --refresh | --stamp-successes [--dry-run] [--db PATH] [--log-dir DIR]`. `--show` and `--tables` print private content. `--refresh` recomputes states now (probation, decay) with no model call. `--stamp-successes` marks the ways learned from runs that were verified successes (the worker also does it daily). The clean-up: `python -m yuki.memory.practice_clean --dry-run [--save FILE] [--user-words TEXT ...] [--db PATH]` (decides, writes nothing, keeps the decisions encrypted in FILE; prints counts only), `--apply FILE` (writes exactly those, no new judgement), or neither (judge and write), `--undo RUN` (a pass's portrait run id, or `daily-YYYY-MM-DD` for a day's cap; once only). Run it with Yuki closed.
- **Only a success is learned** (appLearn, 4 Oct). A way (procedure or habit) is written from a run only when the run was a verified success: it ended done, its last action verified, nobody corrected it, and a post-mortem found it worked in the end; it gets a neutral `succeeded` event. A way learned from a run is shown (Playbook page, the brain's ways) only with `succeeded`, `ran_ok` or `taught`; the return card's Learned box and `workflows()["learned"]` only ever name `succeeded` ways of that run. A failed or stopped run keeps pitfalls only: quiet notes the brain reads for the apps in play, never on the Playbook page, the Activity rows or a card; what failed reaches the user only inside the later success (the way's `avoid`, "What didn't work"). A success after an earlier failed try of the same request (cosine 0.60, within 6 h) is distilled whatever its length.
- **Cleaning what is saved** (appClean, 4 Oct; `practice_clean.py`, report `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/59-build-appClean.md`). The user found too many near-identical Spotify notes under "How you do things" and a saved habit describing a way they would never take. A by-hand pass re-judges every live object (disabled ones are left alone): (1) **provenance, code only** (`practice.provenance`): a way stays only with the user's own word (Save, a teach-back, a quote), a success (`succeeded` / `ran_ok`, or its run's trace is a verified success the learner did not judge wrong) or what the user does (the miner); a way learned from runs none of which was a verified success (`from_failure`) or carried over from old notes and never confirmed (`unconfirmed`) is retired, never shown to a model; (2) **judgement**, one Sonnet call (effort low, worked examples, the notes grouped by app with where each came from, the user's words under a note and after its job, and any `--user-words` given to the pass): near-duplicates **merge** (the survivor is chosen by code: a verified success first, then a procedure with steps over a step-less habit, then clean uses, then the user's word over what is observed, so the one that worked stays and a saved habit never absorbs it; ways merge with ways, pitfalls with pitfalls; a saved plan's merge whose survivor changed since points at its current version, or is left out when it ended), a way the user said they do not want is retired (`unwanted`, only with a quote of their words, checked as a span; a saved way only with words given to this pass), or one the other notes show fails (`contradicted`, citing them) or that is no way (`not_useful`; never a lesson resting on the user's words); every kept procedure is **reshaped** to a short goal title and numbered steps (what the Playbook renders as title + 1, 2, 3; never more steps than it had and a title only when it had none, no 3+ digit number it did not carry, its avoid line kept unless rewritten; the executable `run` and slots kept); (3) **the pitfall cap**, code: at most `PITFALLS_PER_APP` = 4 live pitfalls per app (a request is shown at most 3), the weakest retired (no words of the user's, then confidence, evidence, newest evidence). The practice worker also applies the cap once a day with its refresh (run key `clean:daily-YYYY-MM-DD`, logged `practice_cap`), but only once a pass has been applied and not taken back since (checkpoint `practice_clean_state`: `applied:<run>` / `undone:<run>`), so it never runs before the reviewed pass has merged duplicates, and an undo lasts. Bi-temporal: retired and merged objects get `valid_to` (merged: `superseded_by` the survivor), a reshaped procedure a new version that takes its ledger and keeps its first-seen date; each with a neutral `cleaned` event under the run key `clean:<portrait run id>`; nothing is deleted. Its cost is a portrait run of kind `cleanup_practice` (not drawn from the learner's daily pools). Measured on a copy of the real memory (4 Oct, counts only): 37 live objects, one call, $0.07; 22 decisions: 8 never-confirmed old ways, 1 unwanted habit, 1 pitfall that taught nothing, 3 duplicates, 6 pitfalls past the cap retired, 3 procedures reshaped; Spotify 18 objects -> 5 (2 merged, 11 retired); undo restored the same 37.
- **Cost.** Distill or post-mortem $0.0045-0.011 a pass, intake $0.009, miner $0.03-0.04 a night. Two daily pools (local day): post-mortems their own $0.15 and 25 passes (3 Oct: the single shared $0.15 was spent by 19:09 and ten troubled runs were held; that day's 17 post-mortems cost $0.093); distills, intake and the miner share $0.15 and 20 passes. Past its pool's limit a run is `held` and learned within 2 days; reaching a limit is logged once a pool a day (`practice_budget` in the portrait log). Total at most $0.30 a day (plus at most one pass over each pool), the user's own Playbook edits not counted. No process of its own. Measured: the volume mistake was not repeated after one correction (a budget-matched control repeated it); procedures alone did not cut model calls on Warp. Known (review): the learner and intake are not gated during games or calls (L16), held runs are re-read every tick (L17; since appFix not while their pool is still spent), `playbook_events` is never pruned (L18).

### Check-ins and goals (`yuki/memory/checkins.py`, migration 30)

- **Stores.** `goals` (scope day | thread | project; the goal's text and the user's own words encrypted; source answer | call | commitment | thread + ref; status open | done | dropped | unknown; evidence ids, due, next and last check-in, count), `goal_events` (every change with the question it came from), and `questions.moment`, `goal_id`, `folded_at`, `reply_tone`. A check-in is a question of origin `checkin` on a card of kind `question` (trigger `checkin`, `ref` `question:N`).
- **Runs.** `CheckinWorker` inside `NudgeWorker.tick`, after the welcome back and the due reminders and before the observation cards; every gate of the card worker holds it, and so does an open call. The moments, budgets and learning are in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` ("Proactivity"). The worth-asking vet (`questions.vet`) runs on every check-in before its card is written, and every 6 h on every waiting question.
- **API.** `MemoryClient.goals(scope, statuses, today)`; the standing parts have a `goal` group (today's goals, then open work goals, 700 characters) that reaches every request in the commitments block; pending-nudge rows carry `moment`, which titles the card (Today, Your new thread, Following up, Tonight, Checking on you).
- **Commands.** `python -m yuki.memory.questions --revet [--dry-run] [--db PATH]` re-vets the open and asked questions with the worth-asking test. Settings in `privacy.toml` `[nudges]`: `checkins_enabled`, `checkins_per_day` (3), `checkin_gap_min` (90); `questions_per_day` went from 6 to 2; an attention channel `checkin` (3 a day, an hour apart).
- **Cost.** About $0.03 and 9 s a look (Sonnet writer and Sonnet vet, on the card worker's thread), 2-3 looks a day, so about $0.06-0.10 a day; a fold-in of an answer (Haiku) about $0.004. Model looks per kind per day are capped (day_start 2, followup 3, care 1, evening 2, new_thread 2). Known: detection runs a query burst on every 15 s tick (review L23).

### Typed offers (`yuki/memory/offers.py`; no new table)

Detailed contract: "Observation cards" above. **Stores**: the typed offer in the card row's `inputs.offer`, and `nudges.outcome` / `outcome_at` (migration 26: done, undone, prefilled, stopped, abandoned, expired). **API**: `MemoryClient.offer_act(nudge_id)`, `offer_undo(nudge_id)`, `offer_outcome(nudge_id, outcome)`; card rows carry the `offer` view (fields, `wont`, `undo`, `expires_at`, `target` or `items`). **Commands**: none; `[nudges] offers = "yuki_only" | "off"`. **Cost**: the offer's fields come from the judge and writer calls the card already makes; a click costs no model call. Learned per (channel, kind) from the weights in `offers.WEIGHTS`.

### The personal brief (`yuki/memory/brief.py`, migration 31)

Detailed contract: "The personal brief" above. **Stores**: the brief inside the portrait row (`portraits.sections_ciphertext`, key `_brief`) and `surfaced` (ids of brief lines and memory items per reply and card). **API**: `MemoryClient.brief()`, `brief_slice(consumer)`, `brief_for(consumer)` (a string; `thinker`, `talker`, `cards`), `record_surfaced(...)`, `activity_header(since, until)`. **Commands**: `python -m yuki.memory.brief --show | --surfaced | --judge` (`--show` prints private content). **Cost**: no call of its own; the nightly render grows by about 3.1k input and 1k output tokens, about +$0.025; the personal-ness judge is one Haiku call a night over at most 20 items.

### Keeping memory true (`yuki/memory/recheck.py`, `yuki/memory/commitments.py`)

- **`recheck`** re-checks what the weekly review and the portrait still state against current memory, after journal entries were withdrawn (re-journaled into nothing, or tainted). A portrait fact whose every cited entry was withdrawn is retired (INVALIDATE, with a diary line) and the page re-rendered. For a review, one Haiku call names each sentence resting on a withdrawn entry; code corrects a sentence only when every claim taken out rests solely on withdrawn entries, a whole sentence is quoted verbatim, nothing is added, and the open to-dos, loops and commitments memory holds now count as evidence that a claim is still true. The corrected review is stored as a new version of the same week (same `at` and `nudge_id`, so no new card), the old one marked superseded. **Command**: `python -m yuki.memory.recheck [--dry-run] [--week 2026-W39] [--render auto|now|flag|skip] [--db PATH]`. **Cost**: about $0.01 per review with withdrawn entries. **State**: the first version deleted a still-open to-do ("laminate the certificate for Bro"); fixed on 3 Oct (`c6fb863`, calibrated on the real embedder), not yet run on the real DB.
- **`commitments`** keeps the to-do list true. `close_mirrors` (no model): an open commitment of the other owner from the same source exchanges as a done one is closed with it; `close_joke_commitments` (no model): a call commitment whose own utterance the call's digest marked `humor` ends as a wrong capture. `CommitmentChecker.check` (one Haiku call, at most every 30 min and only when a journal entry or exchange is newer than the last check): the open commitments against the later journal (Yuki's own doing marked) and the later exchanges; a close must cite evidence dated after the commitment, jokes and uncertain entries never count, unsure stays open. Every close writes a true end note naming the evidence ids; nothing is deleted. **Run by** the conversation worker each pass. **Command**: `python -m yuki.memory.conversations check-commitments [--dry-run]` (also `reembed-turns`, which embeds the 115 old exchanges again from the user's words alone after migration 27). **Cost**: about $0.011 a check. On 3 Oct it closed 3 dead to-dos on the real DB.

### Migrations 26-31 (one line each; older code opens a newer `user_version`)

| v | Adds |
|---|---|
| 26 | `calls.hold_since`, `calls.last_alive_at` (one call = one session, restart adoption); `nudges.outcome`, `nudges.outcome_at` (what became of an offer) |
| 27 | `threads.ident_key`, `threads.merged_into`; `conversation_turns.envelope_ciphertext` (the user's words stored apart from Yuki's notes); `portraits.sections_ciphertext` (the portrait's 8 fields, each with a budget); a Python step keys every thread by service + chat and merges split threads (8 merges on a copy; nothing deleted, `merged_into` records the target) |
| 28 | `contacts`, `contact_routes`, `contact_prefs` |
| 29 | `runs`, `playbooks`, `playbook_vec`, `playbook_events` |
| 30 | `goals`, `goal_events`, `questions.moment` / `goal_id` / `folded_at` / `reply_tone` |
| 31 | `surfaced` |
| 32 | two indexes: `journal(batch_id, thread_id)`, `messages(thread_id, first_seen)` (`35-build-review-fixes.md`) |
| 33 | shown forms, the pin, reviews, route `use`, `day_digests` (see "What the app shows, said to the user" below) |

The Store section above stops at migration 24 and 25 (`call_digests.context_ciphertext`). The real DB is at 27 as of the Phase A install (3 Oct ~05:00); 28-31 apply on the first start of the next build.

**After installing a build that adds a migration** (each step with a backup first; the sqlite backup API is safe while Yuki runs; none of these edits memory by hand):

1. Back up `memory.db` and `memory.key` to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\memory\backup-<date>-<name>`.
2. Start once; check `PRAGMA user_version` (31) and `PRAGMA quick_check` on a read-only connection.
3. `python -m yuki.memory.contacts --rebuild`, then `--show` (the brief's people and the miner's routes come from it; the first nightly run does it anyway).
4. `python -m yuki.memory.practice_learn --intake --dry-run`, then `--intake`, then `--refresh` (the migrated know-how rows go on probation).
5. `python -m yuki.memory.questions --revet --dry-run`, then `--revet` (the waiting questions through the worth-asking test).
6. `python -m yuki.memory.recheck --dry-run --week 2026-W39`, then without `--dry-run`.
7. `python -c "from yuki.memory.api import MemoryClient; MemoryClient.open().rerender_portrait()"`, then `python -m yuki.memory.brief --show`.
8. Restart the memory service and the tray (not in a game or a call).

Already done on the real DB at the Phase A install: `conversations reembed-turns`, `conversations check-commitments` (3 closed), `repair_work_thread_dates` (4 repaired).

## What the app shows, said to the user (3 Oct feedback, migration 33)

Why: after using the installed app the user found memory talking about them in the third person with ISO dates ("On 2026-09-25 from about 02:00 ... the user's relative ... they"), a pin that could not be turned off, "Maybe misheard" answers that took a model pass and came back, `route:24` as "Usually", the same person paragraph twice, "Heard in Discord" without the channel, 137 long facts, and no memory between days' chats. Root causes: `docs/research/revamp-2026-10-02/38-feedback-root-causes.md`; this build: `43-build-appA-memory.md`.

**Two forms, one call.** Memory keeps every item in its own third-person form: about 15 prompts read "the user" and the agent reading "you work at X" would confuse the user with itself. Each user-facing item also gets a **shown** form, written by the same model call that writes the item, under one shared rule, `yuki.memory.titles.SHOWN_RULES` (second person; others by name or "your friends", never "they" for a person not named; natural dates "on 25 September", the year only when not this one, never relative words; 12-hour times with AM/PM; for something said on a call, who was on it, or "on a call with other friends" when the person the item is about was not on it; nothing added). Written by: the portrait operations (`shown` next to `summary`, so the nightly, weekly, refresh, fold, audit and routines passes all write it), the threads tidy (`shown` on merge and update; a journal batch's new state clears the old one), the call digest (`gist_shown`; the memory gist is now about "the user" with the others named), the correction pass (a rewritten fact's shown form). **The titles pass** (`Titler`, idle, every 5 min) fills what is missing: facts, thread states and gists, one forced Haiku call per 25, each fact line with the calls it rests on and who was on them (`titles.calls_of_entries`: the journal row's `source.call` joined to the digest's context). The portrait operations see the same: a journal line said on a call shows `[call: Discord, #channel voice (Server) call on Wednesday 30 September, 9:06 PM, with Ana, Sam]`. Validation is plumbing (whole, at most 1,800 characters); the voice is the model's judgement, tested over a recorded dry run (`tests/fixtures/shown_sample.json`), never filtered at run time.

**Structured call place.** The digest tool now also returns `place: {app, kind: voice|text|meeting|direct, channel, server}` (each name kept only when the context it read shows it), stored in the same context JSON; `titles.place_label` formats it ("Discord, #late-night voice (Ana's server)"). Older digests keep their free-text `where`.

**The user's verdicts, direct.** No model on any click path; a "wrong" queues the correction pass for the ripple in the background (as Forget always did), and an Undo within the window takes the verdict back and cancels the pass while it has not started.
- **Pin** (`MemoryClient.pin_fact(ref, on)`): `portrait_facts.pinned`, its own flag (set for every user-origin fact by the migration and for every new one, so nothing changed visibly). A pinned fact changes only on a later correction or later evidence; dreaming, the loop expiry and the withdrawn-evidence check leave it alone. `origin == "user"` keeps meaning "you told me". A new version keeps the pin and the review.
- **Maybe misheard** (`misheard()`, `resolve_heard(ref, right|wrong|undo)`): uncertain journal entries of the last 7 days not answered (`journal.reviewed_at`), newest first, 8 listed with the total, each with `heard_in` (the voice channel from the digest), `channel_known` and who the call screen showed. Right turns the entry into a fact in place (a classification the user confirmed; nothing reworded); Wrong retires it now (superseded by a no-model batch, kept). Undo takes back only an answer that was given; an Undo of a Wrong cancels its queued correction pass first, and once that pass has started nothing is restored (`ok` False, "Too late to undo"; the window keeps the item removed).
- **To review** (`review_queue()`, `review_mark(ref, right|wrong|notmine|undo)`): Yuki's own unpinned facts of the last 14 days not reviewed (`portrait_facts.reviewed_at`), ranked by importance x (1 - confidence), at most 5 a day (answers count against it, counted by distinct answer time so a later version of an answered fact is not a second answer), flagged `unsure` (confidence <= 0.6) or `new`; with the count of facts dreaming merged in the last 7 days ("I merged N similar facts"). Right reviews and pins; Wrong / Not mine retire now; Undo as for Maybe misheard.

**People.** The linking call writes a `use` sentence per route (at most 12 words, said to the user, from their own lines there; `contact_routes.use_ciphertext`) and copies only the relationship words ("father", never "the user's father"). `card()` / `people_list()` return `usual` as one plain line ("Slack DM. Code questions go on the PR.", or "Discord DM · @handle" before the `use` sentence is written; never a `route:N`, and a string so a window that prints it as it is never shows an object) and `usual_route` as the label object (`{"ref", "label": "Discord DM", "sub", "use", "mine", "theirs", "last_at", "tone", "preferred"}`; `usual_ref` keeps the ref), `relation` from the person fact's short label, and **one** `summary` per person (`{"ref", "headline", "text", "pinned"}`); `contact()` lists only the other facts, in their shown form. A person fact written after the last link run attaches by its subject. The agent's card text keeps memory's form.

**A new chat every day** (`yuki/memory/daydigest.py`, table `day_digests`). One Haiku call per user day (04:00 to 04:00, about $0.005) writes a short digest from the day's session summaries, thread events, commitments made and done, call gists and check-in goals: `text` (for Yuki), `shown` (the line at the top of the next day's chat) and up to three `open` items with their threads. Written at the scheduler's first check of each user day (the 04:00 rollover, or the service's start: the nightly slot at 22:00 comes before the day is over, so it can only cover the days before), in the nightly pass for the completed days without one, and lazily when `day_connector()` or `resume_context()` finds yesterday missing (they write the `day_digest` flag; the portrait scheduler builds it in the background). A day whose call fails is tried at most 3 times per service run; a digest saved without its shown form is retried once. `resume_context()` falls back to the last digest and the open promises (at most 1,000 characters) when the last exchange is older than its window, so the first request of a day knows yesterday and says the same as the page; it names the digest's day and, when that is not yesterday, never calls it the last day (and says when yesterday's, a day they talked with Yuki, is not written yet). `chat_days()` and `turns_on(day)` give the window its day folds. **Command**: `python -m yuki.memory.daydigest --backfill --days 7 [--db PATH] [--dry-run]`.

**Dreaming tuned** to the measured embedder: look-alike pairs from cosine 0.60 (was 0.80, above the 0.77 maximum ever seen: the list was always empty), a behaviour and a routine may merge, 16 changes a night (was 8), one-off dated events idle 14 days and more are marked for the model, and the portrait operations are told a "continued on 2 Oct" day is an UPDATE, never a new fact.

### Migration 33

`portrait_facts.shown_ciphertext`, `.pinned` (set for current user-origin facts), `.reviewed_at`; `work_threads.shown_ciphertext`; `call_digests.gist_shown_ciphertext`; `journal.reviewed_at`; `contact_routes.use_ciphertext`; table `day_digests(day PK, text_ciphertext, shown_ciphertext, refs, model, cost_usd, created_at, updated_at)`. Additive only.

**After installing it** (backups first, as above): `python -m yuki.memory.titles --backfill --what shown --dry-run --db <copy>` then on the real DB (about $0.18 for ~280 items on the 3 Oct copy); `python -m yuki.memory.contacts --rebuild` (the `use` sentences, about $0.14); `python -m yuki.memory.daydigest --backfill --days 7` (about $0.03). The titles pass would fill the shown forms by itself within hours of idle time; the backfill only makes it immediate.

## Recording how you work: the action trace (capC1, 7 Oct 2026; migration 38)

Report 78 phase C1, in shadow: nothing reads the trace to change Yuki's behaviour yet. Build report 96. Off until the user turns on **Settings → Privacy and pause → Record how I work** (`[trace] record_actions`; off for a new install); the tray's pause pauses it; `--no-trace` keeps the service from ever starting it.

- **What one row is** (`ui_actions`): the time, the app (`process`), the page (`page_key`, from the timeline) and what was on screen (`capture_id`, the app's latest capture at button-down), the kind (click, dclick, rclick, drag, scroll, keys, type, hover, menu, invoke, select, copy, paste, file_open, file_save, switch, nav, command), the element by role, keyed digests and position, the shortcut or named key (`chord`; typing is only a count, `typed`), the clipboard's sequence number and format names (never content), a file's path encrypted, the outcome class and machine-checked facts (`toggle_state`, `selected`, `page_reached`, `window_verified`, `field_submitted` as a length, `file_saved` / `file_opened`, `command_ok`), the source (`user` hardware, `user_injected` untagged injected input such as Handy, Deskflow or G Hub, `yuki` stamped or matched to Yuki's run trace) and whether a Yuki run was in progress (a `user` action then is a takeover), the pointer's `travel_px` and `gap_ms` since the previous action, a hover's `dwell_ms`, a drag's end element (`end_*`), and the episode once its window is final.
- **Names by control type** (`yuki.memory.trace_privacy`): command controls (Button, CheckBox, RadioButton, ComboBox, SplitButton, toolbar items) keep their name encrypted through the secret filter; menu items, tabs, titles, documents and panes keep a digest and position, with the encrypted name on the raw row only until the same digest was used on **3 distinct days** (then `ui_elements` keeps it); content controls (list items, text, edit fields, links) keep a digest and their container's, never a name.
- **The name rule, nested documents** (phase F, `fPrivacy`, `NAME_RULE = "2"`): a Document or Pane nested in a list item, a list, a tree, a data grid, another Document or a Group (`nested_content`, the parent or any kept ancestor) is content: a chat app's message bubble (Discord, Slack) keeps a digest, never a name; a top-level document or pane (a page title, an app pane) keeps the title rule. One function serves the live capture (counter `content_document_dropped`) and a one-time scrub of the names stored under the old rule (`trace_store.scrub_names_once`, checkpoint `source_checkpoints['trace.name_rule']`, a short-lived thread at the service's start that waits while a game runs, logged `trace_name_scrub` with counts; 29 rows on the 10 Oct copy: 25 decided by the parent, 4 by the recovered ancestry; an ancestry not recovered is looked at again at the next start and cleared at the third try). CLI: `python -m yuki.memory.trace_store scrub-names [--dry-run] [--db PATH]` (a real run refuses the live database).
- **Input**: presence's one Raw Input reader (`yuki.memory.userpresence`) feeds the trace's sink (`yuki.memory.trace_input`): Raw Input registration is one per process, so the trace never registers its own. Keys become a class in the handler (named key, chord, or a count of one character; AltGr via `ToUnicodeEx` with `wFlags=0x4`; Alt+numpad is typing). Moves are never stored: a rest is Windows' own hover rectangle and time (`SPI_GETMOUSEHOVER*`), so the user's vibrating mouse (about 23 reports a second of 0-1 px) is a rest and costs a few additions per report.
- **Window events** (`yuki.memory.trace_hooks`): low-rate events of the app in front (invoked, selection, menus, dialog, focus, the window's title); outcome events (state, value, shown, hidden, reordered) only from an action to 1.5 s after it, at most 200 per action, never location changes; the clipboard listener; the Recent folder, with open/save dialogs (a file from a dialog is that app's, `via=dialog`) and Ctrl+S without a dialog (FL Studio's usual save: the files opened or saved lately are re-checked by their own write time, `via=save_chord`).
- **Identity at button-down** (`yuki.memory.trace` worker, its own thread and UI Automation client): the event path, then `ElementFromPoint` asked at button-down, then a bounded descent in a renderer's pane, else point-only. A change that could alter what is under the press before the answer marks the row `stale` (decided per action as events come; narrowed to machine facts by capC1c, below). Up to 5 changed elements by role, parent role and position in the outcome, with spare lookups only. At most 4 lookups a second.
- **The game gate obeys** (`yuki.memory.gamegate`; since phase F a game **in front**, see "Phase F" below): while it holds, the sink drops input at its first line (inside the gate's own change callback, not at the next beat), the per-app hooks come off and no lookup runs; presence keeps its registration (unregistering would make it deaf in games). With a game behind another window the trace runs, but never reads or hooks the game's own windows (`lookup_refused_game`, `hooks_refused_game`). The learned game set is in `ui_game_processes` with its evidence.
- **Privacy**: blocked apps, private windows, blocked sites, Yuki's own windows and the leave-out list give no row; a password field gives nothing, and typing into a field that could not be checked (or whose IsPassword could not be read) is dropped (fail closed); a typing run that begins right after Tab, Enter or a click is read again once the app has moved the focus, and a run a focus event lands on within half a second is dropped. Nothing from a span while recording was off, paused, in an off hour or gated becomes a row later (Recent links, Warp commands by their own time). Files in `[trace] blocked_folders` are never noted. Logs are counts and codes; an exception logs its type and line only.
- **FL Studio projects** (decision D6, `yuki.memory.flp`): a project opened or saved gives its name, tempo, instrument and effect plugin names, channel names, and pattern names and count, encrypted in `ui_files`, linked to the file action. Never audio or sample content; malformed or huge files fail closed.
- **Warp**: the history reader hands its kept (secret-filtered) commands to the trace as `command` rows with their exit code.
- **Retention**: raw rows 30 days (decision D4), elements unused for 60 days; `MemoryClient.trace_forget(since=, until=, app=)` forgets an hour or an app with what was learned from it (diary line in `memory_changelog`, pass `trace`; undo with `trace_forget_undo`; the forget's before-image goes with the 30-day purge). Episode ids are attached by walking final episodes from a cursor (`nudge_state` `trace:episode_cursor`).
- **Health**: `trace` in the 10-minute stats line and `nudge_state` `trace:health`; the doctor's "Learning how you work" line; `yuki trace` prints the last hour and day as counts.
- **Acceptance tools**: `scripts/trace_acceptance.py --checklist` (the 30-minute supervised session), the analyzer on a database copy (with a typed-marker scan of every column and the logs), and `[trace] ab_hours = true` with `scripts/trace_ab_report.py` for the on/off cost hours. Both read the installed service's logs (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\logs`) and stop when no memory logs are found.

## What the trace teaches: go-tos and the user's own fixes (capC1b, 7 Oct 2026; no migration)

Report 78 phase C1b, build report 97: the first value from the action trace, no segmentation. Nothing new is recorded; it all reads `ui_actions` / `ui_elements` (migration 38). With recording off, nothing here has anything to read.

- **Go-tos and chords per app** (`yuki/memory/trace_habits.py`): per app, the elements the user acts on most and the shortcuts they press, counted by code. Only `source` `user` / `user_injected` (never `yuki`); an element only from a row whose identity was taken (`resolved` point, event, snapshot or focus; never `stale` or `unresolved`) and of an acting kind (clicks, menus, selections, invokes, drags, typing and pasting into a field; never hovers or scrolls); a chord only with Ctrl, Alt or Win. Kept incrementally: `update()` folds the rows since its cursor (at most 5,000 a call) into decayed counts (half-life 14 days), capped per app (60 elements, 30 chords) and overall (60 apps), dropped after 60 days unused, held as one small encrypted `nudge_state` value per app (`trace:habits:app:` + a keyed digest of the process, never its name in the clear) plus an index (`trace:habits`), so a tick reads and rewrites only the apps its new rows are in; the practice worker calls it on its 30-second tick (one indexed read when nothing is new). A forget or its undo (a `memory_changelog` line of pass `trace`) makes the next update rebuild from the rows left.
- **Shown to the brain**: `practice.relevant()` (so `MemoryClient.practice()`) gains `go_tos`: for the first 3 apps in play (the apps the words name, then the caller's, then the open windows) the top 5 elements and 3 chords; `detail=True` gives 6 apps, 10 and 8. Names only from the element registry (a command control's on first use, a menu, tab or title name after 3 distinct days, a content control's never); others show as role and position ("an unnamed ListItem in a List"); an element the registry no longer has is left out. The thinker's task frame gets one line per app ("in chatter the user usually presses Ctrl+K (4 times); goes to Button "Send" (5 times)"), last in its body so it is the first to give way to the 2,500-character frame cap. The talker's lines (400 characters of lessons) and the tool schemas (the 39,000-character guard) do not change. `MemoryClient.go_tos(apps, detail=)` reads them directly (not counted as served). `MemoryClient.practice(go_tos=False)` leaves them out and does not count them; the talker's lookups pass it (it never shows them).
- **The online correction learner** (`practice_learn.CorrectionLearner`, on the practice worker's tick): it watches the runs of the last 10 minutes (plus 2 hours of catch-up) that failed (ended stopped or failed, last action failed or not verified, or an opening that did not show its app), that the learner judged corrected or failed, or in which the user's own input came during the run in one of its apps between the start of the step the run is judged by and the start of the next step that worked (or the run's end): a takeover. A run its own trace verified, not judged wrong, teaches nothing at all, a takeover included. It works out the run's own target from the step it is judged by - its latest failed or wrong step, else its last action, never a state the run itself already verified (no target, nothing learned) - by machine fields (a window of the app it opened, the page it opened, the control it acted on changed - by the element keys of Yuki's own trace rows or the role and name digest - or the file it saved) and looks for the user's own trace row that reached it (`reaches`): only a row that could cause that state - a window: a switch row into that app whose causing input (the user's latest action within 2 s) was a press or a shortcut, or a press or shortcut in the shell or that app whose own outcome brought it up, never typing, a scroll or a hover elsewhere that overlapped a slow window; a page: the page-change row written after a press or shortcut, never after a scroll or typing while Yuki's page loaded; a control: a press, shortcut, typing or pasting on it, never a hover, scroll or drag. Never Yuki's own state (appOpen's rules on trace facts): within 5 s of the run's first action while it ran, or an app whose process (`proc_started`, or its kept launch chain) Yuki's own opening started, unless the user pressed something in the shell right before. Reached: one Haiku call (`FIX_PROMPT`, tool `record_fix`, the post-mortems' budget pool) picks the essence of the user's route (detours left out); code checks every step cites one of the user's own actions shown (never `yuki`, never `stale`), ending with the one that reached it, builds the executable steps from the rows (a named command control -> click by role and name; a chord or named key -> keys; a window brought to the front -> `open_app` of that program; typing -> `type` only with a slot; a page change -> `open_url` only when the way has no slot and the timeline kept that page's address (`page_url`: the stretch with the same page key, a web page, not withheld); a content control, a page change without an address, a right click or a drag stay hints) and writes through `apply`. Not reached within 10 minutes: dropped, no model call. Gate: never while memory is paused, in a call or with something full screen; no idle wait (the user is busy right after their own fix).
- **What it writes**: the way for that narrow target, origin `fixed`, with a `user_fixed` ledger event (weight 3, a success event, state `active` at once: decision D3; `[trace] learn_fix_after_failure` other than `at_once` writes `demonstrated` instead, a candidate). `body.trace` keeps the cited action ids. Yuki's failed route becomes a pitfall with `body.route` (`app`, its step as a skill step, the element keys of Yuki's own rows): a procedure taking that very step ranks 0.6 lower in `relevant()` (`lowered`), an element with those keys ranks lower among the go-tos; never removed, never a block. The same route failing again adds an `observed` event to that pitfall instead of a second one, whether or not the user then fixes it (no model call); from 2 runs it carries `warning: "strong"` and `repeats`, and the frame says "STRONG WARNING ... still allowed". No pitfall, and no repeat, when the user's own actions take that same step (the same element key, the same named control or the same keys in that app); the repeat is counted only once the user's route is known (their fix, or the 10 minutes over). A route lowers only ways for its own app (the way's apps, or a step pinning it), never a way learned from the user's own actions. A fix read from the trace never supersedes a way the user taught (it is added beside it). A preference stated in the user's words ("not that playlist, the liked one") becomes a habit with their quote (`taught`), on the Playbook page and undoable there. One way per run: the appOpen re-check skips a run with a `user_fixed` event, and the fixer one with `demonstrated`. The run's signals get `trace_fix` (`{"at", "result"}`) once looked at. A run still waiting for the practice learner keeps its `learn` state: its post-mortem sees the fix (judged `corrected`, so what was shown or cited on it counts against; with no trigger it is judged failed in code); a run judged before is judged failed then, never one its own trace verified.
- **Health**: `nudge_state` `trace:learn` (every 10 minutes: ways from fixes, route pitfalls and how many failed again, go-tos served today - that count is `nudge_state` `trace:go_tos_served`, `{"day", "n"}`, added to by the Yuki app's process where go-tos are served, at most every 30 s); the doctor's "Learning how you work" line adds them in plain words, with the watchdog's skips.
- **The hung-app watchdog** (`yuki.memory.trace_uia.HungWatch`, in the trace worker): before a lookup, Windows' `IsHungAppWindow` on the target window (sends nothing); after one, a lookup that took 1 s or more marks its process. Before an MSAA call (`AccessibleObjectFromEvent`, no timeout of its own) the window must also take a `WM_NULL` within 50 ms: an app frozen for less than the 5 s Windows waits before calling it hung is marked at once (`hung_unanswered`). A marked process gets no lookups (its actions are point-only, as over budget) and is asked again at most every 2 s (`IsHungAppWindow`, then `WM_NULL` with `SMTO_ABORTIFHUNG`, 50 ms at most); the first answer clears it. Counters `lookup_skipped_hung`, `hung_marked`, `hung_slow`, `hung_skipped`, `hung_recovered`, `hung_now` in `trace:health`. The queue stays bounded by `QUEUE_MAX`.

## Capture quality from the first live recording (capC1c, 7 Oct 2026; no migration)

Build report 99. The first live recording (7 Oct, 8 minutes, 137 actions) showed correct taskbar clicks marked stale (Explorer 8 of 12), clicks in web pages and Electron apps resolved to unnamed containers that then filled the go-tos, plain clicks recorded as drags, and hovers on unnamed elements. Fixed by machine facts; nothing new is stored but two keys in a row's `facts`.

- **Staleness** (`trace.TraceWorker._stale`): the sink takes the top-level window under the press at button-down (`WindowFromPhysicalPoint` + `GetAncestor(GA_ROOT)`, a few microseconds, no UI Automation; the `down` item's 9th field, with that window's process as the 10th, read as the rest of the trace reads a window's process: for a UWP frame the hosted app's, a frame showing only its host 0, `trace._window_pid`). A row is `stale` only when: a structure event (shown, hidden, reordered, menu end) arrives while the lookup was answering and its own window (`GetWindowRect` of its handle) covers the point, unless the event is about the text caret or the cursor (`idObject` `OBJID_CARET` / `OBJID_CURSOR`: a click into a field shows its caret at once; counted `structure_not_content`); for an element whose own window is unknown, the top-level window under the point when `ElementFromPoint` answered (read right then, `Element.root`, before the walk and the ancestry) is not the one pressed; the element's own top-level window (its first native handle, from itself or its ancestors) is not the one pressed; or its bounds no longer hold the point. A window coming to the front is never staleness by itself: after the press it is the click's outcome (a taskbar click's app, a thumbnail or tooltip shown elsewhere). When the pressed window was already covered or gone by the time the worker handled the press, there is no lookup: the row is its own app's (its own gates; a gone window's app from the process the sink read at the press), `resolved='stale'` and point-only; only a gone window whose process the sink could not read gives nothing (`skip_window_changed`). Counters `stale_structure`, `stale_root_changed`, `stale_bounds`, `stale_other_window`, `stale_window_changed`, `stale_window_gone` in `trace:health`.
- **The resolution walk** (`trace_uia.TraceUia.refine`, inside `at_point(refine=True)`): an answer that is an unnamed container type (Group, Pane, Document, Custom, Window) is walked. Actionable patterns are Invoke, Toggle, SelectionItem, ExpandCollapse and Value (UI Automation's own `Is...PatternAvailable`, read with the cached facts), Value only when `ValueIsReadOnly` reads false: every Chromium page Document has a read-only Value, its address. One exposing none is walked to the nearest ancestor with an actionable pattern or a name that still holds the point (never one covering half the window or more), else the smallest descendant under the point with a name or a pattern. One that is actionable but unnamed (Chromium and Electron wrap most named controls in clickable unnamed Groups) gets the smallest named descendant under the point, else the nearest named ancestor, and stays itself when neither exists; a renderer descent ending on an unnamed container keeps the nearest named element on its way down. A renderer's whole pane gets its descent inside the same lookup (before, a second lookup that needed spare budget and was often skipped). One token, one hung-app check, 75% of the lookup's 150 ms for the walk, the rest for the chosen element's ancestry. C1's privacy rules apply to whatever is chosen. An event that names only an unnamed container no longer replaces a current point answer that is a real control. The row keeps the element's patterns as `facts.patterns`; counters `walk_up`, `walk_down`, `walk_descent`, `walk_path` (a container that became a real control by that path), `walk_container_left` (a click whose answer is still an unnamed container, whatever the walk tried), `event_container_kept_point`.
- **Go-tos** (`trace_habits.low_info`): an element that would be shown with no name and exposes no actionable pattern is never shown as a usual control; its tally is still kept. Rows from before capC1c (no `facts.patterns`) are judged by control type (a container type counts as not actionable). Every tally of the app is a candidate, so low-information ones never push real ones out of the top.
- **Drags** (`trace_input.Metrics.is_drag`): a drag only when the cursor at the release (physical pixels, never the device's raw counts) is outside Windows' drag rectangle around the cursor at the press, `SM_CXDRAG` x `SM_CYDRAG` as `GetSystemMetricsForDpi` answers it for the monitor of the press (Windows does not scale this metric: 4 x 4 at every DPI on this PC, so more than 2 px; without that call the system values, unscaled), read once per press. This is deliberately not `DragDetect`'s rule (which also fires when the cursor left the rectangle and came back): a wobble that comes back before the release is a click, and the resting jitter never leaves the rectangle. The trade-off, a drag released where it began (a knob turned and let go at its start), is a click that keeps how far the cursor went as `facts.came_back_px` (counter `click_came_back`; the `up` item's optional 8th field). A drag row keeps `facts.moved_px`.
- **Hovers**: walked like clicks (spare budget only); a hover that still has no name and no actionable pattern (an unnamed Group, List or Window) is not stored (`skip_hover_container`).
- **Measuring**: `scripts/trace_quality.py --copy-real --since HH:MM --until HH:MM` prints, from a backup copy (deleted after), counts only: clicks per app by resolution and element class, stale clicks followed by a window coming up, drags with press lengths and `moved_px`, hovers by class, the counters above, and the go-tos with and without the ranking rule.
- **Electron**: Discord, Slack and T3 Code expose their web content trees to Yuki's UI Automation client with Windows' screen-reader flag off (measured read-only 7 Oct: hundreds of named elements each); no app or launch flag was changed.

## Seeing the user finish things (capQ2a, 9 Oct 2026; no migration)

Build report 103, from a read-only audit of a day of the user's own trace (8 Oct): 8 of 12 tasks had no checked ending because sends and FL Studio saves were not seen; correct taskbar clicks were still stale; hovers came from a drifting mouse; clicks on the other computer (Deskflow) were recorded; the crash log filled with handled COM errors; sidebar threads drawn as Buttons reached the registry by name. All fixed by machine facts; nothing new is stored but counters.

- **Staleness, narrowed again** (`trace.TraceWorker._before_answer`): a structure change counts only when it came before the lookup's answer was *settled* (`Element.chosen_at`, stamped once `ElementFromPoint` and the resolution walk are done; the ancestry and index read afterwards never change which element it is), or when Windows dates it before that (the WinEvent's own `dwmsEventTime`, now carried in the hook items: `("ev", ..., tick)`, `("out", ..., tick, gen)`; the tick count is compared modulo 2^32). A taskbar redrawing itself while the ancestry is read is no longer staleness; a list reordering, a dialog appearing or a menu closing before the answer still is. Review F2: a change heard after the answer but dated by Windows before the press (a hover redraw delivered late) does not count either; the press's tick is the answer's less the worker-clock time between them, less one step of the tick count (`trace_hooks.tick_step_ms`, the clock interrupt interval from `GetSystemTimeAdjustment`). Second verify: a change dated before the press never counts, however soon it was heard (a late hook thread delivering the shell's hover redraw between the press and the answer included); items without their own tick are judged as before.
- **Text fields and sends** (`trace_uia.Element.field_hint`, `TraceUia.field_state`, `trace.TraceWorker._submit_check`): a field is an editable Value (not on a press control: Button, CheckBox, RadioButton, SplitButton, Hyperlink, MenuItem, TabItem), a TextPattern whose document range reads `IsReadOnly` false, or a keyboard-focusable Edit or Document that reads read-only nowhere. Chromium and Electron composers drawn as Groups count (measured read-only on 9 Oct: T3 Code's composer is a focusable Group with an editable TextPattern and no Value; its empty state reads one newline). After Enter (with or without modifiers) following typing or a paste into a field, the field is read again once, from the clicks' budget: `field_submitted` when it holds no visible character (Unicode whitespace, control and format characters such as a zero-width mark are not counted), the length taken from the Value, else from at most 256 characters of the TextPattern. A read-only document never is a field; a password field (or one whose IsPassword cannot be read) is never read at all, not even its length. Counters `field_submitted`, `submit_not_field`, `submit_check_skipped`.
- **FL Studio saves and opens** (`yuki/memory/trace_flrecent.py`): FL Studio's own recent-projects list, `HKCU\Software\Image-Line\FL Studio <n>\MRU` (numbered string values, `0` the latest; the version key Windows wrote last), read-only and lazily (the key's write time first; values read again only when it changed; at most 64 entries). On Ctrl+S in FL Studio (`FL64.exe` / `FL.exe`) its first 8 projects are re-checked by their own write time (`via=save_chord`); 1.5 s after FL Studio's window title changes, a new first project is a `file_open` (or a `file_save` when the file was written with the list; `via=fl_recent`) unless the list was rewritten while the trace was blocked (`fl_before_open`). One save or open seen through two doors (the chord, the list, a dialog's Recent link) is one row. The `.flp` reader (decision D6) runs on each.
- **Hovers only while the user is here**: a rest becomes a hover only when presence's own rule (`userpresence.PresenceInput`: the takeover distance within its window, from the mouse's own reports) saw a real mouse movement since the movement that ended in it began, or (second verify) heard a click, key or wheel since the pointer's previous rest began, so a short nudge right after a click is kept; presence that cannot say keeps none (`skip_hover_away`). The cost: the first rest a drifting mouse makes after the user's last input passes (one per absence; on a copy of the 8 Oct day, 1 of 62 away hovers, against 31 of 44 short real hovers kept).
- **Deskflow** (`yuki/memory/trace_deskflow.py`): this PC is the Deskflow server. Deskflow's own log (`%ProgramData%\Deskflow\deskflow-daemon.log` on this PC, or the file `[log] toFile`/`file` in `%APPDATA%\Deskflow\Deskflow.conf` names) is read on demand from where it was last read (one `stat` when unchanged, at most 256 KB a look); its fixed lines `leaving screen` / `entering screen` give the spans the cursor was on another computer. A span also ends at Deskflow's own start and stop lines (`starting daemon`, `daemon is running`, `daemon is stopping`, `started server, waiting for clients`, `stopped server`: it always starts and stops with the cursor here) and at this PC's boot (`GetTickCount64`), so a shutdown with the cursor over there no longer drops everything after the reboot (review F1; 4 such spans in this PC's log). Any action in such a span gives no row (`skip_other_screen`), nor does any action in Deskflow's own processes (`skip_deskflow`); Deskflow's window coming to the front is never named as an outcome.
- **Content in button names** (`trace_privacy.content_item`): a command-control-type element with a ListItem, TreeItem, DataItem, List, Tree or DataGrid among its ancestors (or as its parent) is a content item: its row keeps the name digest and position only (no name, so readers that judge a row by its role alone find none), its class is `title`, and its name reaches `ui_elements` only after 3 distinct days. A registry row kept as a command control follows the new class from its next use (its name cleared until its third day, and the name dropped from that element's earlier raw rows). The mark is sticky (second verify): a later use of the same element with no current item in view (a sidebar card clicked while no thread is open) still follows the title rule and its raw row keeps no name (`trace_store._upsert_element` / `write_batch`). Toolbar and command buttons outside lists keep their names, and so does a button whose nearest such container is a ToolBar, even inside a list item (review F3; a message's action bar drawn as a plain Group inside a list item stays a content item until its 3rd day). Also a content item: a command control one of whose siblings (or itself) carries ARIA `current` (`AriaProperties`, read in the same children call that gives the index: `Element.current_set`), a set with a current item. Measured read-only on 9 Oct: Discord's server list is a List of ListItems (caught); T3 Code's sidebar draws its thread cards as Buttons in Groups under a navigation landmark, the open thread marked `current=page` (caught by the set rule: its 9 sidebar buttons, nothing else in the window).
- **COM errors are counted, not logged** (`trace_uia.TraceUia.from_event`): every crash-log entry of 8 Oct came from `ElementFromIAccessibleBuildCache`, which is no longer called. A window's own object (`OBJID_WINDOW` / `OBJID_CLIENT`, `CHILDID_SELF`) is `ElementFromHandleBuildCache`; any other object is placed by MSAA (`AccessibleObjectFromEvent`, `accLocation`) and the element is UI Automation's answer at the click's point (inside the object) or its centre, walked up at most 6 levels to the element whose bounds are the object's (1 px of rounding allowed); none matching is `event_unmatched`. COM errors are `com_error_<code>` in the client's counts and in `trace:health`; real errors still log their type and line.

## Links and tasks: the "why" of the trace (capC2, 7 Oct 2026; migration 39)

Report 78 phase C2, build report 98. Reads migration 38's `ui_actions`; with recording off there is nothing to read.

- **Links** (`yuki/memory/trace_links.py`, `ui_links`): exact machine joins only, on the practice worker's tick from a cursor (`nudge_state` `trace:links`, encrypted): `clipboard` (a paste and the user's *latest* copy before it in another app, the same sequence number; a copy and paste inside one app is no link; a number reused after a restart never joins an old copy), `file` (an open of a path digest saved earlier in another app; a download is the browser's save), `url` (a press on a Hyperlink whose identity was taken, never `stale`, then within 3 s another app's `nav`/`switch` row with `page_reached` or a page key different from its last). The user's own rows only; one link per hyperlink press. An Alt+Tab is a `switch` action, never a link.
- **Transitions** (`ui_transitions`): per app, element key -> next element key the user acted on there (identity taken, an acting kind), with `n`, the last 15 uses (gap and time), the gaps' median and `last_at`; never across a 2-minute pause with no input at all. Digests only. A forget of an hour replays the whole stretch between the 2-minute pauses around it and takes back the pairs and times its rows made.
- **The task pass** (`yuki/memory/trace_tasks.py`, `TaskPass`, from `PracticeWorker.task_pass`): each final episode window once (a window the episode worker finished with no episode, empty or given up, too), inside the miner's `blocked()` gate, asked again before each window (idle 10 min; never paused, in a call or full screen); at most 4 windows a pass, oldest first; windows that ended over 7 days ago are never passed; a failed window waits 30 min, 3 tries. Windows with no action of the user's are closed `empty` at once with no model and no gate (`mark_empty`). Code cuts the window into pieces at 2-minute pauses measured from the end of the last input (any of the user's rows, hovers too, is input; a long typing run is input all along) and merges the pieces of a link's two ends when at most one pause lies between them; a link further apart is a LINKS line in the model's input (the two pieces may form one task, the pieces between stay their own). Step lines: never Yuki's rows, stale rows or hovers; names only from the element registry; typing as a count; scrolls and typing in one field folded; about 900 lines at most (long pieces shortened in the middle; lines with an outcome always kept).
