Recorded outcomes are marked on their lines (`{f3 file saved (.txt)}`): the trace's facts plus `paste_sent` (a clipboard link whose paste is followed in the same piece by `field_submitted` on the same element). One Haiku call (`TASK_PROMPT`, forced tool `record_tasks`, worked examples, the window's journal facts and the user's last 6 corrections as context). Code checks: pieces exist and are used once; a goal of 2-16 words and at most 140 characters citing no shown id; an outcome only a fact recorded inside that task, not in a detour (else `unclear`); detours inside the task and not the whole of it. Written to `ui_tasks` (goal encrypted; `outcome_fact`, `outcome_action`, `outcome_target` a machine identity; the episode it overlaps most) and `ui_task_actions` (one range per piece and per detour, read by the actions' times: `task_rows`). `learnable(task)` is false for `unclear` and for a task the user rejected: an unclear task may be a retrieval example, never a trial or a way (C2 writes no playbook at all).
- **Cost**: `ui_task_runs` per pass (cost, tasks, unclear, refused, ok); refused when today's cost reached $0.40 or the next call's upper estimate would pass it (one `refused` row a day; the windows wait for the next day). Not in the practice pools (`portrait_runs`), so the learner's own budgets are untouched: the practice worker can spend up to $0.15 + $0.15 + $0.40 a day (a decision recorded in report 98, review fixes).
- **Families and usual paths** (`yuki/memory/trace_families.py`, `refresh` on the tick inside the same gate): new or renamed goals embedded (the shared embedder, encrypted in `ui_tasks.vec_ciphertext`); a task joins the family whose centre is at cosine 0.75 or more and whose route shares at least a third of the shorter route (longest common subsequence over step tokens), or 0.92 by the goal alone; else a new family. Step tokens: element keys, or `app|keys|Ctrl+K`, `app|switch`, `app|file_save|.ext`, `app|command`, `app|copy` (detours dropped, repeats folded). The usual path: the most typical route (most steps shared with the others) as backbone, each step with how many of the latest 12 routes took it and when last; the steps most of those routes took (half of `path_n`, at least 2) are the usual path; the other routes' extra steps are branches with counts. `path_keys` / `variants_keys` hold machine fields only; the label is a member's goal, encrypted. A family counts its members (`ui_family_members`: each counted task's route, apps, span and goal embedding; no goal text, no names), kept as long as the family, so it keeps its count and usual path after its tasks expire at 30 days and grows when the task is done again. Tasks rejected on the Day page leave their family at once (the correction rebuilds it), renamed ones on the next tick; family ids are never reused (a forget's undo puts a family back under its own id); a family with no member left is deleted.
- **What the brain sees**: `practice.relevant()` (so `MemoryClient.practice()`) gains `path` (the nearest family clearing the ways' own floors, 2 tasks or more: at most 8 usual steps, each at most 100 characters, 700 in all, worded from the registry's kept names only - an element it no longer has is left out) and `example` (that family's latest task: goal, natural date, apps, outcome in words, at most 6 steps; an unclear one may be it); `detail=True` gives 16 steps and the branches. `paths=` defaults to `go_tos`, so the talker gets neither. The thinker's frame shows them before the go-tos (numbered steps, then "The last time the user did this ..."), inside its 2,500-character cap (the pitfalls stay whole); the 39,000-character tool schemas are unchanged.
- **The Day page** (decision D5): `web_data.session_tasks` puts `MemoryClient.day_tasks()` inside the session holding each task's middle: goal, apps, time span, outcome in plain words ("you saved a file (.txt)", "not sure it got done"). Rename (your words), Not one task, Not what I did and Undo go through `task_correct` / `task_correct_undo` (web ops of the same names, on their own short thread) to `user_verdict` (`renamed` | `not_one` | `not_mine`); the pass's own goal stays in `model_goal_ciphertext` for the worked example. The page's change stamps include `ui_tasks`. Never a card, never spoken.
- **Retention and forget** (`trace_store`): tasks, ranges and runs 30 days (D4) with the raw rows and links; transitions 60 days unused; families 90 days unused, and family members older than 90 days (the family is rebuilt from the rest). Forget-an-hour / forget-an-app also deletes the tasks built on those rows (a range holding a forgotten row's time; for an app, every task naming it), takes the family members of those tasks (and of expired tasks whose span holds a forgotten row, or that named the app), rebuilds their families (one with no member left is deleted; a label taken from a forgotten task goes), takes back the transitions the rows made (an app's all), and clears the links cursor's last element of that app; the before-images go in the forget's undo image and `undo_forget` puts them back.
- **Health**: `nudge_state` `trace:tasks` (tasks today, unclear today, cost today and its cap, windows waiting, refused today), every 10 minutes and after a pass; the doctor's "Learning how you work" line says it in plain words.
- **Tools**: `scripts/trace_seeded_session.py` (`--script`: six tasks with 2-minute pauses, one interleaved; the analyzer runs the pass on a backup-API copy of the database for the session's span and scores it, pass 5 of 6, no task from a stale or Yuki row; `--dry-run`: no model call). `scripts/check_day_tasks.js`: the Day page's tasks and corrections in the headless browser.

## Did it my way: the C3 check (capC3, 9 Oct 2026; developer tool, no migration)

Report 78 phase C3, build report 105. Not part of the app: `scripts/c3_eval.py` and `yuki/eval/` (`c3_data`, `c3_arms`,
`c3_score`, `c3`) run on a backup-API copy in `%TEMP%` (refused on the live file), cut it back in time and delete it.

- **Requests**: the user's own in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\c3-requests.json` (with the time; `steps_shown_at` once planned
  steps were shown: later ones are not blind and are left out), plus session-log turns (`*-worker.jsonl` `user_message`
  of a turn with a `memory_acting` begin), in the user's words by Yuki's own format (`request_words`; Yuki's own words,
  a suggestion sent unchanged or a "Do it", are none). Never task goals. A request is checked only on a task after it.
- **Evaluated task and cut**: nearest family by the brain's floors (`trace_families.hints`); the latest `learnable` task
  whose (outcome kind, target) an earlier family task labelled before it reached; cut-off = its start (a conversation
  turn's: the turn's time, the earlier task before it too). `cut`: `trace_store.forget(since=cut-off)`, tasks with
  `created_at` from then, `verdict_at` from then taken back (rejected ones placed again by `refresh`), playbooks created
  and events from then removed, also any `superseded_by` of a row retired from then (a reshape keeps the old
  `created_at`) with the old row's earlier events and its family link (`ui_families.playbook_id`) moved back, `valid_to`/`disabled_at` from then cleared,
  `practice.recompute(now=cut-off)`, runs from then, the go-tos' `nudge_state` tallies refolded. Latest first, one copy.
- **Snapshot** (`snapshot(store, cutoff)`, B0/L only): rows before the cut-off only, never chosen by the task: apps of
  the last hour (6), apps of the week as "not open" (3), 40 most recent controls each.
- **Arms**: B0/L by Yuki's order (a `qualifies` way replayed on the snapshot, stopping at the first step off its path;
  else the thinker: `SYSTEM_PROMPT` + `PLAN_ONLY`, the frame from `relevant()`, forced `record_plan`, no thinking); learning
  off by `trace_replay.set_use_learned` in-process, held for the whole arm (`c3_arms.learning`; by hand before C5).
  B1: the family's latest task's route. Scores over C2 route tokens, every arm cut at C2's 80 steps, planned keys in
  `trace_keys`' names (`c3_score.chord`): element accuracy (positional), step precision/recall (multiset), edit
  distance; composite = their mean.
  Pass: L over B0 and B1 by > 3 points and more wins than losses; no verdict under 10 requests, on an incomplete run,
  on a single run (`repeat` defaults to 2) or when any two runs differ by > 3 points.
- **Limits and output**: 30 tasks, 55 minutes, $3 (expected cost refused up front; each call's upper bound checked
  before it: system prompt as a cache write, tool schema, +15%). Console: counts and scores. Report:
  `%TEMP%\yuki-c3-report-<time>.json/.txt`.

## Your way, learned from what you did (capC4, 7 Oct 2026; no migration)

Report 78 phase C4, build report 100. Reads C2's `ui_tasks` / `ui_task_actions` and C1's `ui_actions`; writes only through the practice learner's `apply` into the practice tables (no new table). With recording off there is nothing to read.

- **Which tasks** (`yuki/memory/trace_demos.py`, `due_tasks`): tasks past the pass's cursor (`nudge_state` `trace:demos_state`, ids and counts only: cursor, retries, tasks put back, forgets seen), only `learnable` ones (a checked outcome fact, never `unclear`, never rejected on the Day page) that ended in the last 7 days; at most 3 a pass. Unlearnable ones are passed for good.
- **The pass** (`practice_learn.demo_pass` -> `Learner.learn_task`, from `PracticeWorker.task_pass` after the families, inside the same `blocked()` gate, asked again before each task): code reads the task's own actions (`task_actions`: its pieces' ranges, detours marked) and builds each line's step (`row_step` -> `practice.demo_step`, the trace sibling of `executable_step`): a named command control -> `click` (role, name, and `aid` only when `proven_aid`: the element seen with the same AutomationId in 2 sessions of its app - distinct `proc_started` - and no element of that app ever seen with two); a content control (`name_class` content) -> `pick` (`in: {role}` of its parent, the item's `role`, the `app`, and `item`: the first 20 hex characters of C1's keyed name digest, or `slot` when the request names it); a chord -> `keys`; typing or a paste -> `type` with a slot (D8: never text); a switch -> `open_app`; a page change -> `open_url` only when the way has no slot and the timeline kept the address; a Warp command -> `command` (thinker only); a scroll -> `scroll`. One Haiku call (`DEMO_TASK_PROMPT`, forced `record_demo`, two worked examples, the shared practice pool, `portrait_runs` kind `practice_demo`) picks the essence, slots, intent, family and expect words. `check_demo` (code): every cited action must be one shown, the user's own (`user`/`user_injected`), its identity taken (not `stale`, not `unresolved`) and outside the detours, else **nothing** is learned from the task; the checked outcome's own action must be the same; steps in time order, none after the outcome, the outcome's action appended when it can be a step; a step that sent what was typed (`field_submitted`/`paste_sent` on its row) marks the way `sends`; expect words only when a later kept name or app shows them.
- **Parts** (`split_parts`): at most 8 steps a part (`MAX_RUN_STEPS`), cut only where the app changes or at a `ui_links` end, and only where the trace recorded a state between the two steps that the skill runner checks live (`PART_END_KINDS`: a window verified, a page reached; a fact such as a control selected never ends a part before the last) - that state is the part's end; the last part ends on the task's outcome. At most 4 parts (`MAX_PARTS`); otherwise, or with a hint-only action (a right click, a drag, a copy), the way is text for the thinker. `body.parts` (validated by `clean_parts`); a single runnable part is also `body.run`. Steps text: one line per step, or one per part past 8 steps.
- **Evidence and states** (`practice.evaluate`): a `demonstrated` event with run key `demo:<task id>:<target>` - the target a keyed digest of the outcome's kind and its target (element key, app, page digest, file digest). Demos group by target: one is a **trial** (`state` candidate, origin `demo`, label "your way, seen once", `trial` in `relevant()` items: a hint, sorted after ways; `voice.practice.qualifies` never offers it and `run_skill` refuses it); `DEMO_TIMES` (2) tasks on the same target, or `DEMO_PROVEN_RUNS` (1) of Yuki's runs that used it and verified (a `succeeded` mark, written by `add_events` beside a `ran_ok` only when `verified_run` says so - a label alone never proves it - and dated after the first demonstration, so runs inherited from an older version never count), or the user's word or fix make it a **way** (active). The two numbers and whether a trial is shown as a hint at once come from the user's `[trace]` settings (`learn_demo_alone_after_times`, `learn_demo_alone_after_proven_runs`, `learn_demo_hint`: `practice.demo_rules`, set by the practice worker from the trace's rules, read from the settings file in any other process). Other sources never add up for a demo object; different targets never combine; an outcome with no target (C2 keeps none) has the task's own signature and never combines. A new demo joins the live demo way with the same target signature in the same apps (`same_target_demo`, by code, whatever the model's words), else a demo way found by similarity with that same target; it never replaces a way not learned from demos, and never one the user taught, fixed or turned off (theirs stands). `provenance` says "user" (learned from what they did).
- **Corrections and forgets** (`sync_withdrawals`, each pass): a task rejected on the Day page, or taken by a forget-an-hour/app (read from the trace's forget diary and undo image), withdraws its demos (`demo_withdrawn`, same run key; `evaluate` skips them); an undo restores them (`demo_restored`). A way whose every demo is withdrawn and that rests on nothing else is retired; its task is learned again once the rejection or forget is undone.
- **Families**: a family whose task taught a way points at it (`ui_families.playbook_id`, followed along `superseded_by` to the live version); `relevant()` brings the family's way along (family -> path -> playbook) within `MAX_WAYS`, and drops the usual `path` when that way (not a trial) is shown. Caps unchanged (frame 2,500, schemas 39,000, talker 400 + 520).
- **Running it** (`yuki/voice/skills.py`): `pick` reads the container (by role, and name when known) in a fresh tree read, takes its visible descendants of the item's role (up to 6 levels down), and clicks the one whose name's keyed digest (`MemoryClient.name_digests`, the tray's own memory key) starts with `item`, or whose name equals the request's words for a slot pick; no container, no match, two matches or memory not answering: the run stops and hands over. A way in parts runs all parts in order, verifying each part's end before the next (`end_ok`: a window of the app in front, a new page of the app in front, or the part's last step verified); a failed end stops with deviation `part_end` and the thinker's context. The talker may give the way's steps, its last steps, or only its typing and slot picks (`fill_slots`). Refused before anything runs: a trial, a `command` step, more than 4 parts of 8, a way learned from a demo with no runnable parts (`hint_only`: a hint for the thinker, also never offered by `qualifies`), a part before the last ending on anything but a window or page, a call that leaves no step to run, and a `sends` way (kept on every demo way) unless the talker declares `sends` with whom and what in the user's words. A click step with a proven `aid` finds that control first.
- **Second verification (report 100)**: the demo run key is `demo:<task id>:<target>.<steps>` - the steps half a keyed digest of the steps' machine shape (`trace_demos.steps_sig`: do, role, kept name, item and container digests, keys, app, address; never slot names or expect words), so demos add up only when they took the same steps to the same target (another route is its own trial); the event's refs keep `target` and `steps` apart. A way that sends turns a fixed-item pick into a slot pick (`SEND_PICK_SLOT`), and `run_skill` refuses a sending way with a fixed pick and requires a slot pick's words to be the talker's `to`; Yuki's own check sees the picked item's live name. `proven_aid` also needs no other element of the app with that AutomationId, and a click by `aid` needs the live control's name to agree (or no name, unique id). A pick keeps `in.digest` (C1's keyed container digest) and matches it live (`MemoryClient.container_digests`); without it the item must be the only match in the window. A `succeeded` mark on a demo way also needs `practice.demo_run_reached`: the run's own trace rows (source `yuki`, its run key) reached a standing demo's outcome kind on the same target, or the run's executed steps are the way's runnable steps. `sync_withdrawals` counts only the user's word or fix or a `succeeded` after the first demo as other support, and when the withdrawn demo is the one the body came from (`body.trace.task`) it restores the newest earlier version learned from a standing demo (`rebody_withdrawn`), else retires this version and learns the standing tasks again. `link_family` links a trial only when the family has no live link, and replaces a way only by a way. A `page_reached` part end compares the end app's own window title just before the part's last step. A demo way's typing is checked only against the user's words. Health counts hint-only demo ways as `hints`.
- **Health and the page**: `nudge_state` `trace:demos` (ways, trials, learned and set aside today); the doctor's "Learning how you work" line says it in plain words. The Playbook page shows such ways as "Learned from what you did" and a trial as "Your way, seen once ... I'll do it on my own once you've done it again, or once it worked when I followed it"; Forget and Turn off work as for any way.

## The workflow graph's uses (capC6, 7 Oct 2026; no migration)

Report 78 phase C6 (the parts that need no routine learner), build report 101. Reads C2's tables as stored (an install
that never recorded has nothing to read; turning recording off stops new rows only).

- **The queries** (`yuki/memory/trace_graph.py`, read only, never raises): `how_usually(query_vec)` (the families
  nearest the words by their goals' centre, floor 0.45 like the brain's, each with its usual path - each step with how
  many of the last routes took it - branches, count, last use, apps, what usually follows it and its latest task);
  `families(app=, query_vec=)`; `in_app(app)` (its families, its go-tos and shortcuts from C1b's tallies read without
  counting as served, its usual transitions); `next_after_element(key)` (its transitions and, for each family whose
  path holds it, the next step of the usual path after it) and `next_after_task(id)` (the families of the next task in the same episode window,
  `followed_by`: only a follower seen at least twice and at least half the times looked at); `task_apps(id)`; `tasks_between(since, until)` (tasks overlapping the range, oldest first, rejected
  ones left out and counted, renamed ones in the user's words); `done_evidence(since, until, thread=)` (learnable tasks only,
  and only those whose outcome made, sent or ran something, `DONE_FACTS`: a file saved, typed text sent, pasted in and
  sent, a command that worked; a window or page that came up, a file opened, an item picked or a setting switched is
  never "done"; their outcome inside the range, `thread`: only tasks with one of that thread's journal entries in their span, newest first, with the task and outcome action ids and the outcome in second-person
  words). Names only from the element registry's kept names (role and position otherwise; an element it no longer has
  is left out); a family's label shows only while the task it came from is still one of its members and not rejected (so a rejected
  task's goal stays hidden after its row expires). A branch past a path cut at 16 steps says "later on". Caps: 8 families, 16
  steps, 4 branches, 8 transitions, 40 tasks, 12 evidence items, 100/140/240 characters a step/label/branch. Reads by
  `ui_tasks_span`, `ui_tasks_family`, `ui_tasks_window` and the transitions' primary key; the families table is read at
  most 2,000 rows (small by retention). About 0.5-8 ms each on 200 families, 1,200 tasks and 4,000 transitions.
- **`MemoryClient`**: `trace_workflows(words, app=)`, `trace_tasks_between(since, until)`, `trace_next(element=,
  task=)`, `trace_task_apps(task)`, `done_evidence(since, until)`.
- **MCP** (`yuki/mcp/server.py`, read only, registered when the client has them): `get_workflows` (`query`, `app`,
  `limit` 1-8) and `get_tasks` (`since`, `until`, `limit` 1-40, default today), plain text with the `Covers:` line in
  local time (`get_tasks` with only `until` reads everything before it); the server's instructions describe them. Calls logged content-free like the others.
- **The Work page** (stamps `ui_tasks`; a task correction on Day refreshes Work too): a Worked on today row gains a 4th item when the PC checked something got done in that thread's
  sessions today (`web_data.worked_evidence`: the newest `done_evidence` item inside them, read per session newest first): `{"text": "you saved a
  file (.pptx) at 4 PM", "goal", "ref" (task), "action"}`, shown as a quiet second line under the title (the goal as
  its hover text); a row without one keeps its three items. No ids on the page; not a card; not spoken.
- **The check-ins**: the writer's message gains WHAT THEY GOT DONE (`CheckinWorker._done_lines`): learnable tasks that made, sent or
  ran something (`DONE_FACTS`), from the last 24 hours, and for a moment about a thread of work that thread's from the last 7 days (a task belongs to the
  thread most journal entries in its span belong to; read on its own and listed first), at most 6, each `[T<task id>]` with its time, apps, thread, goal
  and outcome words. The prompt says the PC checked only that step (never that the whole goal is
  finished), it is a dot to connect, never a check-in on its own, and never to ask whether that step got done. A cited T id is checked like every id (offered, never in the text) and goes in the card's
  inputs as `done_tasks: [{"task", "action"}]`. Same card, same gates and budgets.

## Game test, long calls, task quality (capQ2b, 9 Oct 2026; no migration)

Build report 104, from the audit of the first full day of real data (8-9 Oct).

- **What a game is** (`gamegate.judge_program`, used by the gate and by `avsignals.gaming_with_voice` for the call worker and the timeline): machine facts only. The fixed anti-cheat list (`[trace] game_processes`, the user can add); Windows' own game detection (`HKCU\System\GameConfigStore\Children` `MatchedExeFullPath`, read-only, matched by full path, re-read on a registry change notification, and a change re-matches the running programs at the gate's next check or beat; a `javaw.exe` Windows matched for one game launcher does not make every `javaw.exe` a game); the gate's learned set (Direct3D exclusive full screen while in front, 2 stretches, evidence `d3d_exclusive`; a borderless stretch takes one back from a candidate only, never from a learned game); D3D exclusive full screen now. The GPU share is read and logged, never decisive (FL Studio maximised with the microphone drew 16% on 8 Oct and was taken for a game). Learned entries without `d3d_exclusive` evidence are dropped by code at each gate start (`recheck_evidence`, logged `game_gate_unlearned` why `no_windows_fact`).
- **Task pass during calls** (`PracticeWorker.task_blocked`): an open call alone no longer holds the task pass (one remote Haiku call); it runs in a call when the user has been idle for the miner's idle time, the game gate says no game runs (`game_fn`, from the service; none known: never) and nothing is full screen. The families refresh (local embedder) and the demo learner keep `blocked()`: never in a call. Budget ($0.40 a day) and 4 windows a pass unchanged.
- **Task input**: apps by their program name (`trace_tasks.program_name`, from `yuki.core.appnames`: version info, cached); a PROJECTS section from `ui_files` (tempo, instrument and effect plugins, counts; the project's name only once the file was used on 3 distinct days; never channel or pattern names); the model may mark pieces as `noise` (counted in `nudge_state` `trace:tasks:noise` and the health's `noise_today`, never a task row). A window coming to the front is no outcome when nothing else was done in that app inside the task, or when the work went on in it after, or when it went back to the app of the last action before it (another program took the focus for a moment) (`trace_tasks.window_only`): the task stays `unclear`, not learnable. Tasks stored before that rule are re-checked when read for learning (`learnable(task, store)`, read-only). The CORRECTIONS lines name apps by program too.
- **Logs**: `trace_learn_pass` (task windows, tasks, unclear, noise, cost, outcome kinds; demos, result kinds, cost, errors) and `trace_learn_waiting` (why the pass waits, on change) in the memory log, content-free. **Doctor**: the trace lines' "today" numbers only when the health row is from today.

## Doing it your way: the replay guard and the A/B switch (capC5, 9 Oct 2026; no migration)

Report 78 phase C5, build report 102. Reads C1's `ui_actions`, the timeline's cleartext `host`, C2's tasks and families and the runs table; writes nothing new (no table, no `nudge_state` key).

- **The switch** (`yuki/memory/trace_replay.py`, `use_learned`): `[trace] use_learned` in the privacy file, default `true` (packaged default; a file without the key reads as on). Read live in every process: one `stat` a call, parsed again only when the file changed, the last good value on a broken file. Off: `practice.relevant` leaves out every object the trace taught (`from_trace`: origin `demo` or `fixed`, or a body citing a task) and works out no go-tos, path or example; `voice.practice.qualifies` re-checks; `run_skill` refuses an item marked `trace` or `demo`. Ways learned from Yuki's own runs stay. `relevant()` items of a procedure with origin `demo` or `fixed` carry `trace: true`.
- **The recorded path** (`way_path`, `MemoryClient.way_path`): for a way whose `body.trace.actions` line up one per executable step (parts' steps, else `run`): per step the app (`app_key` of the row's process), the site (`timeline.host` of the row's `page_key`, `www.` off), `same_window` (same process, the row no `switch`/`nav` of its own, and no `window_changed`/`dialog` in the step before's decrypted outcome summary unless that step was itself a switch or nav), `dialog` (its own outcome summary has `dialog`, or `owned_window`: the window that came to the front after it was owned, `GW_OWNER` - an app's own modal window, which Windows does not mark with DIALOGSTART, review F7), `new_window` (a `window_changed` that was not owned: the trace now also counts `top_window`; a row recorded before that fact reads as a top-level window, the stricter reading; second verification N4: it never allows an owned window after the step); `dialog_apps`: apps where a `dialog` outcome came up in any of the way's demo tasks (`playbook_events` `demonstrated` run keys `demo:<task>:...`, and `body.trace.task`) - still returned, no longer used by the runner (N4: per step only). The trace worker (`yuki/memory/trace.py`, `_owned`) adds `owned_window` / `top_window` to a row's outcome summary next to `window_changed` (one `GetWindow` call when the front window changes; capC5 second verification, small additive change). A row that is gone or not `user`/`user_injected` is `{"known": false}`. Read only; never raises.
- **The guard** (`yuki/voice/skills.py`): `SkillRunner.expectations` turns the path into one `Expect` per step that runs (apps, host, same_window, dialog, recorded; without a recording the apps the way names). Machine facts only: window identity and `owner_hwnd` (an unlisted dialog in front counts through `foreground_owner_hwnd`), the app's process, the page's Document address host; never titles or positions. Off the path (another app or window, another site, a new owned window the recording never showed - only one owned by a window the run acted in or of the step's app counts, never another app's popup elsewhere, review F4 - the target missing or more than one): the step is not done, the run stops with deviation `app` / `window` / `page` / `dialog` / `not_found`, and `facts["handover"]` = "I stopped at step N: <what was different>." heads the report and the thinker's `skill_context`. A keys step goes only into a window the run acted in or opened, the app's window in front when it began (before any step acted), a window owned by one it acted in, or one the step just before brought to the front by its own measured report (`details.foreground_changed` / `foreground_hwnd`) when that step's recording allows a window change (`new_window` or `dialog`; no recording: the report decides) - never a window that came up by itself (second verification N5). Just before a step acts (and again after Yuki's own check), an owned window that came up since the step before began (not in its pre-step window set nor open before the run), that the step would act in or that is the way's and in front, stops the run when the step before's recording had no dialog; with no recording it is on the path only when the step acts inside it (N3); a dialog already open when a step acted is never that step's. The app of a window is its process, except a UWP frame (`ApplicationFrameHost.exe`), which is the app it hosts (`hosted_app`: `content_pid`, as the trace records it; N2); words to the user name apps as people say them (`yuki.core.appnames.prettify`), never a process file. A way with no recording may end on the dialog its last step opened (review F5). After an `open_app`/`open_url` whose report could not tell, the next step's window is the one in front only when it is that app's, else none (review F6). Takeover (report 63's `TakeoverThread`, real hands only) is looked at inside every wait and just before every action, so a step the user took over during does nothing (review F3), and it stops the run: a `takeover` step in the run trace, `settled`, no handover, outcome `unverified`.
- **Anchors**: proven `aid` (C4 rules), role + name, pick by digest within its container, then the RuntimeId of the control the same step acted on earlier in this session (`remember_rid` / `recall_rid`: in RAM only, keyed by the hands' identity and memory's session id, same window and app, 2 hours, 200 kept; the live name must still bear the name it had, whole words in order, so a count added is the same control and a toggle renamed is not, review F2; never for a pick by the user's words, review F1). It is a re-find only when no control fits by name: several name matches stop the run as ambiguous, and a run with a pick by the user's words uses no RuntimeId at all (second verification N1).
- **Each step verified by report 58's diff**: `skill_dispatcher(hands)` makes one `Dispatcher` per set of real hands with `verify_outcomes=True`, 1,000 elements, 2.5 s a read.
- **Deviations per way** (`deviations`): hand runs (`runs.step = 0`, `source = 'hand'`) citing the way with outcome `handed_over`, in a row since its last `final`, 30 days; `relevant()` multiplies the way's rank by 0.7 per deviation (at most 3), adds `deviations` to the item and says so in `why`; never out.
- **Chord words** (`yuki/memory/trace_habits.py`): with the store's decrypter, `fold_rows` counts per chord the outcome classes its rows recorded (`o`), the readable uses (`on`) and the element used next in the same app within 4 s (`x`, at most 4); `chord_did` words the classes seen in at least half the readable uses and the most-used next control (registry names only), at most 110 characters; `go_tos` items carry `did`; the frame's go-tos line shows it. Only `user`/`user_injected` rows.
- **Health** (`health`, `health_words`, `MemoryClient.trace_replay_health`): over 7 days, hand runs citing a way: done (`final`), stopped off the path (`handed_over`) and of those followed by a thinker run within 120 s, taken over (a `takeover` step), held; the doctor's "Learning how you work" line words them and the switch when off.
- **The live check** (`scripts/c5_live_check.py`): each request once with the switch on, other requests for the switch off (`--off` / `--off-file`), the switch alternating request by request (`--paired`: each twice, only for requests that leave nothing lasting behind, review F8); the pass needs at least 10 requests with it on and 7 of 10 done, and compares on and off by the share done, then by the time against the user's usual for each kind of task, since they are other requests (second verification N6); a plan file outside the memory folder (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\c5-live-check.json`, the requests as said); the analyzer works on a backup-API copy and reads only cleartext run columns, the decrypted reason of the thinker's control steps (its takeover pause) and the families' spans.

## Phase F: moments, the game gate in front, the Spotify window, one request id (10 Oct 2026; migration 40)

Plan: `docs/research/audit-2026-10-09/PLAN.md` rev 4 (phase F, decisions D0-D20); build reports
`docs/research/audit-2026-10-09/builds/{fPrivacy,fGate,fMoments,fLive,evalFix,fWire}.md`. Zero setup throughout (D0): no
account, key, sign-in or token anywhere.

- **Moments** (`yuki/memory/moments.py`, table `moments`, migration 40): one rebuildable table of state changes in one
  vocabulary (D1): `call_join`, `game_voice`, `site_front`, `media_play`, `media_context`, `page`, `volume_change`,
  `message_sent`. Derived kinds (`call_join`, `game_voice`, `page`, `message_sent`) are computed from rows memory already
  keeps, each pointing back at its source row; a rule change is a code change (`RULE_VERSION`, now 2: page moments take
  their entity from the sites' URL templates, `yuki/connect/sites/*.toml`), and the service rebuilds them once on its next
  start (`python -m yuki.memory.moments rebuild | backfill --since | stats | bench`, each with `--db`). Live kinds are
  written once when the change is seen: `media_play` from the timeline's 15 s media read (the title only as a keyed digest
  unless `[moments] media_titles`), `site_front` from the watcher's sighting of a calling site (app and product name only,
  plus whether the Meet tab was identified, `facts.tab_known`), `volume_change` from the volume watcher (master and each
  app's mixer level, Core Audio's own callbacks, coalesced 1.5 s; Yuki's own changes carry its audio context GUID and are
  `by='yuki'`) and from Spotify's slider (`means='in_app'`), `media_context` from Spotify's window (which playlist; the
  name and a learned `spotify:` link encrypted). No polling and no thread of its own: the service's 1 s loop beats it
  (refresh every `refresh_s`, sooner after a call starts or ends). D18: a moment inside a Yuki run's window is
  `by='yuki'` with its `request_id`, so Yuki's own runs never become "your habit". Kept `keep_days` (90); never in the MCP.
  `[moments]` in the privacy file: `enabled`, `volume_events`, `media_titles`, `refresh_s`, `page_dwell_s`, `keep_days`.
- **One meeting rule** (D7): a join Yuki saw (a screen judged in a call, the old timeline route, or a calling site's
  meeting row), or a microphone hold of an app with a seen call on record (or in `[calls] apps`), never a game's
  program (`gamegate.judge_program`, machine facts only). The timeline's display proof (`Store._apply_proof`, all three
  readers) uses the same rule (`moments.seen_call_record`, `moments.is_call_join`), so a pre-gate row that held the
  microphone of such an app reads as a meeting there too (fWire). FL Studio, Settings and VALORANT are never meetings;
  the browser exemption is gone (every hold needs call evidence). Mic-hold days count for a routine only inside one with
  >= 3 seen days (the miner's rule, phase R).
- **The game gate = a game in front** (D9-D11, `fGate`): the trace's input gate holds while a game is in front (or
  Direct3D exclusive full screen, or an unknown full-screen program still being judged, first stretch only); with the
  game behind another window the trace and the cards run. Cards hold while a game or full-screen app is in front and for
  `[nudges] return_hold_s` (120 s) after leaving it. Background CPU jobs (the practice learner, the scrub) still wait
  while a game runs anywhere (`running_holds`). Rollback in one edit: `[trace] game_gate = "running"` brings back the old
  rule for the trace and the cards (stricter only; any other value means "front").
- **Nothing reaches a game** (D17): `gamegate.refuse_input` is asked right before every key, click, pointer move, focus
  (UI Automation focus too), window restore or activation, opened link and app launch, and refuses with a game in front
  or aimed at a game's window; a UI Automation pattern call may run with a game in front but never on the game's own
  window (`front_ok`). It fails closed and the tray logs every refusal (`input_refused_game`: action, game stem, where).
  "Game in front" is a local read of the foreground program, widened by the Now board's fresh `game_front` (the memory
  gate's answer, learned games included; `now.board_front_source`, asked at most every 0.25 s). A source-walking test
  keeps every such call under `yuki/actions`, `perception`, `skills` and `connect` behind the guard. Media transport and
  the system volume moved off keys: the talker's `media_key` commands one media session (the named app's, else the playing
  one, else the current one; never a game's) and the volume keys set the Core Audio endpoint, so they work mid-game.
- **Spotify's own window** (D4, `yuki/connect/spotify_desktop.py`, read-only UI Automation): warmed once when its window
  appears or its music first starts (child windows asked, at most 3 s, never with a game in front); a Library read
  (0.3 s) when its music starts, a slider read when it leaves the front, a daily counts-only probe per window state
  (covered, on another screen, minimised). Labels are data per language with a shape fallback and a row wording learned
  only after a row's button flips. `[spotify]`: `enabled`, `library_reads`, `read_budget_s`, `warm_budget_s`,
  `state_probe`. It only feeds the moments: with `[moments] enabled = false` at start it is not built, and it stops
  (no job, no moment) before the hub does. Doctor: "Spotify desktop".
- **The Meet tab by identity** (D8, `callevidence.SiteTabs`): a calling site's tab (the Meet tab in Zen) is learned by
  its RuntimeId, name digests and pinned place, never its title, at most once per 10 min per window; `tab_strip` logs why
  a strip read empty; `census` finds it again when an offer is due. The shipped site list's call verdicts are kept across
  restarts (`nudge_state['call_evidence:sites']`). The Now board carries `calling_tabs`, `game_front` (+ the game's
  stem) and `signals` (microphone holders and playing apps, app keys only).
- **One `request_id`** (`fPrivacy`, wired by `fWire`): the session log mints `<session id>-<n>` at the user's words and
  stamps every record; the agent's acting marker, the memory log's `acting` line, moments and the `requests.csv` row
  carry the same id; a front desk's request handed to the worker keeps the front desk's id, a spoken request handed to
  the thinker is `voice-<turn>-t<n>` and each voice hand `voice-<turn>-h<n>` (its marker and its run trace).
  `python -m yuki.log.follow <request_id>` puts one request together across the logs, content-free: the request's own
  session file and its run's others (after a hand-off the worker's records are in `<run>-worker.jsonl`, the UI log's
  `handoff` / `queued` lines in `<run>.jsonl`), the voice log (a hand's `hands` line names its id), the memory log,
  moments and `requests.csv`. Digests in logs are 12 hex characters (`log_digest`, D20).
- **Holes in the timeline** (`timeline_gap`): a gap of a minute or more with no stretch is logged with the window classes
  seen, short rows and probe timeouts (not for pause or sleep).

## Skills Yuki learned from you: phases S and L (10 Oct 2026; migrations 41 and 43)

Plan: `docs/research/audit-2026-10-09/PLAN.md` (phase S, phase L, D21-D24); learner design
`docs/research/audit-2026-10-09/learner/design.md` rev 2; build reports `builds/{sCore,sSpotify,sVoice,sWeb,sWire,
lSplit,lCore,lPage,lName,lWire}.md`. The user's rule (10 Oct, "learn, don't author"): code ships only generic
capabilities; Yuki's own pipeline finds the user's habits and writes them as skills. Zero setup throughout (D0).

- **Three layers.** Verbs (code: closed lists of verbs, done-check probes and slot types) -> **capabilities** (generic
  app-driving data, the same for every user: `yuki/skills/connections/*.toml`, 8 files, 7 capabilities: `volume`,
  `spotify.playlist`, `web.open_page`, `web.open_video`, `browser.site_tab`, `message`, `discord.voice`) -> **learned
  skills** (rows the learner writes in `skills`, `origin = 'learned'`; only these are "the user's ways"). A shipped
  file that carries a habit (a `[skill.*]` table, `routine_on`, `offerable`, `learn_from`, a means default, a process
  or service literal in a route) is refused whole (the habit-key refusal in `connection.py`).
- **The catalog** (`yuki/skills/catalog.py`): the capabilities from the files plus the live learned rows of a store
  (one count query when nothing changed; specs decrypted only when their version moved). A learned skill specialises
  its capability: its bound key values become fixed slot values and route arguments, a means slot is never bound, a
  free entity slot takes a taste kind from the site data (`taste.slot_kind`, e.g. a channel on a site's channel
  pages), a learned rung is B or C (never A), `wont` and `commit` only get stricter. Each process tells its own log
  once about a refused file (`connection_error`) or an unreadable learned row (`learned_error`), content-free: the
  tray's UI log (`MemoryAccess.set_skills_log`, set at start) and the memory log (the routine miner's and the card
  lane's loads).
- **Tables.** Migration 41 (phase S): `skills`, `skill_routes`, `skill_runs` (the ledger: one row per run written
  before its first step, then its outcome, the done-check's verdict and the re-check; D18 reads it so Yuki's own runs
  never become the user's habit), `skill_route_tries`, `prefs`, `pref_events`, `tastes`. Migration 43 (phase L):
  `occasions`; `skills.capability_id / spec_ciphertext / name_ciphertext / provenance / deleted_at`;
  `moments.entity_key` (one entity is one key across browsers). Moments `RULE_VERSION` 3 adds host-scope `page`
  moments for sites without a page template (60 s or more of dwell, `[moments] host_dwell_s`). None of these tables
  is in the MCP.
- **Running a skill** (`skills/engine.py`, `resolve.py`, `donecheck.py`): code fills the slots from the user's exact
  words, a told value, a live fact, the preference, taste, then the capability's default (guessed values are named in
  the done line); the engine runs the route ladder (rung A the app's own controls, B a recorded UI Automation path, C
  the planner with the skill as a plan seed), reads again before every rung that takes focus or sends input whether a
  game is in front (D17: that rung is skipped, else "after the round?"), and says "done" only when the done-check saw
  it (D19), else "I couldn't confirm it". The commit table (D24, `engine.COMMIT_BY_END` / `COMMIT_BY_NEED`) says who
  commits an end: `none` (Yuki may reach it), `words` (only on the user's plain words: a send, decision 7's Discord
  voice join) or `user` (always the user's click: a meeting join). A run that would reach a `user` end is refused,
  and so is a card-origin run of a `words` or `user` end. Sends follow rule 8 ("send X to Y" sends; naming only the
  person leaves a draft).
- **The learner** (`yuki/skills/learn/`): an **occasion** is one user state change (a moment) plus the visit that led
  to it (gaps of 10 s or less), its steps from the trace and its context (weekday and minute, game in front, call
  open, a request in the 10 minutes before). Dropped: Yuki's own moments and anything inside its acting steps or
  skill runs (D18; an end the user must commit stays the user's even then), games, spans the user marked not theirs,
  pages outside a browser. Occasions are bound to a capability from its `serves` / `bind` data only (no app list in
  code), grouped by (capability, end, key values), and a group becomes a skill at 3 occasions on 3 days in 28 (the
  last within 14), or 2 asked runs on 2 days; at most 20 live (the rest `listed`, Playbook only). States `candidate`
  / `trusted` (`registry.offer_ready`) / `listed` / `dormant` (14 days without an occasion or a use) / `retired` (60
  days); `deleted` and `not_mine` are tombstones the learner never revives and whose occasions it never reuses. Only
  key slots are bound; fill slots never are (a learned message skill never pre-fills a person). Provenance is
  content-free (counts, days, means shares, the code label).
- **When it runs.** Nightly, inside the portrait scheduler's night passes (`portrait._night`), right before the
  routine miner so the miner reads that night's skill ids (about 0.5-1 s CPU; it holds back with a game in front or a
  call open). Incrementally from the moments hub's 1 s beat (no thread of its own, no polling): due after an end
  moment, run on a short thread at most every 10 minutes and only while the user has been idle 120 s, with no game in
  front (the memory service's own gate rule too, so a game only that gate learned holds it) and no call open
  (`learn.quiet`; about 15 ms CPU). On the first start with no full pass written, the hub's first idle slot runs the
  full pass and the back-fill once (retried at most hourly). CLI: `python -m yuki.skills.learn rebuild [--db PATH]
  [--dry-run] [--report | --json] [--show] [--no-name]`, `backfill-usuals`.
- **Naming** (`learn/name.py`): one batched Haiku call a night for at most 10 groups (at most one call per 20 h): the
  capability's line, the bound app / service / site / page values, means shares, varying slots by name and kind,
  times in words, counts and up to 3 of the user's own phrasings; never a value read from an entity, a value or a
  contact (a playlist, a level, a person), and a phrasing only when it carries none of those. Code checks the answer;
  a failed name leaves the skill nameless with a code label ("CallSite in BrowserX: site tab"), still runnable, never
  offered when its offer class needs a real name. A name the user gave (Rename) is never replaced. Its log and cost
  go to `logs/memory/learn-YYYYMMDD.jsonl`. `backfill.run_once` reads the user's stated usuals once (rule, preference
  and feedback facts in their own words) and proposes them as guesses until the user answers.
- **What else is learned from occasions** (lWire): observed preferences (`prefs.feed_observed`, nightly: one
  `observed` event per occasion whose program is known, at capability level, e.g. `web.open_page:browser@<site>`;
  they decide only below a confirmed usual and never outvote it), a fill policy per learned browser slot (scored on
  history: `front_or_recent`, `band`, `site`, `most`; the winner only when it beats `most` by 5 points), taste kinds
  from the site files (page kinds with a speakable variable, e.g. a YouTube handle), and routine onsets keyed by
  skill id.
- **Offers.** `offers.skill_offerable`: only a live learned skill that never sends or commits, runs on rung A or a
  verified rung B, and whose offer class allows it (a `judged` one also needs a real name and `everyday`); never a
  capability. Its card is the routine card (next section).
- **The Playbook** shows "Skills Yuki learned from you" (`skills/playbook.py`, `MemoryClient.workflows()["skills"]`):
  each learned skill's name, "Seen N times on D days, last ...", how it runs and the step that stays the user's, when
  (its routine), trust ("Worked 3 of 4 times when you asked") and whether it may be offered and why not. Rename /
  Forget this / Not mine (`MemoryClient.skill_edit`): one write on the row, no model; Forget and Not mine tombstone
  it, and Not mine also keeps what it was learned from out of every later pass. Nothing of it is logged. The run
  card's chips read only the practice part (`workflows(skills=False)`).
- **Doctor:** "skills: n capabilities / n learned / n trusted" (read-only).

## Routines from moments and the "Your usual" card (phase R, 10 Oct 2026; migration 42)

Build reports `builds/{rMiner,rLane}.md`; keys from learned skills: `builds/lWire.md`.

- **The miner is code** (`yuki/memory/routines.py`, `RoutineMiner`, run in the portrait's night passes; no model,
  about 0.2 s CPU). It reads the last 28 days of occasions and moments per learned skill (`learn.routine_inputs`, the
  key `skill:<id>`; Yuki's own moments never count, and skills whose run sends or joins are left out) and keeps
  day-onsets. A window passes at 4 or more hits on 4 or more days, a weighted rate of 0.6 or more of the eligible days
  (14-day half-life), lift 3, a spread of 10 minutes or less, a permutation test (p < 0.05) and two stable halves;
  day-sets weekdays / weekends / all; minutes counted from 04:00. A microphone hold counts only inside a window with
  3 or more seen hit days of its own (D7).
- **Tables (migration 42):** `routines` (key, skill id, day-set, usual minute, hits / eligible / rate, state
  `active` / `dormant` / `retired`, `offer_mode` early / late / quiet / off, which the miner keeps when it updates a
  row) and `routine_events` (every state change, and the card lane's `offered`, `held`, `accepted`, `run_ok`,
  `run_failed`, `declined`, `ignored`, `self_served`, `never`, `mode_change`). A routine whose skill was forgotten or
  marked not mine retires (`why = skill_removed`).
- **The portrait's Routines lines come from counts:** one `routine` fact per active routine, origin **`measured`**,
  written by code in a `portrait_runs` row of kind `routines`, model `code`, cost 0 ("On 5 of the last 6 weekdays the
  user joined ... at about 09:00"). The portrait's validator refuses a model's UPDATE or INVALIDATE of a measured
  fact; a model's tidy-up of one is undone the next night, while the user's own "not right" turns it off for good.
- **The card** (`yuki/memory/routine_offers.py`, `RoutineLane`, in the card worker): kind `notice`, trigger
  `routine`, heading "Your usual", a why line, Will / Won't lines (the Won't line from the capability's `wont`, e.g.
  "you click Join"), Open it / Not now / Less of these. Shown in the usual window (mode early: from
  `routine_lead_min` to `routine_lead_last_min` minutes before the usual time; late: 1-3 minutes after), at most
  `routine_per_day` (2) a day, after `routine_settle_s` once the user sat down, never over a game in front, a call,
  full screen, quiet hours, a pause or a snooze, and only when the skill is offerable and has 2 checked runs (D14).
  Right before it shows, the tray asks memory again whether it is still due. Not now, Less of these and ignoring move
  its mode down a ladder (early -> late -> quiet -> off); acceptance under 30% after 10 meeting days quiets it.
  `[nudges]` keys: `routine_offers`, `routine_per_day`, `routine_lead_min`, `routine_lead_last_min`,
  `routine_expire_after_min`, `routine_settle_s`; `offers = "off"` stops it too.
- **CLIs:** `python -m yuki.memory.routines --show` (read-only), `--report [--at ISO]` (on a temporary copy);
  `python -m yuki.memory.routine_offers offer-now <id> [--in N] [--wait S]` (a test card through the real gates; it
  counts against the day's 2 and is never learned from). Routines are not in the MCP.

## "You right now" and the one card judge (10 Oct 2026; shadow; no migration)

Design `docs/research/audit-2026-10-09/next/state-design.md` rev 2 (sections 3, 4); build reports
`builds/{yState,jCore}.md`.

- **YouNow** (`yuki/memory/younow.py`, `YouBuilder`): one typed, capped "you right now" record in the memory
  service's RAM only (never on disk, never in the MCP, at most 12 KB): `presence` (present / away / locked / off,
  settled, at the PC since, today's time, the last break, the session), `focus` (hold facts only: the game in front by
  the one rule `younow.focus_game`, the return hold, full screen, the meeting and its audio, the call, Yuki acting),
  `changed` (the last 30 minutes of moments plus `sit_down`, `switch`, `call_end`, `game_end`), `work` (the thread
  now, active threads, goals, items due in 24 h, plans), `usual` and `prefs` (empty until yUsual fills them), `about`
  (the brief by reference) and `cards` (the card on screen, today's cards and reactions, the judge's own facts). It is
  checked on the service's existing 1 s beat and a section is rebuilt only when its inputs moved (the gate's and the
  call worker's change hooks, the acting follower, the timeline's flags, the hub's front and media events, one cheap
  database-change probe at most every 2 s); holds that end with time are timestamps compared when read
  (`holds_now`). An idle beat costs about 0.02 ms. Stats line `you=`. `[you] enabled = false` leaves it unbuilt (the
  judge then holds everything). `python -m yuki.memory.younow --show` prints it content-free.
- **The card judge** (`yuki/memory/cardjudge.py`, `candidates.py`, `attend.py`), called once per card-worker tick
  after the old makers. Every card idea becomes a candidate in one pile (reminders, confirms, standing requests, the
  five check-ins, break nudges and call-backs, stuck, recaps, follow-ups, plans, threads, questions, work, the
  review). One attention policy (`attend.py`) holds during a game in front, a call (an unreadable call status holds,
  then lets reminders and standing requests through after 10 minutes), a meeting, away, settling, locked, quiet
  hours, pause and snooze, and checks again right before a card would show. It shows at most one card, only at a
  natural pause (`confidence x timing >= 0.30`; never while the user types), at most about 4 a day besides reminders,
  standing requests, the routine card and the Today card; things that can wait go into **one Today card** at the
  first settled sit-down after the night (a check-in head and a few lines), or a "while you were away" list. A
  per-(kind, trigger) ledger moves an ignored kind down (to the Today card, then off); "Less of these" acts at once
  and the ledger never raises a kind the user lowered; reminders, standing requests and the routine card are never
  lowered by it. It never offers the user's work.
- **Shadow (this release):** `[judge] mode = "shadow"`: the old makers still show every card; the judge only writes
  what it would have shown, content-free (`nudge_state` keys `judge_seen`, `judge_types`, `judge_shadow`, and
  `judge_decision` lines in the card worker's log), makes no model call and writes no card. `"off"` does not run it;
  `"on"` (jSwitch, a later release) makes it the one card maker. `[judge] digest = false` turns the Today card off.
  `python -m yuki.memory.cardjudge --report` reads the ledger and the shadow days (the go-live gate after 7 shadow
  days); `--dry-run --at ISO` replays moments on a copy.

## The new brain and what memory sees of it (10 Oct 2026; no migration)

Design `docs/research/audit-2026-10-09/next/brain-design.md` rev 2; build reports
`builds/{bCore,bExec,bWire,bTalk}.md`.

- **The switch:** `brain` = `loop` (default: today's agent loop for everything), `typed` (typed requests on the new
  brain, live check 17a) or `all` (spoken requests too, with the lean talker, live check 17b). Set by Claude in
  `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Yuki\settings.json` (`"brain": "typed"`) or `YUKI_BRAIN`, read when Yuki starts, so a change needs
  a restart; going back to `loop` is the whole rollback (no database change). The UI log says `brain state=ready`
  (or `unavailable`: the old loop goes on answering) on a lane's first request.
- **One path** (`yuki/brain/`): the user's exact words -> a learned skill or capability through the engine, else one
  streamed plan call (goal, target, route, consent, done-check, steps) -> the executor acts step by step under one
  consent rule in code, with one small yes/no check per plan only on steps that could send, delete, buy, close or
  join -> one done-check; the "done" line is written by code from what the check saw, else "I couldn't confirm it".
  A second look a few seconds later that disagrees is told once on a card ("Looking again, I can't see that any
  more.").
- **The user's words only.** Only what the user typed or said (heard turns, each exactly as heard) counts as their
  words; card text, coach notes, where it was typed and the voice's own context go in as labelled context that is
  never quotable. A request with none of their words (a card suggestion sent unchanged, a request the window built
  from a click) can only leave drafts. Only the user's own words are ever sent; a message Yuki words itself stays a
  draft.
- **What memory gets is unchanged in shape:** the same `log_turn` exchanges, the acting marker with the request's id
  (moments inside it are `by='yuki'`), `skill_runs` rows for skills, run traces, and the `requests.csv` row with new
  code columns (`plan_kind`, `plan_steps`, `replans`, `probe`, `verdict`, `plan_ms`, `plan_calls`, `check_calls`).
  Ids: a typed request keeps its session-log id across the front desk's hand-off; a spoken one is
  `voice-<turn>-t<n>`.
- **Voice under `all`** (bTalk): the lean talker (core prompt under 8,000 characters, no app names), its hand-off
  tool `hand_over` (`say_first`, `intent`, `replaces`; no paraphrase field) and `cancel_hand_over` (both in Yuki's own
  words list, `core/ownwords.py`, so they are never spoken), no router, the speech guard's two code rules only; "how's
  it going?" is answered from the plan by code. The runtime gives a spoken request to the lane's brain only under
  `all` (`Lane.brain()`, made once under a lock; the voice reads ahead on it with `Brain.begin_early` when the user's
  turn ends), with the heard turns as the user's words, and a spoken request set aside on a question goes on with the
  spoken answer added to them.

## Footprint

The owner's rule (2026-09-24): Yuki uses as little CPU, GPU and RAM as possible (RAM is the more acceptable of the three, but stays low). Private bytes are what Windows commits for the process (what counts against RAM); working set is what is resident now.

**Before, live** (read-only, 20:10-20:20, Discord call recording with large-v3 loaded on CUDA): memory service 431-826 MB working set, **3.1-3.7 GB private**, 57 threads, 3.4% of one core, listed by `nvidia-smi` as a compute (CUDA) process; tray 104-171 MB working set, **587 MB private**, 33 threads, 0.1% of one core. The tray never used the GPU.

**What dominated** (each measured alone in a fresh process, 2026-09-24):

| Component | Private | Working set | Threads |
|---|---|---|---|
| numpy (OpenBLAS: one thread and a ~32 MB buffer per core, 16 on this CPU) | +490 MB | +20 MB | +15 |
| the embedding model loaded (fastembed: tokenizer ~260 MB, ONNX session the rest; see "Embedder, 2026-09-27" below: ~175 MB now) | +520-570 MB | +520-580 MB | +7-26 |
| large-v3 on CUDA (CUDA context, cuBLAS/cuDNN, weights) | ~2.6 GB | ~0.4-3 GB | +20 |
| service imports (UIA, comtypes, watcher, timeline) | +20 MB | +25 MB | +1 |
| WinRT media sessions | +20 MB | +40 MB | +6 |
| Silero VAD session (while recording only) | +10 MB | +20 MB | +1 |
| Qt + the tray's imports / anthropic + boto3 client | +20 MB / +50 MB | +35 MB / +55 MB | 0 / +3 |

**Cuts** (none loses a capture, a fact or a transcript):
- **No speech model in the service, ever**: calls are spooled and transcribed later in a separate below-normal process that exits after its batch (see "Calls: both sides"). The ~2.6-3 GB and the GPU context are gone from the service; the GPU is used only while that worker runs.
- **`OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS=1`** set in `yuki/__init__.py` before numpy loads (a user-set value wins): -490 MB private in every Yuki process (service, tray, CLI, MCP). numpy's work here is matrix-vector products of a few thousand rows (2000x384 x100 in 9 ms single-threaded).
- **Embedder**: loaded on first use (as before), now **unloaded after 5 minutes without a call** (`yuki.memory.embed.IDLE_UNLOAD_S`; the next call reloads it in ~1-2 s), ONNX Runtime at 2 threads without the CPU memory arena, the tokenizer's thread pool at 2 (`RAYON_NUM_THREADS`). Vectors are unchanged (cosine 0.9999999 against the old settings, so stored vectors stay valid). Unloaded, the process falls back from ~600 to ~75 MB private.
- **Call detection** reads the microphone's users only when Windows changes the ConsentStore key (registry change notification), with a 60 s backstop, instead of a ~30 ms read every 5 s.
- The service already runs at below-normal priority (checked live: `BELOW_NORMAL_PRIORITY_CLASS`); the transcription worker too.

**After, same 10 minutes side by side** (two temp-database instances of the service, before = HEAD 7c3d928, after = this change; journal, conversations, episodes, timeline, watcher and Warp on; coach, review, portrait and calls off; the owner was using the PC - both made 76-78 captures and 11 journal calls):

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Private, before the embedder first loads | 531-536 MB | **51-54 MB** |
| Private, embedder loaded (journal active every few minutes) | 1,131-1,139 MB | 643-648 MB |
| Working set, peak | 707 MB | 698 MB |
| Threads | 32 / 56 | 17 / 22 |
| CPU, one core, average over 9 min | 3.8% | 3.0% |
| GPU | none (calls off here; live: CUDA context held all call) | none outside the deferred batch |

Tray, measured by its imports offscreen (the running tray was not touched): Qt + `yuki.ui.app` + a memory client + the Bedrock client + agent and perception imports = **112 MB private**, 151 MB working set (live before: 587 MB private). It grows by the embedder's ~520 MB only while `recall` or a to-do matched by text was used in the last 5 minutes.

**Targets**:
- Service idle under 250 MB private: **met when idle** (~51-100 MB with the embedder unloaded, i.e. 5 minutes after the last journal batch). **Missed while the user is active**: the journal embeds its facts every few minutes, so the model stays loaded (~645 MB). Unloading sooner would reload it every batch (~1.3 s of CPU each, ~0.7% of one core) - a CPU-for-RAM trade the owner can choose (`IDLE_UNLOAD_S`).
- Service under 1% of one core on average: **missed while the user is active** (3.0%: the captures themselves - UIA reads of the window in front at 8 per minute - and the embedder's load and embeds), not measured with the user away (the owner was at the PC; the day's live logs show 1.4-2.5% in 10-minute windows of use). Cutting the capture work would lose captured memory, so it was not cut.
- Tray under 200 MB: **met** by measurement of its parts (112 MB private), to be confirmed on the next restart.
- Zero GPU use outside the deferred batch: **met** (the service never loads ctranslate2/faster-whisper; NVML queries, Silero and Opus create no GPU context - checked with `nvidia-smi --query-compute-apps`).

**Embedder, 2026-09-27** (`scripts/embed_bench.py`, each model in a fresh process; quality = 45 hand-written English/Hinglish/Japanese queries over a copy of the real memory, target ranked among all current rows of its table; table in `yuki/memory/embed.py`):
- The cost was the **tokenizer**: the `tokenizers` library holds XLM-R's 250k-piece vocabulary in ~268 MB; `sentencepiece` runs the same vocabulary with identical ids (1,556 texts checked) in ~45 MB. fastembed's import alone was another ~33 MB. The graph is now run with onnxruntime directly (identical vectors to fastembed: cos 0.9999998).
- **New default: the same MiniLM with int8 weights** (sentence-transformers' `model_quint8_avx2.onnx`, 118 MB) + sentencepiece: loaded 176 MB private instead of ~555 MB, load 1.3 s, 9-12 ms a query, same quality (R@1/R@5/MRR .62/.89/.74 vs .60/.89/.73; 5x5 5/5; fast-memory cosines unchanged, so `MIN_COSINE` stays). Its vectors differ (cos .985 to the old ones), so older databases move by the background re-embed (`yuki.memory.reembed`, migration 20); until the flip they keep the old model (with sentencepiece installed, the old model already drops to 336 MB, identical vectors). Measured move of a copy of the real memory: 2,041 vectors, ~4 min wall at one ONNX thread, killed and resumed mid-way.
- Rejected: multilingual-e5-small int8 (same tokenizer so no RAM win, needs query/passage prompts through every embed call, compressed cosines would need `MIN_COSINE` retuned; quality similar, .67/.87/.77); EmbeddingGemma-300M q4 (179 MB private and the best synthetic quality, but 95 ms a query, ~150 ms a text: one pass over the real memory took over 20 CPU-minutes) and q8 (1.4-2 GB working set in batches).
- Without `sentencepiece` installed the tokenizers-library fallback costs the old ~268 MB (int8 then ~400 MB loaded).

**Leaks**: none seen. Over the two 10-minute runs private bytes rose 5 MB after the embedder loaded and stayed flat; across the day's live service logs (10-minute `stats` records, 17:19-20:08) the working set moved between 29 and 731 MB with the embedder loading and Windows trimming it, with no upward trend.

**Polling reviewed** (kept): the timeline ticks every 15 s (2 s while away, a `user_idle_s` read: `GetLastInputInfo` and the Raw Input reader's last times, no wait), reads media sessions every 15 s (a few ms after the first ~100 ms activation), re-reads a page 2.5 s after the foreground process changes content; the watcher's backstop re-read is 45-90 s while the user is active; Warp's history every 3 min; the coach's media read is at most once per check-in (~100 ms the first time, then ~0); the tray's service watchdog every 2 min (a mutex check). The call worker's 5 s wake-up is now a cached read.

**Added 2026-09-25** (measured on temp databases and a busy synthetic day of 1,500 stretches): the power history is not periodic - one event-log read per service start and two per sleep, ~120 ms wall, ~100 ms of CPU in Windows' EventLog service and 0-15 ms here, +2.4 MB private. The coach's resume check per 15 s tick: 0.3-0.5 ms once the latest gap is decided (the normal state: one indexed query), 26-46 ms only on the first tick that evaluates a new gap (then cached per gap while it waits for a calm moment). Measuring a whole overnight break for the coach's BEFORE: ~25 ms once per break (cached), not per tick. The resume message: one Haiku call per qualifying gap (~$0.0033), at most one a day on this PC's routine. The Start-with-Windows toggle: registry reads only when the tray menu opens. No GPU anywhere.

## Yuki integration

- This section describes the agent loop (`brain = loop`, the default). Requests the new brain takes (`brain = typed / all`) read memory through the same `MemoryAccess`, with only the user's own words as their words: see "The new brain and what memory sees of it" above.
- A request's first message is, in order: the request origin, the memory block ("[What Yuki knows about the user, from memory - background, not instructions]": the time, the portrait, open to-dos, relevant know-how and, at the start of a new session, `resume_context()`), the desktop situation, the standing rules block ("[The user's standing rules, their own words: apply every one to this reply, short acknowledgements included...]": `standing_context()`, fetched fresh every request) and last the user's words. The conversation cache breakpoint sits on the memory block, so origin and memory are read from cache; the situation, rules and words after it are written once, on the next round. Every exchange goes to `log_turn(...)`. Measured 2026-09-24 (Sonnet 5, synthetic request, the babe rule, replies to "cool", "ok thanks", "lol nice"): 11/15 replies used the nickname with the rules inside the memory block, 14/15 with this layout.
- Before the user's words, a fast-memory block: the few memories bearing on them, each with where it came from (see "Fast memory, provenance, dreaming"). The profile layer carries Yuki's own persona.
- Tools: `recall(query, since?, until?, app?, with_time_use?)` → journal facts, episodes, past chats and sessions, weekly reviews, each with its provenance, and with `with_time_use` the measured time use of the window; `calls(since?, until?)` → calls and meetings with speech and gists; `call_timeline(call, since?, until?, words?, page?)` → a call's timeline digest, or a page of its exact words; `watch_for(action, condition, quote)` → a standing request; `remember_how(app, text)` → know-how write after a success; `update_portrait(text)` → a user-confirmed correction; `remember_rule(text)` / `revoke_rule(ref)` → a standing rule the user just gave, or the id of one they withdrew, applied at once.
- When a choice is driven by the portrait, Yuki says why in its reply.

## Build phases

1. **A**: watcher + store + journal (this contract's first half). Deliverable: `uv run yuki-memory` runs quietly; a `scripts/memory_report.py` prints today's journal and capture health.
2. **B**: portrait + know-how + Yuki integration.
3. **C**: open loops / to-dos (the merged list and the coach: see "Nudges and to-dos"), weekly review, MCP server for other agents.
